## Where we stopped (end of session 36 — the session 35 residual-gaps list closed out, 2026-09-26) — start here next session

Picked up session 35's own explicit next-step list (below) in the order it recommended, one PR per item, same discipline as every prior E2EE step:

1. **PR #195** — `fn_delete_message_for_everyone` now also deletes every `e2ee_message_envelopes` row for the message, not just blanking `messages.body`/media fields. Closed the real privacy gap: a party who hadn't yet fetched a specific envelope before "delete for everyone" could previously still decrypt it afterward.
2. **PR #196** — safety-number change-detection alert. `apps/mobile/lib/e2ee/safetyNumberStore.ts` (new) keeps a trust-on-first-use baseline of each partner's identity key; `useSafetyNumberChangeAlert` (`thread/[id].tsx`) compares against it on an e2ee-active thread and surfaces a dismissible banner on a mismatch, reusing the "🔒 protected" banner's surface. Informational only, matching Signal/WhatsApp's own notice — doesn't block sending.
3. **PR #197** — message editing wired into the e2ee client. `useEditMessage` now encrypts via the same `encryptForThread` path a send already uses when `e2eeStatus === 'active'`, sending `envelopes` instead of `body`. Server-side envelope-replacement path (step 4, session 35) was already correct; the client had just never called it. Added HTTP-layer test coverage for `edit-message`'s e2ee path (previously only ever exercised via a direct RPC call).
4. **PR #198** — forwarding wired into e2ee-active threads. `useThreads` now selects `e2ee_status` so `ForwardMessageModal` can tag which 1:1 targets are encrypted; `handleConfirmForward` encrypts the same way. Offline forwarding into an e2ee-active thread is explicitly rejected (the outbox has no concept of encrypting at drain time) rather than silently queued and failing later — flagged as a pre-existing gap shared with a plain offline send into an e2ee thread, not fixed here (out of scope for a forwarding-specific pass). Added HTTP-layer coverage proving `envelopes` + `is_forwarded` actually compose through the real Edge Function request.
5. **Still blocked, not fixed**: on-device verification of `react-native-libsodium`'s native HKDF path against the RFC 5869 vectors — needs real iOS/Android hardware, which this Node-only dev environment still can't reach. Unchanged from session 35's own note.
6. **Decided**: the independent (non-AI) review question (`docs/21` §7's own standing recommendation) — user's call was to ship on the adversarial AI pass alone for now, given resource constraints, and treat an independent review as a backlog item for before this handles a much larger user base or higher-stakes usage, not before this initial rollout. Recorded in `docs/21` §7 directly.

**What's still genuinely open, carried forward from session 35, untouched by this session:** multi-device isn't real yet (`docs/12`); media isn't supported on e2ee-active threads (by design); group chat is out of scope entirely; the offline+e2ee outbox gap noted in item 4 above (affects a plain send too, not just forwarding); the two pre-existing unrelated bugs already on record (`fn_start_thread`'s asymmetric `threads_participants_unique` check, and two unexplained `fraud-functions.test.js` failures — memory `fraud-functions-test-pricing-config-leak`).

No `eas update`/OTA publish was pushed this session for the four client-side (JS-only) PRs above — commit/PR/merge only, per this session's instruction. Worth surfacing next time a build/update comes up, alongside the already-queued voice-notes and cert-pinning items.

## Where we stopped (end of session 35 — real end-to-end encryption, all 7 build-order steps shipped, 2026-09-26) — start here next session

**Session 34 left the E2EE decision (`docs/19` §0 / `docs/18` Tier D) explicitly held for later.** This session the user asked for it directly: "I would really want to use the E2EE" — scoped in full first (`docs/20-E2EE-SCOPING.md`), then a complete technical design (`docs/21-E2EE-TECHNICAL-DESIGN.md`), then built step by step on explicit go-ahead, one PR per step, same discipline every prior phase in this project has used. All 7 of docs/21 §8's build-order steps are now shipped and merged (PRs #185–#192, plus one unplanned CI-fix PR #189 and one unplanned bugfix PR #191 — see below). Real Signal-style Double Ratchet/X3DH, implemented from the public specs (not a native `libsignal` bridge — this Windows dev environment has no local iOS compile/verify path), opt-in per one-on-one conversation.

**Key design decisions made this session, all user-confirmed against explicit recommendations:** no pre-send moderation on E2EE threads (can't moderate content you can't read); byte-length billing replaces word-count billing for E2EE threads only (byte length is measurable on ciphertext without decrypting it, word count isn't); multi-device-aware schema from day one even though linked devices (`docs/12`) isn't built yet; a hand-rolled Double Ratchet/X3DH on `react-native-libsodium` primitives chosen over a native `libsignal-client` bridge specifically because this environment can't compile/debug native iOS linking, and because the design keeps the ratchet swappable behind one clean interface (`encryptForThread`/`decryptEnvelope`) if that ever changes.

- **Step 1 (PR #185) — schema + handshake functions.** `e2ee_devices`/`e2ee_signed_prekeys`/`e2ee_one_time_prekeys`/`e2ee_message_envelopes`, `threads.e2ee_status` (`'off'`/`'active'`, defaults `'off'`, never flickers back), `fn_register_e2ee_device`/`fn_fetch_prekey_bundles`/`fn_replenish_one_time_prekeys`/`fn_enable_e2ee`. Two real bugs caught by the test suite itself before merge: missing `ON DELETE CASCADE` on three FKs, and Postgres's `encode(bytea,'base64')` silently line-wrapping at 76 characters for the 64-byte signature field (not the shorter 32-byte fields, which is exactly why it needed checking explicitly rather than assumed to generalize) — both fixed same session, `fn_base64_encode_nowrap` added.
- **Step 2 (PR #186) — client-facing Edge Functions** wrapping step 1's functions (`register-e2ee-device`, `fetch-prekey-bundles` rate-limited, `replenish-one-time-prekeys`, `enable-e2ee`), Zod-validated per `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rule.
- **Step 3 (PR #187) — the crypto core, built and tested in complete isolation** (`apps/mobile/lib/e2ee/x3dh.ts`/`doubleRatchet.ts`, pure functions against a `SodiumProvider` interface, zero platform dependency). HKDF-SHA256 and its underlying HMAC are hand-rolled and verified against RFC 5869/4231's own published test vectors, not trusted by construction — needed because neither available Node-testable libsodium binding exposed what was needed directly (a real, live-verified finding: the non-sumo `libsodium-wrappers` package's actual runtime is missing most of what its own `.d.ts` claims). Production uses `react-native-libsodium`'s real native HKDF binding directly instead. 30 assertions.
- **Step 4 (PR #188) — server-side billing wiring.** `fn_send_message`/`fn_edit_message` gain an `e2ee_status`-aware branch — the `'off'` path is byte-for-byte unchanged, confirmed via `pg_get_functiondef` before writing the migration. `fn_release_escrow` needed **zero code changes** — its duplicate-content check already no-ops for a null (e2ee) message body via Postgres's own NULL-propagation semantics, verified live rather than assumed. Two real bugs caught by this step's own test suite before merge (a `jsonb_array_length` crash on JSON-null vs SQL-NULL, and an ambiguous column reference from `RETURNS TABLE`'s column names shadowing PL/pgSQL variables). 33 assertions.
- **Step 5 (PR #190) — client integration.** `identity.ts`/`prekeys.ts`/`session.ts`/`safetyNumber.ts`; `useSendMessage`/`useThreadMessages` now branch on `e2ee_status`; thread screen gets an enable-encryption action, a "🔒 protected" banner reusing the payer banner's surface, a safety-number view. **A real architectural gap found while building this, not in the original scope**: Double Ratchet forward secrecy means a message's key is used once and discarded — this app had no local plaintext message store, so without one, every already-decrypted E2EE message would become permanently unreadable on app restart. Added `plaintextCache.ts` (AsyncStorage, no expiry, its own namespace — not `_layout.tsx`'s general 24h TanStack persister — no new dependency). Also found live: PostgREST returns `bytea` as `"\x"+hex` over JSON, not base64 — `hexToBytes` added, the one place a mobile client reads `e2ee_message_envelopes` directly instead of through an Edge Function's own base64 re-encoding.
- **Unplanned, between steps 5 and 6 (PR #189): CI had been red since step 1, not caught until now.** `apps/mobile`'s `tsc --noEmit` was rejecting the `.ts` import extensions Node's ESM loader requires for the crypto-core test suites — `gh run list` showed 4 straight failing merges before this was checked. Fixed (`allowImportingTsExtensions`), plus a real latent type error surfaced only by this project's own `tsc` run. **Worth internalizing for next time: check CI status after merging, don't just trust local `deno check`/Node test runs.**
- **Step 6 (PR #191) — the adversarial self-review `docs/21` §7 calls for**, run as a fork explicitly told to distrust its own prior design reasoning and try to break `x3dh.ts`/`doubleRatchet.ts`. Found 3 real, reproduced-live bugs, all one root cause (`session.ts`'s load-ratchet-save sequence had nothing serializing overlapping calls for the same device): a **critical** AEAD key+nonce reuse across two different real plaintexts on concurrent encrypts (XOR-recoverable by anyone holding both ciphertexts — the server always does), a forward-secrecy violation on concurrent decrypts, and a session-reset via redelivery of the original bootstrap envelope (realistic under normal at-least-once delivery, not contrived). `doubleRatchet.ts`/`x3dh.ts` themselves were clean. Fixed with `deviceLock.ts` (a real per-`(thread, device)` async mutex) plus a one-line guard; 15 new assertions including negative-control runs proving the tests actually fail against the old logic. Also found while investigating a more speculative note: `threads_participants_unique` only constrains one ordering of `(participant_a, participant_b)`, not its reverse — two threads between the same two people are reachable, not just theoretical (not fixed, pre-existing, unrelated to E2EE — flagged for whoever next touches `fn_start_thread`); session storage is now keyed by `(threadId, peerDeviceId)` as defense-in-depth regardless. **A second, completely unrelated pre-existing bug was found and fixed along the way**: `fraud-functions.test.js`'s per-hour-cap test had left `pricing_config.escrow_release_earnings_per_hour_cap` stuck at `3` (its own test value) in the shared dev DB instead of restoring it to the seeded `100`, breaking an unrelated test in the same file — restored via direct SQL. Two more failures in that same file remain open and unexplained (not config corruption, not caused by this session's work) — see memory `fraud-functions-test-pricing-config-leak`.
- **Step 7 (PR #192) — ToS/Privacy Policy rewrite.** `packages/legal-content/privacy.ts` §9 and `terms.ts` §11 rewritten in the same plain-disclosure voice the rest of these documents already use — what E2EE actually protects (Sun Media genuinely cannot read an encrypted conversation's content, for any reason) and, just as plainly, what it doesn't (moderation stops working on it, metadata is still visible and fraud detection still runs on it, it's opt-in and one-way, not available for group chats, not yet independently audited). `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md`'s own stale "E2EE is a larger lift explicitly deferred" line corrected to match.

**What's still genuinely open, not silently dropped — `docs/21` §5/§7 record all of these:**

- Editing an E2EE-active message isn't wired client-side (`fn_edit_message`'s server-side envelope-replacement path shipped in step 4; `useEditMessage`/the thread screen's edit flow still only builds a plaintext-body request).
- Forwarding a message _into_ an E2EE-active thread isn't wired either — the server correctly rejects it (`e2ee_envelopes_required`, a visible failure, not a silent leak), just not a working feature yet.
- `delete-message-for-everyone` scrubs `messages.body` and the local plaintext cache but not the corresponding `e2ee_message_envelopes` ciphertext row server-side — a party who hadn't yet opened a specific message before it was "deleted" could still decrypt it afterward. Narrow, but real.
- The safety-number screen has no change-detection alert if a contact's key ever rotates (arguably the higher-value half of a real safety-number UX).
- Multi-device isn't real yet (`docs/12`, three times deferred before this session, still not built) — `session.ts` resolves "the sender's device" as the first active one found, correct only because nobody has a second device today.
- The native adapter's HKDF path (`react-native-libsodium`, production) still isn't vector-verified on-device — needs the actual RN runtime, which this Node-only environment can't reach.
- Media is not supported on E2EE-active threads at all (by design, this pass) — rejected outright server-side, not silently degraded.
- Group chat is out of scope entirely, same boundary every other structural change this project has drawn.
- **The one call that's explicitly yours to make, not a build step**: `docs/21` §7's own standing recommendation is that this adversarial self-review pass is necessary but not sufficient — a genuinely independent (non-AI, or at least fresh-eyes) review before this touches real user data is still the right bar for something this consequential.

**Next session: explicit instruction to start here, closing this session out.** Work through the residual gaps above, in this order (roughly risk/value order, not a hard requirement):

1. `delete-message-for-everyone` doesn't scrub the `e2ee_message_envelopes` ciphertext row — the real privacy gap of the list, fix first.
2. Safety-number change-detection alert — the higher-value half of that feature is currently missing.
3. Wire editing into the client for E2EE-active messages (server-side path already shipped, step 4).
4. Wire forwarding into an E2EE-active thread (currently a clean server-side rejection, not a working feature).
5. On-device verification of the native HKDF path (react-native-libsodium) against the RFC 5869 vectors — needs real hardware, can't be done from this Node-only environment; note this explicitly if it's still unavailable when picked up.
6. The independent (non-AI) review question — still the user's call, not a build step; ask again if it hasn't been decided by the time this list is picked up.

If picking up the E2EE work cold, start by re-reading `docs/21` §5/§7 in full rather than trusting this summary's compression of it. Two pre-existing, unrelated bugs are also on record and not fixed: `fn_start_thread`'s asymmetric `threads_participants_unique` check (two threads can exist between the same two people), and two unexplained `fraud-functions.test.js` failures (memory `fraud-functions-test-pricing-config-leak`) — pick either up only if directly asked to, not as part of the E2EE list above.

## Where we stopped (end of session 34 — voice-notes backend, a real safe-area bug fix, chat/status refinement batch Tier A+B+C1 shipped, a full security-hardening pass shipped, 2026-09-26) — start here next session

**Six pieces of real work this session, each scoped before being built, same discipline session 33 established.**

**Piece 1 — a real, reported UI bug, fixed and shipped (PR #177).** User reported a blank strip below the header on the 1:1 chat screen. Root cause: `Screen`'s `SafeAreaView` reserved the top inset unconditionally, correct for tab screens using the in-content `AppHeader` but redundant — and visibly double-padding — on any screen with a native `Stack.Screen` header, since the native header already renders within the top safe area. Same bug existed on 9 other native-header screens (group thread, profile, six settings pages), not just the one reported — all fixed together via a new `edges` prop on `Screen`. Merged and shipped via OTA.

**Piece 2 — voice notes (docs/17-VOICE-NOTES-SCOPING.md), backend only, client deliberately held (PR #178, merged, deployed, live).** Scoped in depth first (full WhatsApp interaction parity — hold/slide/lock recording, real waveform, scrub/speed/auto-advance playback), on explicit go-ahead. Backend built and shipped: `media_type` allow-list widened to include `'audio'`, `duration_seconds`/`waveform_samples`/`audio_played_at` columns, a server-enforced `message_audio_max_seconds` cap, `message_audio_credits` as its own `pricing_config` key, `fn_mark_audio_played`, and `create-chat-media-upload-url`/`send-message` both extended accordingly. Audio moderation added (transcribe via OpenAI, then the existing `moderateText` mapping) — confirmed reaching OpenAI live, but **this account currently has no billing/credits for the paid transcription endpoint** (a real, confirmed `insufficient_quota` 429), so it always fails open (allowed, unmoderated) until that's resolved — a real open item, not silently accepted. **Client work (recorder gesture, playback, bubble UI) is explicitly NOT started** — `expo-audio` isn't installed, so it needs a real `eas build`, and the user said to hold it and bundle it into whatever `eas build` happens next for any reason, rather than triggering one just for this (saved to memory: `voice-notes-client-queued-for-next-eas-build`). 35 new test assertions across both new suites, all passing; existing photo suite re-run clean each time.

**Piece 3 — a ten-item batch UX/economy request, scoped into four risk tiers before any of it was touched (docs/18-CHAT-STATUS-REFINEMENT-BATCH-SCOPING.md).** Tier A (six independent, low-risk pieces) and Tier B (one bounded billing change) both built and shipped this session; Tier C (two structural/economic proposals) was scoped but explicitly held for the user's own review before any code, per their own instruction.

- **Tier A (PR #179, merged, OTA'd):** read-tick size/color (new `tickRead` token, sized up, turns yellow on read — a deliberate deviation from WhatsApp's blue, flagged as such), status-viewers list converted to a half-screen bottom sheet, Buy Credit surfaced in the chat header + 3-dot menu (reusing the existing modal), the "+" new-chat flow rewired onto the same `MemberSearchList` widget the group member picker already uses (name search + exact-match-only phone lookup, deliberately not a new partial/prefix phone-search surface), Calls removed from the tab bar (kept on disk via `href: null`) with Groups promoted into that slot as its own top-level tab (lifted wholesale out of the Chats screen's segmented row), and the standalone emoji picker removed entirely from both 1:1 and group composers (it existed in both files, removed from both).
- **Tier B (PR #180, merged, deployed, live, OTA'd):** status replies are free, but scoped exactly as agreed — only the sender's genuinely first-ever message in a thread, replying to a real/unexpired/visible status, with no media attached; everything after that is billed normally. A free message never creates an escrow (`messages.status` gained a new `'sent'` value for this). **A real gap caught against the live schema, not assumed**: `messages_credits_charged_check` was `> 0` and would have rejected every free insert outright — relaxed to `>= 0` in the same migration. 19 new assertions (ledger-conservation, the exploit-closing "second message isn't free" case, media forfeiting the exemption, fake/wrong-poster/expired-status rejection). The OTA publish for this piece needed four attempts — three consecutive transient network failures in this environment (socket hang-up, ECONNRESET, then a DNS resolution failure) before one succeeded; worth knowing if an OTA publish seems to hang or fail without an obvious cause again, it may just need a retry, not a real fix.
- **Tier C1 (payer/earner role) — fully redesigned this session, not built.** The original scoping pass (kept in `docs/18` as a collapsed "superseded" note) correctly flagged the brief's literal "pending consent, decide on reply" mechanic as genuinely risky — it breaks this app's atomic-debit invariant and needs a new message lifecycle state. Asked to go deeper and give a best recommendation, the actual redesign models "who pays" as a **role** (`threads.payer_id`, nullable, defaults to `participant_a` so every existing thread is unaffected) resolved _before_ a message is ever created, rather than a per-message decision — so `fn_send_message` keeps its exact current atomic shape, just resolving the payer dynamically. "Charge from me" sets `payer_id` to the caller's own id (self only, enforced server-side); stepping down sets it to `null`, which rejects sends from either side until someone claims the role again — no new message state, nothing retroactive. Reuses the thread screen's existing static "they pay for this conversation" banner, made live and tappable, instead of any new UI surface. Fraud mitigation addressed directly: a reassignment cooldown (new `pricing_config` key) plus a new collusion-detection signal for rapid payer-flip-then-reply pairs, both feeding existing infrastructure rather than inventing new. **This is now genuinely ready to build on go-ahead — no further design work needed first**, unlike Tier C2.
- **Tier C2 (boosted/"Suggested" status) and Tier D (the "end-to-end encryption" ask) are untouched, exactly as the user asked** — C2 still needs its own dedicated design pass (audience-selection algorithm, pre-publish moderation, new revenue-table entries, a real compliance look); D was flagged as a factual-accuracy problem, not a complexity one — real E2EE is directly incompatible with the content moderation this app already ships, and the recommendation (accurate "secured in transit/at rest" copy instead of a false E2EE claim) is written up but not applied to any UI yet.

**Piece 5 — Tier C1 (payer/earner role), designed, reviewed a second time, corrected, and built — same session, on explicit go-ahead (docs/18 §C1, migration `20260926140000_thread_payer_role.sql`).** Before build, a second review pass (requested explicitly, not offered) checked the design's own claims against the live migrations rather than trusting its summary, and found one real bug in the plan: `fn_release_escrow` hardcoded `threads.participant_b` as "the payee" in three places (wallet resolution, the hourly release-rate cap, the release-time duplicate-content check) — safe only because participant_b genuinely was the payee of every escrow in every thread until this feature made that no longer true. Fixed by giving `fn_release_escrow` an explicit `p_sender_id` parameter and filtering its release loop to `payee_id = p_sender_id`, which let `fn_send_message`'s old hardcoded-to-participant_b `v_is_reply` pre-check be deleted outright rather than repaired. A second refinement (also requested during review, not offered) replaced the originally-scoped flat "cooldown since last reassignment" with a 24-hour conversation-idle gate on _taking over_ the payer role — closed against the "step down then immediately re-claim" bypass by applying to claims out of `null` too, not just takeovers from an active payer; stepping down itself stays instant and ungated. Shipped: `threads.payer_id` (nullable, backfilled to `participant_a`, `fn_start_thread` updated to set it on new threads), `thread_payer_history` (append-only audit table), `fn_set_thread_payer` (self-only, current-payer-only stepdown, idle-gated claim, row-locked same as `fn_send_message`), the `fn_release_escrow`/`fn_send_message` correction above, a new `set-thread-payer` Edge Function, and the mobile banner (`thread/[id].tsx`) made live off `payer_id` instead of the old static `participant_a`-based read. 45 new test assertions across two new suites (`thread-payer-functions.test.js` — DB-level, includes the specific mixed-payer-escrow scenario that would fail against the pre-fix `fn_release_escrow`; `set-thread-payer-function.test.js` — HTTP-level request/error-mapping); 156 existing assertions across every suite touching `fn_send_message`/`fn_release_escrow` (chargeback, base send-message, chat-media, chat-audio, free-status-reply) re-run clean, confirming no regression. Both Edge Functions deployed and confirmed reachable in production (not just locally tested — see the deployed-function-testing-gap lesson from session 14).

**Piece 6 — a full security-engineering review requested end-to-end (encryption, network/transport, DoS, input validation), scoped first into `docs/19-SECURITY-HARDENING-SCOPING.md`, Tier A built and shipped same session, Tier B item 1 built and held.** The review corrected a real architecture mismatch in the request itself: several of the originally-suggested pieces (Nginx/Caddy config, Redis-based rate limiting, a custom WebSocket gateway, an ORM) assumed a self-hosted server this app doesn't have — it's Supabase-managed end to end (`docs/01`). The actual highest-stakes finding: real Signal-Protocol E2EE ("even your database cannot read them") is mechanically incompatible with server-side content moderation (required for App Store/Play compliance, already shipped, extended twice this session) and with the already-drafted ToS's own "no E2EE" disclosure — same tension `docs/18` §D1 already flagged from a different angle. Recommendation (Path 1: accurate posture copy, not a crypto rewrite) given, **decision explicitly held by the user for later** — nothing built against it either way.

- **Tier A (PR #183, merged, deployed, live):** systematic Zod request validation across every Edge Function with a validatable body (~40 functions, `_shared/validate.ts`), replacing ad hoc hand-rolled checks; Postgres-native fixed-window rate limiting (`fn_check_rate_limit`, deliberately not Redis — no new infrastructure) wired into the genuinely exposed endpoints (`web-send-otp` per-phone+per-IP, `find-user-by-phone(s)` per-caller, `send-message`/`buy-credit` per-caller); two verification checks done directly rather than assumed (HSTS confirmed present live, the built mobile bundle grepped and confirmed to never contain the real service-role key); `CLAUDE.md` gained a Security & Hardening Rules section. One incidental bug found and fixed along the way: three test fixtures seeded threads via a direct SQL insert bypassing `fn_start_thread`, left with a null `payer_id` by this same session's earlier Tier C1 migration (piece 5) — confirmed via grep that no production path has this issue, test-only fix. Full `test:functions` suite (39 files) re-run clean twice.
- **Tier B item 1 (TLS certificate pinning) — built and merged, explicitly held for the next real `eas build`, same holding pattern as piece 2's voice-notes client.** `apps/mobile/plugins/withCertPinning.js`, a custom Expo config plugin (no new npm dependency), pins the Supabase host's TLS chain **at the root CA level, not the leaf** — the live leaf cert (Cloudflare-fronted, confirmed via `openssl s_client`) rotates every ~90 days with zero visibility into that schedule from this app, so all four Google Trust Services root CAs (fetched directly from `pki.goog`) are pinned instead, stable for decades. Android gets a real fail-open safety valve (Network Security Config `expiration`, ~12 months out — pinning silently stops enforcing past that date rather than bricking the app); **iOS has no equivalent mechanism** — a future switch to a non-Google CA for this domain would hard-fail every iOS request until a new build ships, flagged as a real, disclosed residual risk of pinning third-party (Cloudflare/Supabase) infrastructure this app doesn't operate. Verified locally as far as Windows allows: a real `expo prebuild` confirmed the generated Android `network_security_config.xml` and manifest wiring exactly; the iOS Info.plist logic was verified by direct function invocation (full iOS prebuild needs macOS/Linux) against Apple's documented schema. **Cannot ship via OTA — native config baked in at build time — sits inert until bundled into whatever real `eas build` happens next**, per the user's own explicit instruction to build it now and hold it.

**What's still genuinely open, not silently dropped:**

- Voice notes client (docs/17 §10/§11 step 7) **and now also TLS cert pinning (piece 6, Tier B item 1)** — both held, both queued for the next real `eas build` for any reason. Do not start either without that build actually being authorized.
- OpenAI account has no billing for the transcription endpoint audio moderation depends on — real, confirmed, not assumed; worth fixing before voice notes reach general availability, independent of the client-build gate above.
- Tier C2 (boosted status) and Tier D (encryption copy) — still open, no design work started beyond what's in `docs/18`. Tier D and `docs/19` §0's E2EE decision are the same underlying question — resolve together whenever that decision gets made.

## Where we stopped (end of session 33 — four chat/status UX bug fixes + chat photo attachments, all merged and shipped, 2026-09-25) — start here next session

**Explicit user instruction this session: park session 32's planned next step (linked devices, `docs/12`) and instead work through a punch list of mobile UX complaints** (stale presence, chat opening at the wrong scroll position, chat not feeling realtime/smooth, status not expiring, viewed/liked-by being two separate surfaces), refined into five root-caused pieces, worked one at a time (each its own branch/PR). All five are now merged into `main`; the mobile-facing ones are live via OTA. Chat media then went from "scope only" to a full build, also on explicit go-ahead, in the same session.

**Piece 1 (PR #169, merged, `fix/presence-realtime-reliability`):** Realtime's websocket had the exact same background-suspension bug the auth-refresh timer already had a documented fix for (session ~20) — nothing reconnected it after backgrounding, so a thread's live last-seen subscription could silently miss updates. Mirrored the existing `AppState` → auth-refresh pattern in `lib/supabase.ts` for `supabase.realtime.connect()`/`disconnect()`, plus a self-healing foreground refetch of the partner's presence row in `thread/[id].tsx` as a second layer that doesn't depend on the socket redelivering a missed event.

**Piece 2 (PR #170, merged, `fix/thread-realtime-smoothness`):** Two confirmed causes for "doesn't feel like WhatsApp" — the thread `FlatList` never had an initial scroll position (opened at the oldest message every time), and `useThreadMessages` treated every Realtime event as "refetch the whole thread," a full network round trip per new message/read-receipt/escrow-release event. Fixed: the list now lands on the latest message and auto-follows while the reader is near the bottom; Realtime events patch the cached list in place instead of refetching; a new send shows via an optimistic bubble that hands off to the real row from whichever of Realtime/the HTTP response lands first, with a 6s self-healing refetch fallback.

**Piece 3 (PR #171, merged, `fix/status-expiry-both-sides`) — deployed and live, not just merged in code:** `status_updates_select_own` never actually gated on `expires_at`, so a poster's own status stayed visible forever past 24h. Tightened via `alter policy` so expired means gone for the poster too. Added `expire-statuses`, a new Edge Function on the same `pg_net`/`X-Cron-Secret` cron shape as `reconcile-topups`, scheduled every 15 minutes, deployed with `--no-verify-jwt`, verified live via a real deployed-smoke test.

**Piece 4 (PR #172, merged, `feat/status-combined-viewers-list`):** Viewed-by and liked-by used to be two separate surfaces on your own status. Replaced with one `useStatusViewers` hook (union of `status_views`/`status_likes`, both already poster-readable via existing RLS — no migration needed) and one `StatusViewersModal` showing every viewer with a small heart next to whichever ones also liked, matching WhatsApp exactly.

**Piece 5 — chat photo attachments, scoped in `docs/16-CHAT-MEDIA-SCOPING.md` then fully built on explicit go-ahead, same session. Two PRs, both merged:**

- **PR #174 (backend, `feat/chat-media-backend`) — already deployed and live**, same as Piece 3's posture: migration applied directly via `pg.Client`, all three Edge Functions deployed and smoke-tested against the real project. Mirrors the status-media pipeline's shape (private `chat-media` bucket, signed-upload-URL-only writes, `storage.objects` RLS re-deriving the messages/threads visibility rule). `fn_send_message` gained `p_media_path`/`p_media_type` — validates the path was actually minted for the caller **and** that the object actually exists in Storage (a real gap in the _status_ pipeline's own `fn_post_status`, which trusts a caller-supplied path with no ownership check at all — flagged in the migration's header comment, deliberately not fixed there since it's a different feature, not silently carried forward into this new one), bills a new `message_media_credits` surcharge additively on top of the word-count formula, and relaxed `messages.word_count`'s CHECK constraint to `>= 0` (a captionless photo is a real message). `fn_delete_message_for_everyone` now clears media fields and returns what it cleared so the real Storage object gets removed too. Image moderation extended to `moderateImage` (OpenAI `omni-moderation-latest`'s image input) — confirmed reaching OpenAI live during testing (a real per-image parse-error response came back for the test suite's tiny synthetic JPEG, not a stub). **A hardcoded `fn_send_message` grant-check signature string in `admin-message-pricing-strategy-functions.test.js` broke CI after the signature change** (real failure, not a flake — caught and fixed same session before merging).
- **PR #175 (client, `feat/chat-media-client`) — merged, shipped via OTA to the `preview` channel.** Composer attach button (camera/gallery via ActionSheet, resize/compress to 1600px JPEG — not the architecture doc's aspirational WebP, matching StatusComposer's own proven-safe choice), optimistic send reusing Piece 2's own bubble mechanism, `MessageBubble` image rendering with a plain full-screen tap-to-view (deliberately no pinch-zoom — matches `StoryViewer`'s own existing pattern rather than risking new Reanimated/Gesture-Handler code, which this codebase has a documented bad history with). Forwarding a media message is excluded (forwarding only ever carries `body` today). Offline media sends are rejected with a clear error, not silently queued (the outbox has no concept of re-uploading a local file later). No new native dependencies or `app.json` changes — confirmed via real EAS build history that `expo-image-picker`/`expo-image-manipulator` are already compiled into the currently-installed build (no build has run since the one that shipped status media, which uses these same two libraries) — OTA-eligible, which is exactly how it shipped.

**What's still genuinely open, not silently dropped:**

- None of this session's mobile changes (Pieces 1, 2, 4, and Piece 5's client half) were confirmed on a real device/simulator — no device was available. Typecheck/lint pass for all of them, and everything DB/Edge-Function-side has real test coverage against the live project, but an actual on-device pass is still owed.
- The pre-existing `fn_post_status` media-path-ownership gap (see Piece 5 above) is real and documented but not fixed — a genuine follow-up if picked up.
- Chat media's own scoping doc (`docs/16` §2) explicitly left video, voice notes, documents, albums, forwarding, and a media-grid gallery view out of v1 — all still out, none silently expanded into.
- Session 32's own next-step handoff (linked devices, `docs/12-LINKED-DEVICES-WEB-SCOPING.md`) is still exactly where it was — deliberately parked again this session, not forgotten.

**Next session:** linked devices (`docs/12`) is still the queued next major piece per session 32's own handoff, now twice-deferred. An on-device pass to actually confirm this session's mobile work (presence, thread smoothness, status viewers, chat photos) looks/feels right is a real, cheap thing to do first if a device becomes available.

**Two follow-up passes reshaped what Phase C shipped, both on direct user feedback, both merged (PR #164, #166):** signup moved out of the homepage hero into its own dedicated `/signup` page (blending a form into marketing content wasn't standard practice); the whole site was then repositioned end-to-end from "pay per message" (spender-first) to "earn per message" (earner-first) — every page's headline/copy reframed around what the _replier_ gets, a new shared `<WhoItsFor />` persona component (time-conscious professionals, people who value personal space, remote tutors, high-attention people) on Home and About, and an explicit "who earns first, and why" explainer on How it works, backed by a real new `platform_earning_take_bps` field added to `get-public-pricing`'s whitelist so per-reply earnings figures are live config, not invented copy. The nav was reorganized from 7 flat top-level links into **Product** (How it works/Guide/Pricing) and **Company** (About/Security & Trust/Contact) dropdown groups plus Download standalone — a new click-to-open dropdown pattern, no new dependency.

**Also shipped (PR #165): a fully animated countdown to the December 1, 2026 launch on the Home hero (Framer Motion, careful to never call `Date.now()` during the initial render to avoid a hydration mismatch), and a new `/guide` page** walking through signup (including the 100-non-withdrawable-credit early-adopter bonus, deliberately scoped as guide _copy_ describing a real launch-day commitment, not a live backend feature — `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` flags signup-bonus-style incentives as blocked on an OTP/carrier-detection prerequisite that isn't built, so the actual credit-granting mechanism is a real open item for whoever picks it up, not an oversight), downloading, how credits work, withdrawing, and community standards — every specific claim there is a plain-language translation of `packages/legal-content/terms.ts`'s real clauses, same principle the Security & Trust page already used.

Caught and fixed a real bug from the first pass at this in the same session: the splash-screen asset had mistakenly inherited Android adaptive-icon's 62% safe-zone shrink (a real launcher-mask constraint splash doesn't have), making it read far smaller than intended — direct user feedback ("too tiny and not sharp") caught this. Also added a real `AnimatedSplash` component (`apps/mobile/components/AnimatedSplash.tsx`): the native static splash hands off the instant it mounts, plays a brief Reanimated entrance, then fades to reveal the app once session state resolves — same window the old `if (isLoading) return null` guard covered, just with a real animated cover instead of a blank frame.
