## Where we stopped (end of session 31 — admin UI/UX overhaul, Piece 4 closed + dashboard home built, 2026-09-24)

**Picked up session 30's admin UI/UX workstream at Piece 4 (responsive verification pass), on explicit user direction.** The Chrome browser tool never connected this session — `tabs_context_mcp` failed every retry, `list_connected_browsers` returned `[]`, and `switch_browser` found nothing to broadcast to, even after the user fully restarted Chrome twice and confirmed the extension was enabled and signed in. This is a harder failure than session 30's (where the tool connected but `resize_window` silently didn't affect the real viewport) — worth a closer look if it recurs, since it blocks all live UI verification, not just narrow-viewport testing. User chose to proceed with a code-level audit instead of waiting further (PR merged as #160).

**Real, confirmed bug found and fixed**: 6 of the app's `<table>` elements — all 4 in `users/[id]/page.tsx` (KYC, Bank accounts, Withdrawals, Transaction history) and both in `treasury/page.tsx` (Daily activity, Withdrawals) — were missing the `overflow-x-auto` wrapper `div` that every other table in the app already uses (`users/page.tsx`, `pricing/history/page.tsx` both have it). Without it, those tables would force the entire page to scroll horizontally on a narrow viewport instead of scrolling within their own box — a real mobile-layout break, not a hypothetical one, found by direct comparison across every table in the codebase. Fixed by wrapping all 6 in `<div className="mt-N overflow-x-auto">…</div>`, matching the existing pattern exactly. Every other page's tables/forms/grids (fraud-signals, user-reports, pricing × 3, pending-actions, admins × 2, users/analytics, the Sidebar/drawer itself) were read in full and already use `flex-wrap`, `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-*`, `max-w-*`, and `w-full` correctly — no other responsive anti-pattern found.

**Not done, and this is the real gap**: none of this was confirmed live at a real narrow viewport — the browser tool blocker made that impossible from this side both this session and last. `turbo run lint typecheck --filter=admin` passes and the local dev server compiled every touched route with zero runtime errors, which is real but weaker evidence than a screenshot. **Worth knowing for next time**: mid-session, the dev server log showed a devtunnel (`*.euw.devtunnels.ms`) already forwarding `localhost:3000`, apparently the user testing live on their own end — this hit a real, previously-undiscovered bug: Next.js's Server Actions origin check rejects `POST /login` when `x-forwarded-host` (the tunnel's own host) doesn't match `origin` (`localhost:3000`), logged as `Invalid Server Actions request` / `POST /login 500`. Not fixed this session (out of scope, not asked for) — but if devtunnel-based real-device testing becomes the standing workaround for this project's recurring "can't get a real narrow viewport" problem, `next.config.ts`'s `experimental.serverActions.allowedOrigins` will need the tunnel host added, or every Server Action on every tunneled test will 500.

**Also built, on direct user request mid-session (not originally part of Piece 4)**: the dashboard home page (`/dashboard`) had shown Phase A's placeholder text ("Real feature pages start in Phase B") unedited since session 28, even though every phase through G is now built. Replaced with real permission-gated stat tiles — one query per section the admin can actually see, mirroring the sidebar's own permission gates exactly: total users, treasury revenue by currency (both revenue wallet kinds, correctly currency/unit-aware via the existing `formatWalletAmount`), unresolved fraud-signal count, unresolved user-report count, pending-actions-awaiting-decision count, admin account count. Each tile links to its full page. No new dependency, no chart library (matches this app's established zero-chart-library posture) — plain stat cards in the same `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3` responsive pattern the rest of the dashboard already uses.

**User then asked for a broader "polish every other page" pass; scoped it with a clarifying question (missing functionality vs. visual polish vs. consistency pass vs. something specific) and the user said "its good for now"** — so that broader ask is deliberately deferred, not forgotten, not silently dropped. Nothing further was built beyond the dashboard home and the table fix.

**Shipped**: PR #160 (`feat(admin): responsive table fix + real dashboard home`), squash-merged directly to `main` on explicit user instruction ("merge now"), feature branch deleted. `docs/14`'s ad-hoc UI/UX workstream (session 30 + this session) now has Pieces 1–4 all closed — Piece 4's live-viewport confirmation is the one open thread, blocked on the browser tool, not on missing work.

**Next session: no fixed next step handed off** — the user didn't specify one beyond what's captured above. If picking this up cold: check whether the Chrome extension connects before assuming it still doesn't (retry `tabs_context_mcp`/`list_connected_browsers` early), and if it does, get the real screenshot confirmation Piece 4 has been missing for two sessions running now.

## Where we stopped (end of session 30 — admin dashboard UI/UX overhaul, in progress, 2026-09-24) — start here next session

**With `docs/14` fully built (see the session-29 entry below), the user asked for a real UI/UX pass on the admin dashboard itself: apply the real brand + light/dark mode, replace the header nav with a collapsible sidebar organized by parent/child, fix dropdown-contrast issues, give staff/admins their own management interface (turned out to already exist), and make the whole thing mobile-responsive.** This isn't in `docs/14` — it's a new, ad-hoc workstream on the same app. Two pieces built and merged this session; two more scoped but not started.

**Piece 1 (PR #158, merged) — Deep Wine rebrand, light/dark mode, dropdown-contrast fix.** Real, confirmed gap: `apps/admin/app/globals.css` was still the unedited Next.js template (hardcoded dark placeholder palette, zero light-mode support) since Phase A — `docs/14`'s original scoping call was explicitly _not_ to give admin its own design system ("no reason to hand-roll one for an internal tool"). This reverses that call on explicit request, mirroring `docs/04-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`'s already-shipping Deep Wine palette exactly (same hex values as `apps/mobile/theme/tokens.ts`). Full light/dark token set, three-state theming (system preference, explicit `next-themes`-driven override wins in both directions), existing token _names_ (`--foreground`/`--background`/`--surface`/`--border`/`--accent`) kept since hundreds of call sites already reference them. **Real, confirmed bug fixed**: every `<select>` in the app used `bg-transparent` with light text — native `<option>` popups don't inherit that, so they rendered with browser-default (usually white) chrome under light text, near-zero contrast. Fixed globally via `color-scheme` + explicit `select, option` CSS rules, not per-component. 86 hardcoded Tailwind color utilities (`text-red-400`, `bg-green-600`, etc. — never adapted to theme at all) across 25 files replaced with new semantic tokens (`--danger`/`--success`/`--warning`/`--on-accent`) via a scripted exact-string replacement. Verified via real browser screenshots in both themes (not just lint/build) plus a live `getComputedStyle` check on a fresh `<select>`/`<option>` pair.

**Piece 2 (PR #159, merged) — collapsible, grouped sidebar.** Replaces the flat 9-link horizontal header nav. Sub-routes that already have a real URL hierarchy fold into their parent (Users → Location stats; Pricing → Service pricing/Strategy/History; Admins → Create admin) rather than staying flat. Collapses to an icon-only rail (user's confirmed preference over fully-hidden), persisted via `localStorage`. Mobile: slide-in drawer with backdrop below `md`. **A real Next.js RSC bug was found and fixed while building this**: a plain data export (`NAV_ICONS`, holding live lucide-react icon component references) from a `'use client'` file, consumed by the Server Component layout to build a props array — that crossing silently fails ("Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components"), confirmed via a live browser check where every icon came back `undefined` despite resolving fine in isolation and despite `tsc`/`eslint` both passing clean. Fixed by passing only serializable string icon keys across the boundary; the real icon lookup map lives client-side, next to where it's rendered. Also added `experimental.optimizePackageImports: ['lucide-react']` to `next.config.ts` (Next's own docs name this exact package) — without it, Turbopack's dev bundler failed to resolve lucide-react's barrel-file icon imports at all, not just a bundle-size cost.

**Verification technique worth knowing about for next session**: no admin session can be minted in this environment (established earlier this session, memory `browser-auth-session-testing-blocked`), so real UI pieces this session were verified by temporarily adding an unauthenticated preview route (outside the `(dashboard)` auth-gated route group) rendering the component in question with fake props, screenshotting/interacting with it via the browser tool, then **deleting the route before committing** — never shipped. This is how the RSC bug above was actually caught; lint/typecheck/build all passed cleanly with the bug present. Worth doing again for future UI-heavy pieces in this app.

**Piece 3 — staff/admin management interface: confirmed already sufficient, not rebuilt.** The user asked whether `/dashboard/admins` (built session 29, Phase G piece 2) already satisfies "separate interface, edit permissions when clicked" — asked directly, confirmed yes (the inline "click Edit roles, a panel expands in the same row" pattern is fine as-is). No changes made. The other named gap ("show name not email") was already fixed as a side effect of piece 2's sidebar rebuild — the one place email leaked (top-right header corner) no longer exists in that form.

**Piece 4 — responsive verification pass: scoped, not started.** The sidebar rebuild structurally fixes most of the nav's own mobile problem, but two things remain unverified/undone: (1) the mobile drawer itself was never seen at an actual narrow viewport — the available browser-resize tool in this environment didn't affect the real render viewport (confirmed: `resize_window` reported success but `window.innerWidth` stayed at the wide default), so this needs a real check on an actual phone or a manually narrowed desktop browser before it's trusted; (2) no page's own _content_ (tables, forms) has had a dedicated mobile pass yet — they rely on the `overflow-x-auto` baseline from earlier phases, untested at real phone widths.

**Next session: start with piece 4** (get a real narrow-viewport check somehow — ask the user to test on their end if this environment still can't do it — then do a focused pass over each page's tables/forms), unless the user redirects. After that, this ad-hoc UI/UX workstream is done.

## Where we stopped (end of session 29, continued again — Phase G complete, `docs/14` fully built, 2026-09-24) — start here next session

**Phase G (`docs/14-ADMIN-DASHBOARD-SCOPING.md` §8, polish) is done — all three scoped pieces built and merged this session, same session as everything below. With this, every phase (A through G) plus both items trimmed out of Phase D's original scope is live. Nothing is left open in `docs/14`.** A real pre-build diligence pass first found that most of §8's own "traffic-scale defaults" ask (cursor pagination, `pg_trgm`-indexed search, server-side aggregation) was already built as a byproduct of Phase B/C — Phase G turned out to be a much smaller punch list than the doc's own framing suggested. It also surfaced a real §7-vs-§8 tension (§8 lists "threads" among list views that should get search; §7 explicitly commits to "never a blanket message-search-everyone feature") — resolved by scoping general thread search out entirely, doc-recorded, not silently built.

- **Piece 1 (PR #155)** — user location stats. New `/dashboard/users/analytics`: a live `GROUP BY country` count via a new `users_country_stats` Postgres view (`security_invoker`, explicit revoke/grant, same pattern as Treasury's `platform_wallet_daily_totals`), honest "Unknown" bucket for pre-country-capture/incomplete-onboarding users rather than a guess. No new charting dependency — plain CSS bars, matching this app's existing zero-chart-library posture. The doc's optional reverse-geocode historical backfill job was deliberately not built — depends on whether Supabase Auth actually retains historical sign-in IPs on this project, unconfirmed, flagged as a possible follow-up.
- **Piece 2 (PR #156)** — admins list/management page. A real gap found independent of §8's own list (not one of its named items): only `/dashboard/admins/new` (create) existed, nothing to view/edit/disable an existing admin. Closed two pre-existing gaps in the process — `admin_users.disabled_at` had been checked at login since Phase A with nothing to ever set it, and `fn_admin_reset_mfa` had existed since Phase A with zero UI wired to it. New `fn_admin_set_admin_account_status` (dual-approval, both directions — §4.4 explicitly names "disabling another admin" as materially risky; this migration reasoned its way to extending the same treatment to reactivation, since a compromised `manage_admin_roles` holder reinstating an off-boarded account is symmetrically dangerous) and `fn_admin_set_admin_roles` (single-admin, matching the doc's materiality list, which names disabling specifically and nothing else here). Self-target blocked on role changes; deliberately _allowed_ on status changes (proposing to disable your own suspected-compromised account is legitimate — the safety property is "a different admin must approve," same as every other dual-approved action, not a target-must-not-be-self rule). 17 new test assertions. Verified: lint/typecheck/clean-build all pass, every page query shape and RPC parameter name cross-checked against the live dev DB via a throwaway script separate from the pg-based test suite (to also exercise the actual supabase-js client shape the real Server Actions use).
- **Piece 3 (PR #157)** — CSV export for the Users list, the last item in this entire document. Deliberate deviation from §8's literal "async export job" wording (no job-queue infra exists anywhere in this codebase, same shape of reasoned deviation as Phase A's argon2->scrypt call) — synchronous but hard-capped at 20,000 rows, refused (not silently truncated) above the cap. Required this app's **first Route Handler** (`/api/export/users`) — Server Actions can't hand the browser a file to download, and `middleware.ts` only matches `/dashboard/:path*`, so the handler re-derives and re-checks the admin itself rather than relying on any page-level gate. New `apps/admin/lib/csv.ts`, a minimal RFC 4180 writer, no new dependency. Verified: lint/typecheck/clean-build pass, the route correctly 401s with no session, every query shape DB-verified, and CSV escaping (comma/quote/newline) verified correct in isolation.
- **A real CI-vs-local test contention incident happened mid-piece** (documented in memory `ci-vs-local-test-contention`, this is its second occurrence this session): running `npm run test:admin-account-management` locally right after merging PR #155 raced with that merge's own `push`-triggered CI run, both toggling `admin_audit_log_no_delete` concurrently — a spurious "append-only" failure, not a real bug, confirmed by re-running clean after the CI run finished (17/17 passed). Leftover test fixtures from the crashed run were found and cleaned up; the append-only trigger's live state was checked and confirmed correctly enabled (not left disabled by the crash).

## Where we stopped (end of session 29, continued — §4.3 pricing-strategy-switch framework, 2026-09-24) — start here next session

**`docs/14-ADMIN-DASHBOARD-SCOPING.md` §4.3 is now fully built end-to-end, both pieces, same session as Phase E/F/the Service Pricing toggle below.** With this, every phase/item in `docs/14` is built except Phase G (§8 polish — analytics/location stats, saved views, CSV export — explicitly low-priority, not urgent).

- **Piece 1 (PR #153)** — schema + functions. `message_pricing_strategy` (per-currency, since `pricing_config` is bigint-only and a strategy tag isn't a number), three pure `STABLE` pricing functions (`fn_price_message_tiered_word_block`/`fn_price_message_flat`/`fn_price_message_linear`, each fails loudly if its currency's config is missing rather than silently defaulting), `fn_admin_set_message_pricing_strategy` (dual-approval-gated unconditionally — a strategy switch changes the shape of the anti-abuse economics, not just a rate, so it doesn't get the `_bps`-only materiality carve-out). `fn_send_message` refactored (dropped and recreated from its live `pg_get_functiondef`, every other line copied verbatim) to fetch the payer's wallet — including currency — before computing price, then dispatch via a plain `CASE` statement, never dynamic SQL. Confirmed `message_max_words` stays a strategy-independent hard guard, closing off the `flat_per_message` exploit-reopening risk `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §1 would otherwise flag. Also fixed a latent bug found while touching this code: `message_max_words` lookup wasn't currency-filtered before. 31 assertions, including a real end-to-end test that switches a QA currency's strategy through the actual admin RPC flow and confirms `fn_send_message` charges differently under each of the three strategies.
- **Piece 2 (PR #154)** — the UI. New `/dashboard/pricing/strategy` page: each currency's active strategy, a plain-English description of what it does and which `pricing_config` keys tune it, and a propose-switch form (disabled while a change is already pending/approved, same "one open proposal at a time per row" pattern the rest of the dashboard uses). Wired `message_pricing_strategy_change` into the pending-actions queue's describe/apply branches. The two new `pricing_config` keys from piece 1 (`message_flat_credits`, `message_credits_per_100_words`) categorized on the main pricing editor. Verified: `turbo run lint typecheck --filter=admin` clean, a real clean `next build` (dev server killed, `.next` removed first — this exact contention has bitten this session before), and the page's precise Supabase query shapes plus every RPC parameter name cross-checked against the live dev DB and the migration source directly (not assumed).

## Where we stopped (end of session 29, 2026-09-24) — start here next session

**Phase E (dual-approval workflow) and Phase F (treasury withdrawal) are both fully built, merged, and deployed this session — `docs/14-ADMIN-DASHBOARD-SCOPING.md` §4.4 and §5 are done.** Seven PRs (#145–#151), one piece at a time, same discipline as every phase before. The Service Pricing toggle (§4.2's "free-vs-charged" item, deliberately trimmed out of Phase D) was also built this session as a small standalone piece, once Phase E/F cleared — see below.

**Phase E, three pieces (PR #145, #146, #147):**

- **Piece 1 (#145)** — `admin_pending_actions` + `fn_admin_propose_pending_action`/`fn_admin_approve_pending_action`/`fn_admin_reject_pending_action`/`fn_admin_consume_approved_pending_action`. Real finding from the pre-build review: `finance_admin`/`super_admin` already held both the propose and approve permissions together, so `fn_admin_approve_pending_action` hard-blocks `requested_by = actor` regardless of permissions held — role separation alone doesn't guarantee two different people. A real bug was caught by the test suite itself, not hypothetical: an early draft tried to flip a stale row to `'expired'` and then `RAISE EXCEPTION` in the same call — the raise silently unwound the update, so the flip never persisted (an uncaught raise rolls back everything the current call did). Fixed; the raise alone is the real enforcement, a cosmetic hourly cron just keeps the _displayed_ status honest. 32 assertions.
- **Piece 2 (#146)** — retrofit `fn_admin_update_pricing_config` so any `_bps` key requires a matching approved pending action (previously applied every change immediately, confirmed directly); built `fn_admin_post_manual_adjustment`, the first-ever function in this codebase that posts a manual ledger adjustment, dual-approval-gated unconditionally since it's a first build, not a retrofit. 31 assertions across two suites.
- **Piece 3 (#147)** — `/dashboard/pending-actions` queue UI, the pricing editor's propose/apply flow, a manual-adjustment form on the user detail page. Verified end-to-end against the live dev DB with a throwaway script (propose → approve → apply for both action types) rather than a live browser click-through — no way to mint/obtain a real admin session from this environment, and the `__text_mode_READY__` extension hydration issue from prior sessions is assumed still present, not re-checked this session either. **Still worth a real click-through by the user** before treating this piece as fully proven the way Phase A's UI was.

**Phase F, three PRs plus a production deploy (#148, #149, #150, #151):**

- **Piece 1 (#148)** — `platform_bank_accounts` (dual-approved registration, single-admin deactivation — deliberate asymmetry: registering a destination is the enabling step for theft via a single compromised account, deactivating only removes an option) + `fn_admin_convert_platform_earnings_to_cash`. **Real finding, not in the original scope**: `platform_revenue_topup_fees` (kobo) and `platform_revenue_earnings_cut` (credits) are denominated in different units — §5's "debits ...by the requested amount" glossed over this. The earnings-cut wallet needs converting to cash first, the same `earnings_pending → withdrawable_cash` shape every user earning already goes through, which never existed on the platform side until this piece. Also corrected: §5 pointed at `fn_initiate_withdrawal` (single-wallet) as the ascending-lock-order pattern to reuse — the real template is `fn_transfer_credit`, and the _actual_ two-wallet operation in this phase is the earnings conversion, not the withdrawal itself. 22 assertions.
- **Piece 2 (#149)** — `platform_withdrawals` + `fn_admin_initiate_platform_withdrawal`/`fn_admin_complete_platform_withdrawal`/`fn_admin_fail_platform_withdrawal`. Deliberately pure Postgres — none of these call `packages/payments`' `initiatePayout()`, which stays one layer up exactly where `fn_initiate_withdrawal` already keeps it, so this piece carries zero real-money risk from its own test suite. Confirmed directly that `packages/payments` has no Deno-specific dependencies (plain `fetch`/`node:buffer`/`node:crypto`) — resolved the original scope's open question, it's importable straight into the Next.js admin app. 23 assertions including a real 6-way concurrent-webhook-redelivery race.
- **Piece 3a (#150)** — `webhook-flutterwave` extended to route `transfer.completed` to the new platform-withdrawal completion functions as a fallback (existing user-withdrawal success/failure paths untouched byte-for-byte) when the reference doesn't match a `withdrawals` row. **Deployed to production this session** (`supabase functions deploy webhook-flutterwave --use-api --no-verify-jwt`, version 12→13, `verify_jwt: false` confirmed) and verified live against the real deployed endpoint via the project's own existing deployed-smoke test (2/2 passing) — not just the local `deno run` harness, per this project's own hard-learned lesson about that exact gap.
- **Piece 3b (#151)** — the real UI: bank account registration (two-step — "Look up" resolves the real name via `resolveBankAccountName` without proposing anything, "Propose registration" then calls `createTransferRecipient` for real and proposes with the resolved payload), deactivation, earnings-conversion form, withdrawal propose form, withdrawal history. `apps/admin` now depends on `@involveme/payments` directly (added to its `package.json`, resolved via the existing npm workspace, no new infra). The one branch that calls a real payment provider (`applyPlatformWithdrawal`) is pulled out of the otherwise-uniform single-RPC apply branches and mirrors `withdraw/index.ts`'s exact debit → resolve-destination-server-side → call-provider → compensate-on-failure shape. **Never scripted against the live Flutterwave API** (deliberately) — verified by exact RPC-parameter cross-checks against the migrations plus a DB-level-only throwaway script standing in for the provider-calling parts, and by code review against the proven `withdraw/index.ts` pattern. `next build` clean, `turbo run lint typecheck` clean.

**A real cross-run test contention bug was diagnosed this session, not a code regression** — recorded in memory (`ci-vs-local-test-contention`), worth knowing for next session too: merging a PR triggers a `push`-triggered CI run that exercises the _entire_ `admin-*` test chain against the same shared dev DB local sessions also use. A local test run overlapping with PR #148's post-merge CI caused a spurious cross-file "append-only" failure in `admin-rbac-functions.test.js` (an unrelated file, run by CI, not locally) — diagnosed via the failure's exact signature (that PR's own gating `pull_request` CI had already passed cleanly). Check `gh run list --limit 3` before running local `admin-*` suites right after a merge.

**Both production deployment prerequisites the user asked about directly are now done**: `apps/admin/.env.local` has the real Flutterwave production credentials (copied from the project root `.env`, not `apps/mobile/.env` — mobile has none, Flutterwave calls are server-only per `CLAUDE.md` rule #1) so the treasury flows can be exercised for real locally; the webhook is live in production. **Not done, and not this session's to do**: the same env vars on the actual Netlify production deployment — no Netlify CLI session/credentials available from this environment (confirmed: `netlify status` → "Not logged in"), a manual step on the user's end, same as `docs/14` §3/§8.1 already documented for this project's Netlify setup generally.

**Service Pricing toggle (§4.2, trimmed from Phase D) built as a small standalone piece after Phase E/F cleared it to be revisited**: `/dashboard/pricing/services`, a curated table (not a new DB table — one real entry, `group_chat_enabled`, didn't justify a registry table on spec; `apps/admin/lib/servicePricing.ts` is the extensible list, same shape `lib/pricingCategories.ts` already established) showing every free-vs-charged kill-switch with a one-click Enable/Disable, writing through the exact same `fn_admin_update_pricing_config` every other non-`_bps` key already uses — no new write path, no new dual-approval decision (the currently-shipped materiality rule is `_bps`-keys-only; this isn't one). `docs/14`'s top-of-file status line, which still said "nothing in this document is built" as of session 28, is corrected.

**§4.3 was also picked up and finished later this same session — see the "continued" entry above.** With that done, `docs/14` has nothing left open except Phase G (explicitly low-priority). Next session: Phase G, or something outside this workstream entirely (`docs/11` LiveKit calls, `docs/12` linked devices/web client, `docs/15` marketing site/PWA, the netinfo native-module build queued since session 26). Do the same real pre-build diligence pass this document's every phase so far has gotten before committing to a piece breakdown — this session's own findings (platform wallet unit-denomination, the wrong lock-pattern reference, the stale doc status line) are proof that pass keeps finding real things, not a formality.

## Where we stopped (end of session 28, 2026-09-20) — start here next session

User asked for a real pre-build engineering review of `docs/14-ADMIN-DASHBOARD-SCOPING.md` before starting Phase A, then to scope and build Phase A one piece at a time — same discipline as every prior punch-list session. The review caught a real factual error in the doc itself (not assumed — checked directly): §2 claimed `pricing_config_history` doesn't exist; it does, with a working audit trigger since the very first schema migration, just with `changed_by` hardcoded to `'system'`. Corrected in place (PR #127) rather than left standing. Also closed 4 real pre-build gaps the original scoping pass hadn't addressed — bootstrap chicken-and-egg, which auth mechanism to build, MFA recovery path, CI/deploy wiring — folded into `docs/14` §8.1 as an addendum (PR #125) before any code was written.

**Phase A (admin identity & RBAC foundation) is fully built and merged — three PRs, one piece at a time, each with real CI and, for the last one, a real end-to-end browser test against the live dev DB:**

**Piece 1 (PR #126) — `apps/admin/` Next.js skeleton.** New workspace member, App Router, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4. Joins the existing turbo/CI setup with zero CI file changes needed (the existing `lint-and-typecheck` job already covers any workspace with `lint`/`typecheck` scripts). `shadcn/ui` deliberately deferred to Phase B — not worth scaffolding a component kit for a login form and a placeholder.

**Piece 2 (PR #128) — RBAC schema + `SECURITY DEFINER` functions.** `admin_users`/`admin_roles`/`admin_permissions`/`admin_role_permissions`/`admin_user_roles`/`admin_audit_log`, RLS-enabled-zero-policies, seeded with the 6 roles from `docs/14` §4.1. `admin_audit_log` is append-only enforced by a **blocking trigger** (same pattern as `ledger_entries`' `prevent_ledger_mutation`, not just a REVOKE — this project already learned once, the hard way, that a bare REVOKE can be a no-op on this Supabase project's default-ACL setup) plus an explicit REVOKE so even the Next.js backend's service-role connection can't write to it except through `fn_admin_log_action`. 9 `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, each with the explicit revoke/grant pair CLAUDE.md rule #11 requires. Two counters (failed-login lockout, recovery-code consumption) are row-locked against real concurrent races, same discipline as wallet balances — verified under genuine concurrent load in the new `supabase/tests/admin-rbac-functions.test.js` (37 assertions, wired into CI as `npm run test:admin:ci`).

**Piece 3 (PR #129) — Next.js auth wiring.** Password hashing via Node's built-in `scrypt`, not a native argon2 package — a deliberate deviation from the addendum's original "argon2id" wording, decided while actually building this piece: same memory-hard-KDF security property, zero new native-compiled dependency (this project has none anywhere today), matching `CLAUDE.md` rule #10's "check existing tooling first." TOTP via `otpauth` (pure JS), session as a short-lived signed JWT cookie (`jose`, edge-compatible) verified in `middleware.ts` on every `/dashboard/**` request. Every Server Action re-derives the caller from their own session cookie and re-checks permission server-side — never trusts a client-passed value. `scripts/create-first-admin.ts` is the only way to create the first `admin_users` row; it refuses to run if the table isn't empty and deliberately skips MFA setup itself, so the first real login walks through the exact same `/mfa-enroll` flow every later admin uses.

**This piece was verified for real, not just built:** ran the bootstrap script against the live dev DB, then drove the entire flow in an actual Chrome browser (via claude-in-chrome) — bootstrap → login → real TOTP enrollment (code computed with `otpauth` the same way an authenticator app would, not faked) → dashboard with the create-admin link correctly visible → created a second admin with the `support_agent` role → logged out → logged in as that second admin → confirmed its own independent MFA enrollment → confirmed the create-admin link is hidden **and** the page itself redirects away for that account (defense in depth, not just a hidden link) → confirmed every step wrote the expected `admin_audit_log` row directly against the database. **A real bug was caught and fixed in the process, not hypothetical:** Node's `scrypt` default `maxmem` (32MB) rejects `N=2^15` at its default blockSize — the memory need lands almost exactly on that boundary — fixed with an explicit, generous `maxmem`. Both test admin accounts and their audit rows were deleted afterward (same disable-trigger/delete/re-enable pattern `wallet-functions.test.js` already established for `ledger_entries`), so `admin_users` is empty again and ready for a real bootstrap run.

**Phase A's exit criteria (`docs/14` §9) is met and directly verified, not assumed:** a super admin can log in, create a second admin with a limited role, and every action either takes produces a real audit row.

To actually use this locally: `cp apps/admin/.env.example apps/admin/.env.local`, fill in `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`/`ADMIN_SESSION_SECRET`/`ADMIN_TOTP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (generation commands are in the file's own comments), then `npm run create-first-admin --workspace admin -- --email you@involveme.com --name "Your Name" --password "..."` — see `apps/admin/README.md`. The user has since bootstrapped 3 real admin accounts of their own.

**Phase B (read-only visibility: users, wallets, transactions, treasury) is also fully built and merged this same session — three more pieces, same one-at-a-time/PR/merge discipline, real browser verification against real data throughout:**

**Piece 1 (PR #131) — search/pagination infra + Users list.** New migration adds `pg_trgm` GIN indexes on `users.phone`/`users.display_name` — confirmed directly no search index existed on this table before (`pg_trgm` was only ever used for fraud duplicate-content detection). `lib/pagination.ts`: cursor-based (seek-method) pagination on `(created_at, id)`, not offset, per `docs/14` §8's explicit instruction. `/dashboard/users`: search + paginate, gated behind `view_users` (already seeded).

**Piece 2 (PR #132) — user detail page.** `/dashboard/users/[id]`: a user's 3 wallets with live balances/frozen status, KYC records, linked bank accounts, withdrawal history, and a paginated/filterable `ledger_entries` view. Two real findings caught before shipping: `ledger_entries.reason`'s check constraint has been widened 5 times since the original schema (23 values live today, not the documented 12 — `lib/ledgerReasons.ts` has a curated label per value plus a title-case fallback so a future addition degrades gracefully); and amount formatting had to be gotten right per wallet _kind_, confirmed against `apps/mobile`'s own convention rather than guessed (`lib/money.ts` — `topup_credit`/`earnings_pending` are credit-denominated, `withdrawable_cash` is kobo; getting this backwards would misrepresent a balance by 1000x). Also converted every internal `<a href>` in `apps/admin` to `next/link`'s `<Link>`, caught by eslint's `no-html-link-for-pages` rule on this piece and applied everywhere for consistency.

**Piece 3 (PR #134) — Treasury view.** `/dashboard/treasury`: the 4 platform wallets' live balances (grouped by currency, revenue vs. reserve), a server-aggregated daily-activity table (new `platform_wallet_daily_totals` view, `security_invoker=true` — same load-bearing reasoning `ledger_entries_chat_counterparty_view.sql` already documents), and a placeholder noting platform-withdrawal history is Phase F's to build. Manual refresh only (explicit choice over Realtime — Phase B's exit criteria is visibility, not live-push). Gated behind `view_treasury`. **Second real unit-denomination finding, more consequential than piece 2's:** `platform_revenue_earnings_cut`/`platform_reserve_earnings_cut` are _also_ credit-denominated, not kobo like the topup-fee wallets — found by reading every `fn_credit_platform_revenue` call site directly (escrow release, credit transfer, group message all take their cut as a percentage of credits, with no cash-conversion entry ever written for the platform's own wallets, unlike a user's earnings_pending → withdrawable_cash pairing). `lib/money.ts`'s credit-denominated set now includes these two.

**A genuinely serious bug was found and fixed mid-piece-3, not a hypothetical (PR #133), and the whole phase paused for it:** building the Treasury view surfaced that all 4 platform wallets showed a real ₦0.00/0-credit balance in the live dev DB despite substantial paid message activity and 14 completed top-ups with real fees (~₦46) having actually happened. Root-caused, not assumed: a `resetPlatformWallets`-shaped helper existed in **six** test files (`wallet-functions`, `chargeback-functions`, `group-chat-functions`, `send-message-function`, `transfer-credit-function`, `webhook-flutterwave-function`), each unconditionally deleting _every_ `ledger_entries` row on these four wallets and zeroing their balance as routine cleanup — but unlike every other cleanup helper in this suite (scoped to one fresh random-UUID test user, safe to delete), these four wallets are a **shared, global, production-adjacent resource**. Every test run's cleanup was destroying every other run's — and every real top-up's/message's — revenue history right along with its own, on `npm run test:db` locally and in CI's `test-wallet-code` job on every PR touching `supabase/migrations/`, which is most of this project's history including several PRs merged earlier in this same session. Asked the user how to handle it before continuing; they chose "fix now, as its own PR, before Treasury." Fix: removed every such helper and its call sites; the two call sites that genuinely needed the wallet to look like it started at a known value switched to snapshot-before/assert-delta (the pattern `chargeback-functions.test.js` already used correctly everywhere else in that file) rather than requiring an absolute post-test value — every other call site's assertion was already relative (ledger-sum-equals-balance conservation, which holds regardless of starting balance) and never actually needed the reset. All 6 affected suites re-verified passing after the fix (127 assertions total). `supabase/tests/README.md` gets a new section recording this so a future test file copy-pasting an old one doesn't reintroduce it.

**Phase B's exit criteria (`docs/14` §9) is met:** an ops person can answer "what does this user's wallet look like and why" (Users list → user detail) and "what does the company currently hold in revenue" (Treasury) without opening Supabase Studio — and that revenue figure is now honest, not perpetually reset to zero by the project's own test suite.

**Phase C (reports & fraud review queue) is also fully built and merged this same session — three more pieces:**

**Piece 1 (PR #136) — schema + resolve/freeze/suspend functions.** Two real gaps closed before writing any UI: `is_frozen`/`is_suspended` had literally never been set by any admin action anywhere in this codebase (confirmed by grep — `20260915093000_collusion_detection.sql`'s own header says so: "this app has no admin UI... this job NEVER sets `is_frozen` itself"), and neither `fraud_signals` nor `user_reports` had any resolution-tracking columns. New `resolve_user_report` permission, distinct from `resolve_fraud_signal` — `docs/14` §4.1's own role table wants `support_agent` to resolve reports without touching fraud signals/wallets, which one shared permission couldn't express. 4 new `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (`fn_admin_set_wallet_frozen`/`fn_admin_set_user_suspended` — the first-ever admin-triggerable freeze/suspend paths, both reversible; `fn_admin_resolve_fraud_signal`; `fn_admin_resolve_user_report` — "banned" deliberately reuses `is_suspended` rather than inventing a second account-status dimension nothing else in this schema has). Also went live with a real `OPENAI_API_KEY` (local `.env` + live Supabase secret) per the user's explicit go-ahead — confirmed valid against `/v1/models`, but the moderation endpoint itself 429s consistently on this account (likely a billing/access-tier setting, not fixable from here); user chose to proceed with the moderation queue UI regardless. 17 new DB-level assertions, all passing.

**Piece 2 (PR #137) — fraud signals queue UI.** `/dashboard/fraud-signals`: filterable by type/severity/resolved-status, cursor-paginated. Real data waiting: 178 unreviewed high-severity `ledger_mismatch` signals (two specific wallets, persistent since 9/13 — looks like historical test pollution, not an active bug, but genuinely never reviewed until this queue existed) plus 2 `shared_device_fingerprint`. Dismiss/escalate actions; a "Freeze user's wallets" action that loops the freeze function across all of a user's wallets in one click, since the queue's point is acting on the flagged _user_, not one wallet at a time. Also added a freeze/unfreeze toggle directly to Phase B's user detail page wallet cards.

**Piece 3 (PR #138) — user reports queue UI, completes Phase C.** `/dashboard/user-reports`: filterable by reason/resolved-status, cursor-paginated, links to both reporter and reported user. Warn/suspend/ban/dismiss actions. Scope trim stated plainly: no admin thread/message viewer exists anywhere in this app (deliberately, per `docs/14` §7.4's message-content-privacy posture), so a report's `thread_id` renders as plain text, not a link to a page that doesn't exist.

**A real, persistent browser-testing limitation surfaced starting piece 2, flagged honestly rather than worked around silently:** a browser extension in the Chrome profile used for `claude-in-chrome` testing injects `__text_mode_READY__` onto `<body>` on every fresh page load, which breaks React hydration badly enough that Server Action form submission (click, Enter-key, even a direct `form.requestSubmit()` call) crashes into a client exception — confirmed unconditional across fresh tabs and fresh navigations, re-checked before piece 3 and still present, not a retry-fixable flake, not something fixable from this environment. Pieces 2 and 3 fell back to verifying the actual data-logic layer directly instead: running the exact query compositions each page uses and the exact RPC sequences each Server Action makes, via throwaway scripts against the live dev DB with real fixtures, then cleaning them up. This is real but not complete verification — the literal click-through UI flow is unconfirmed for both pieces, unlike every earlier piece this session (Phase A and Phase B's first two pieces all got full interactive browser verification before this extension issue appeared). Worth a real click-through by the user themselves, or a fresh Chrome profile without whatever extension is doing this, before treating pieces 2/3 as fully proven.

**Phase C's exit criteria (`docs/14` §9) is met, modulo that verification caveat:** both previously-invisible queues have real UI and real (manual-only, no auto-action) actions.

**Before Phase D started, a real bug from Phase C was caught and fixed as its own small PR, per the user's explicit "fix it now, before Phase D" (PR #140):** `fn_is_withdrawal_trusted` (built session 14/15, predates Phase C's `resolved_at` column) never checked it — so dismissing a fraud signal through the queue Phase C piece 2 just built had zero effect on a payee's withdrawal-trust status; they stayed disqualified for the full 90-day lookback exactly as if the signal had never been reviewed, which defeats the point of building a review queue at all. Fixed via `create or replace function` adding `and (resolved_at is null or resolution = 'escalated')` to the disqualifying condition — unresolved and escalated signals still disqualify, dismissed ones no longer do. Confirmed directly (not assumed) that `create or replace` preserves the function's existing revoke/grant lock. New test (`testAutoSweepDismissedSignalUnblocksTrustButEscalatedDoesNot` in `supabase/tests/fraud-functions.test.js`) covers all three cases; full suite (35 assertions) passing.

**Phase D (pricing & fee control) is also fully built and merged this same session — two pieces, scope trimmed by explicit prior decision to drop the pricing-strategy-switch framework (§4.3) and the separate Service Pricing toggle table (§4.2's free-vs-charged UI) from this pass, leaving just the fee/rate control surface itself; both deferred, not abandoned, and not yet re-scoped:**

**Piece 1 (PR #141) — `pricing_config` real attribution + `fn_admin_update_pricing_config`.** Same correction already on record in `docs/14` §2/§4.2: `pricing_config_history` and its audit trigger already existed since the very first schema migration and already captured `old_value`/`new_value` correctly on every change — the actual, narrower gap was `changed_by` being hardcoded to the literal `'system'`, with no way for a caller to attribute a real admin. Fixed via a transaction-local Postgres setting (`set_config('app.admin_actor', ..., is_local => true)`), not session-scoped — this project's connections go through Supabase's pooler, and a session-scoped setting could leak into a later, unrelated query on the same pooled connection. New `fn_admin_update_pricing_config` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, permission-gated on `edit_pricing_config`, the grant lock explicit per CLAUDE.md rule #11 and verified via `has_function_privilege`) is the one sanctioned write path: sets the actor, updates the value, and adds two sanity guards the bare `bigint not null` column never enforced — no key can go negative, and a `_bps`-suffixed key can't exceed 10000 (100%), closing off a typo'd extra zero on a take-rate. 11 new DB-level assertions (`supabase/tests/admin-pricing-config-functions.test.js`), including one that specifically proves the transaction-local setting does _not_ leak to a later direct UPDATE on the same pooled connection.

**Piece 2 (PR #142) — pricing editor UI + change history view.** `/dashboard/pricing`: all 31 live `pricing_config` keys, grouped by rough category (message/status pricing, platform take rates & fees, credits & transfers, withdrawals, account & velocity limits, fraud & abuse detection, feature flags — a display-only grouping; any future key not yet categorized still renders, under "Uncategorized", rather than silently disappearing), edit-in-place per key with its description shown, writing through piece 1's function. Display hints (percentage for `_bps` keys, ₦ for `_kobo` keys, Enabled/Disabled for the one flag key) are purely presentational — the write path always submits the same raw integer the column stores, never a client-side conversion, per CLAUDE.md rules #1/#2. `/dashboard/pricing/history`: filterable by key, cursor-paginated, same seek-method pattern as every other paginated list in this app — required generalizing `lib/pagination.ts`'s `cursorFilter` to accept a column name, since `pricing_config_history`'s timestamp column is `changed_at`, not `created_at` like every table this helper was written against so far. Both routes gated on `edit_pricing_config` for viewing and editing alike — no separate view permission exists in `admin_permissions` (checked directly), so this matches who can actually act on it.

**Same browser-verification caveat as Phase C pieces 2-3, checked again rather than assumed still-present:** the `__text_mode_READY__` extension hydration issue is still there today — confirmed via `document.body.className` immediately before piece 2's PR, on a fresh tab. It blocks the login form itself now, not just a page's own Server Action forms, so no page past `/login` could be reached by browser automation at all this piece. Fell back to the same verification used for Phase C: ran the exact Supabase queries both new pages issue, and the exact `db().rpc('fn_admin_update_pricing_config', …)` call path the edit form's Server Action uses end-to-end, against the live dev DB with a disposable QA admin (`finance_admin` role) and a disposable `pricing_config` row — value updated correctly, `pricing_config_history.changed_by` correctly attributed to the QA admin's real email, everything cleaned up afterward (`admin_users` confirmed back to exactly the 3 real accounts). `next build` also confirmed both new routes compile and prerender. Worth a real click-through by the user, or a fresh Chrome profile without whatever extension is doing this, before treating this piece as fully proven the way Phase A/early-Phase-B pieces were.

**Phase D's exit criteria (`docs/14` §4.2) is met, modulo that same verification caveat:** every pricing/fee `pricing_config` key has a real UI, with real per-admin attribution on every change, replacing what used to be Studio-only hand-edits attributed to nobody.

**Next session: start with Phase E (dual-approval workflow, `docs/14` §4.4/§9).** Phases A through D are fully built, merged, and verified (modulo the standing browser-extension caveat on Phase C pieces 2-3 and Phase D piece 2 — re-check `document.body.className` for `__text_mode_READY__` before assuming click-through testing is possible again). Covers: a full RBAC schema (`admin_users`/`admin_roles`/`admin_permissions`, 6 seeded roles — `super_admin`, `finance_admin`, `compliance_officer`, `support_agent`, `content_moderator`, `read_only_auditor`), pricing/fee controls over the existing `pricing_config` (plus building the `pricing_config_history` audit table `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` already describes but which was never actually built — checked directly, confirmed missing), a "pricing strategy" framework letting a super admin switch between pre-built, pre-tested billing formulas without an app release (explicitly **not** a way to author new billing logic from a text box — that stays an engineering task, stated plainly to avoid overpromising), a real dual-approval workflow enforcing in code the "second admin sign-off" rule that `docs/02` already asserts but nothing currently enforces, and the fraud/reports review queues (`fraud_signals`/`user_reports`) getting their first UI ever — today both are Studio-only with zero client `SELECT` grant by design. **The one genuinely new capability found and designed, not previously known about:** there is no mechanism anywhere in this codebase for InvolveMe to withdraw its own accumulated platform revenue (`platform_revenue_topup_fees`/`platform_revenue_earnings_cut`) to a real company bank account — `fn_initiate_withdrawal` is scoped entirely to user wallets. §5 designs `fn_initiate_platform_withdrawal` for this, reusing the same `packages/payments` `payout()` interface `withdraw` already calls, gated behind the dual-approval workflow with no materiality floor (every platform withdrawal needs two admins, no exceptions). 7-phase build plan (A: identity/RBAC → B: read-only visibility → C: reports/fraud queue → D: pricing controls → E: dual-approval → F: treasury withdrawal → G: analytics polish), ordered so nothing powerful ships before it's auditable.

**`docs/15-MARKETING-SITE-PWA-SCOPING.md`** — a new `apps/marketing/` (Next.js/Astro, SSG for real SEO — not the Expo stack, no benefit to reusing it for a content-driven public site), Netlify-hosted, separate from a second Netlify site (`app.involveme.com`) serving the PWA. **The load-bearing finding that reframes the whole PWA ask:** `docs/12-LINKED-DEVICES-WEB-SCOPING.md` already confirmed, live, that Expo Router's web export works today — so "mirror the full app as a continuously-updated PWA" is not a second product to build and maintain in parallel, it's packaging (manifest + service worker + Netlify CI auto-deploy) over a build that already exists; every mobile feature is already in the PWA the moment it merges, because it's the same source tree. Homepage OTP signup reuses the existing phone-OTP Edge Functions directly (no new auth logic), landing on a download page rather than a full web login — with one new risk flagged as required-not-optional: a public, unauthenticated website is a much more attractive OTP-abuse target than an app-store-gated mobile client, so a bot/CAPTCHA guard ships in the same phase as the signup form, never after. The "scan QR to use on PC" ask is identified as literally `docs/12`'s own pairing flow, just surfaced on this site — no new pairing/session design happens in this doc, and two of its six build phases are explicitly marked as blocked on `docs/12` being built first, not silently deferred. Also recommends promoting the mobile app's existing ToS/Privacy content into a shared `packages/legal-content/` so the two surfaces can't drift, rather than a second hand-maintained copy.

**Both docs cross-referenced into the existing structure, not left standalone:** `docs/08-BUILD-PHASES-ROADMAP.md`'s deferred-items list now points to both (and its stale "no admin tooling exists or is planned" line — written session 15 — is corrected to reflect that this is now a scoped, phased item, still not built), and `docs/09-PROJECT-STRUCTURE.md` gained placeholder entries for `apps/admin/`, `apps/marketing/`, and `packages/legal-content/`, each marked "PLANNED, not built."

User confirmed session 24's 5-item punch list (items 1-4) tested working on a real device, then came back with three follow-up fixes to items already shipped: the status-reply keyboard, contact-name resolution not reaching the chat header/profile screen, and group creation's "none of your contacts are on InvolveMe" bug plus an explicit ask for a search-and-add-by-phone feature. One PR (#101), merged, CI green, shipped OTA to the `preview` channel — no `eas build` triggered. User has since confirmed this round tested working too; session closing here.

**Status-reply keyboard, missed by session 24's own reply-bar addition:** `StoryViewer`'s reply/like bar sits over full-bleed status media as `position: absolute, bottom: 0` — not in normal flex flow, so it never benefited from `KeyboardAvoidingScreen`'s flex-flow spacer mechanism (which only pushes content that's actually part of the flex column above it). Fixed by exporting `KeyboardAvoidingScreen`'s `useKeyboardHeight` hook and applying the real measured keyboard height directly to the reply bar's own `bottom` offset — same underlying measurement as the composer's own fix, different application for an absolutely-positioned overlay.

**Device-contact-name resolution, previously chat-list-only, now shared everywhere a person's name is shown:** session 24's item 1 built this rule (device-saved contact name > phone number > never the partner's own self-chosen `display_name`) only inside `chats.tsx`. Extracted into a new shared hook, `usePhoneContactNames()` in `lib/contacts.ts` (syncs device contacts once, re-syncs on `useContactsChangedStore` bumps, exposes a `resolveContactName` function) — `chats.tsx` now consumes this shared hook instead of its own inline duplicate, and it's newly wired into `thread/[id].tsx`'s open-chat header (title, header avatar, header text) and `profile/[id].tsx`'s profile name/avatar. All three surfaces can no longer drift apart on what name they show for the same person.

**Group creation's real "none of my contacts are on InvolveMe" bug, root-caused rather than guessed at — two distinct causes found:** (1) `toE164NigerianPhone` trusted any `+`-prefixed number as already-correct E.164, so a genuinely common phonebook mistake — typing a number in local trunk format (`0802...`) then later prefixing the dial code without deleting the leading `0` (`+2340802...`) — silently never matched the same person's correctly-stored `users.phone`; fixed by having the Nigeria-specific wrapper strip that redundant post-dial-code zero (scoped there only, not the general `toE164Phone` the signup country-picker flow uses for arbitrary countries, to avoid any risk of a similar assumption being wrong for a different country's numbering plan). (2) `NewGroupModal`'s `findUsers` mutation had no error handling at all — a transient failure rendered pixel-identical to "genuinely no matches," which is exactly the failure shape the user described; added a real error message plus a "Retry" action (a bumped `retryKey` re-triggers the same sync-and-match effect, same shape `thread/[id].tsx`'s `headerRefetchKey` already uses). **Verified against the real deployed `find-users-by-phones` function, not just reasoned about** — a throwaway script confirmed the exact reported scenario (a device contact saved in local format) failed to match before the fix and matched correctly after, run against the live Supabase project, not a local mock.

**Also explicitly requested and built in the same pass:** a "search and add by phone number" flow inside `NewGroupModal`, reusing the same single-lookup `find-user-by-phone` path `NewChatModal` already uses elsewhere in this file — types a number, taps "Add", and the found InvolveMe user is added directly to the group's member selection. Always available regardless of device-contacts sync state (denied permission, empty phonebook, or a `find-users-by-phones` failure no longer fully blocks group creation).

**Next up:** nothing outstanding from this session — all three fixes confirmed working by the user on a real device. Two scoped-but-unbuilt features remain on hold from prior sessions, awaiting the user's own go-ahead to pick either up: `docs/11-VOICE-VIDEO-CALLS-SCOPING.md` (LiveKit calls) and `docs/12-LINKED-DEVICES-WEB-SCOPING.md` (linked devices/web client).

Same discipline as every prior punch-list session: organized into an ordered plan (smallest/most-blocking first, the billing-sensitive item last), worked one item at a time, real root-cause investigation before each fix rather than reapplying the same guess that had already failed twice. Four PRs shipped, merged, CI green, four separate OTA publishes to the `preview` channel — no `eas build` triggered.

**Item 1 — composer still hidden behind the keyboard, the real bug this time (PR #90, merged, shipped OTA):** two earlier sessions (19, 20) believed this was fixed via `KeyboardAvoidingScreen`'s wrapper — the wrapper _was_ correctly in place, but on iOS its `padding` behavior computes the gap to the keyboard by comparing its own `onLayout`-relative frame against the keyboard's _absolute_ screen position; when a native stack header renders above it (every `headerShown: true` screen, not a `Modal`), those two coordinate spaces disagree by roughly the header's height unless `keyboardVerticalOffset` explicitly accounts for it. Fixed once, centrally, in `KeyboardAvoidingScreen` — reads the real header height from `HeaderHeightContext`, deliberately imported from expo-router's own vendored `react-navigation/elements` rather than a freshly-installed `@react-navigation/elements` package (which would be a second, disconnected copy of the same-named Context that would silently never see the real value — confirmed no top-level `@react-navigation/*` dependency exists anywhere in this repo before writing this). Fixes `thread/[id].tsx`, `group-thread/[id].tsx`, and three Settings screens in one place. **Still not confirmed on a real device in this sandbox** — the fix follows well-documented RN behavior, not a device-verified measurement; genuinely worth a real check before assuming it's fully resolved this time.

**Item 4 — chat list requiring a manual refresh, done alongside item 1 in the same PR (#90) since both were "core UX plumbing":** real root cause, confirmed live against the actual database rather than assumed: `messages`/`wallets` were already in the `supabase_realtime` publication (session 13), but `threads` never was, and `useThreads()` had no Realtime subscription at all. New migration adds `threads` to the publication; `useThreads()` and the tab-bar unread badge (`useTotalUnreadCount()`) both now subscribe to it, and the badge's old 15s poll was removed now that a live subscription exists. Verified end-to-end against the real deployed project: reproduced that an unauthenticated-for-realtime subscription doesn't receive events, then confirmed a properly authenticated one does, using the exact subscription shape `useThreads()` now uses.

**Item 3 — emoji picker (PR #91, merged, shipped OTA):** user explicitly chose emoji now, stickers scoped separately later — real custom stickers need a whole new Storage/moderation pipeline, disproportionate to this punch list. New `components/chat/EmojiPicker.tsx`: curated Unicode emoji (`lib/emojiData.ts`, ~9 categories, no image assets, no new dependency), category tabs, a "Recently used" category (AsyncStorage-backed), a backspace key, rendered in-line at a fixed height swapped in for the real keyboard — not a floating `Modal`. Wired into both `thread/[id].tsx` and `group-thread/[id].tsx` with cursor-position-aware insertion/backspace (tracked via `onSelectionChange`), not always-append.

**Item 2 — message editing, done last as the biggest and most billing-sensitive item (PR #92, merged, shipped OTA):** read `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md`/`docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` before writing any code, per CLAUDE.md's standing instruction — editing sits directly on top of the escrow lifecycle, and the two obvious naive implementations (a raw client `UPDATE`; "recompute the formula and charge the difference") both reopen exploits this project already closed once. New `fn_edit_message`: only the original sender, only while `status = 'escrowed'` (once `released`/`refunded` the transaction is settled and content can't change), only within `message_edit_window_minutes` (new config, default 15 — WhatsApp's own real window), and the edited word count's required credits must never exceed what was **already charged** (compared directly against the stored `credits_charged`, immune to a `pricing_config` change landing between send and edit) — shrinking or same-tier edits are free, crossing into a more expensive tier is rejected outright, never silently truncated. Row-locked against the real concurrent writer this status check has to race against: `fn_release_escrow` (and the expiry-sweep cron behind it) also writes `messages.status` from a completely different code path. Verified four layers deep before trusting it: 9/9 direct-SQL checks against every rejection case, 10/10 through the local Edge Function, then confirmed a fourth time against the actual **deployed** function — per this repo's own recorded deployed-function-testing-gap lesson. Mobile: long-press an own editable message reveals an Edit action, editing pre-fills the composer with an "Editing message" banner, and a successful edit shows a small "Edited" label via the new `messages.edited_at` column. Group-message editing deliberately out of scope — group messages are free (no escrow, no billing concept), so the core "cannot be charged twice" requirement doesn't apply there the same way.

**Item 1 addendum — the fix above still didn't work; real fix landed same day (PR #94, merged, shipped OTA):** user tested on a real device and reported the composer still completely fixed/hidden, not just misaligned — and gave the actual diagnostic clue himself: this session's own emoji picker panel (a plain `View`, not the keyboard) correctly pushes the composer up when opened, via ordinary flex layout, while the real keyboard did nothing at all under the same layout shape. That asymmetry means `KeyboardAvoidingView` was never successfully reacting to the keyboard opening on this device in the first place (most likely Android's edge-to-edge display mode, on by default in recent Expo/RN versions, silently breaking `windowSoftInputMode="adjustResize"`) — the `keyboardVerticalOffset` fix above was correcting an offset on a mechanism that wasn't firing at all, which is why it didn't help. Real fix, per the user's own explicit request to "mirror the model that made the emoji feature work": `KeyboardAvoidingScreen` no longer uses `KeyboardAvoidingView` at all — it measures the keyboard's real height directly from `Keyboard`'s show/hide events and renders a plain spacer `View` of that height, the exact same mechanism the emoji picker already used successfully. `isModal`/`keyboardVerticalOffset` (props specific to the old, now-removed `KeyboardAvoidingView` approach) are gone from the component and all 4 call sites that passed them.

**Next up:** nothing from this session's original 4-item list is open, including item 1 as of the addendum above. This is the third attempt at the keyboard bug across three sessions (19, 20, this one) — the first two grounded in code review, this one grounded in the user's own live diagnostic observation, which is a materially stronger basis, but it is **still not confirmed on a real device in this sandbox** (none available). If it resurfaces a fourth time, don't reach for another `KeyboardAvoidingView` variant — the working model now is direct `Keyboard` event measurement, proven by the emoji picker's own already-shipped behavior. ## Where we stopped (end of session 22, 2026-09-19) — start here next session

User came back with a 4-item real-world punch list after reviewing session 21's LiveKit scoping doc (still on hold, awaiting the user's own go-ahead — nothing built there this session). Same discipline as prior punch-list sessions: organized into an ordered plan (smallest/most-blocking fixes first, biggest rebuild last), worked one item at a time. Four PRs shipped, merged, CI green, four separate OTA publishes to the `preview` channel — no `eas build` triggered.

**Item 2 — upload mime-type bug, done first since it was an active blocker (PR #87, merged, shipped OTA):** profile photo and status photo uploads were failing outright with a mime-type-rejected error. Root cause, confirmed live against the real deployed project rather than guessed: `@supabase/storage-js`'s `uploadToSignedUrl` only reads its `fileOptions.contentType` argument on its raw-body code path — for a `Blob` body (this app's exact case, from `fetch(localUri).blob()`) it takes a different internal branch (FormData) that never looks at `fileOptions` at all and relies entirely on the Blob's own `.type`, which RN's `fetch(file://...).blob()` leaves empty/wrong. First fix attempt (passing `fileOptions.contentType`) was verified live to be a **no-op** — caught before shipping, not after. Real fix: re-wrap as `new Blob([original], { type: 'image/jpeg' })`. A second layer of care here: the obvious alternative fix, `new Blob([arrayBuffer], { type })`, would have shipped a bug that only breaks on a real device — confirmed by reading React Native's own `Blob` polyfill source (`BlobManager.createFromParts`) that it explicitly throws on `ArrayBuffer`/`ArrayBufferView` parts, something a Node-only verification would never have caught. Both `lib/queries/profileMedia.ts` and `lib/queries/status.ts` fixed identically.

**Item 3 — Contacts search + alphabetical order (PR #87, same PR as item 2):** the Contacts sub-tab (`chats.tsx`'s `ContactsList`) had neither — contacts rendered in raw device order with no filter, unlike the sibling `chats` sub-tab which already had both. Added a search box (same shape/placement) and `localeCompare`-based alphabetical sort within both the "On InvolveMe" and "Invite" sections.

**Item 4 — WhatsApp-scale font bump (PR #87, same PR):** bumped the typography token scale (`body` 16→17, `bodyMedium` 16/500→17/600, `title` 20/600→22/700, `caption` 13→14) after an explicit "too tiny, thicken/bolden the chat-row list" report. Every consumer already reads these through `useTheme()` tokens, so this one token change fixed chat-row contact names, Settings row labels, and profile-screen text app-wide with no per-screen overrides. Also fixed several pre-existing hardcoded `fontSize: 16` literals (message/group composers, PIN inputs, profile link inputs, search bars) that had drifted out of sync with the token scale — a real, separate class of bug from the token value itself, worth having caught in the same pass. `docs/04-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`'s typography table updated to match, including a pre-existing stale line unrelated to this session's own change (the doc said `tabBarLabel` was 12/700; the code already said 14/700 since session 13).

**Item 1 — Status tab full WhatsApp-Updates-parity rebuild, done last as the biggest item (PR #88, merged, shipped OTA):** replaced the old horizontal ring row + plain vertical "my statuses" list (with a trash icon per row, which the user called "a manual boring list") with: a horizontal preview-card carousel sized like WhatsApp's own Updates screen (104×168 cards) showing **real content** — photo thumbnails or the poster's text-status background/caption, not just avatar rings — with a small ring avatar overlaid top-left and the poster's name on a bottom scrim strip; a first carousel slot that's the owner's own entry (empty circle + "+" badge + "Post Status" label when nothing's active, taps straight to the composer per the explicit spec; their own preview card with an extra "+" badge to add another once they do have one); a "Viewed updates" vertical list below the carousel for already-seen posters (standard contact-row shape, not a second carousel); and a single floating camera+pen compose button replacing the old full-width "New status update" button. Also fixed `StatusComposer`'s "Write a text status" option, which was a small underlined text link visibly smaller than "Take Photo"/"Choose from Gallery" — now the same big-option button size/shape, matching the user's explicit ask. `StoryViewer` (full-screen tap-to-view, 6s auto-advance with a top progress bar, swipe-between-posters) was already built correctly in an earlier session (Batch F, session 18) and needed no rework — left untouched except a small header-parity addition (poster avatar + relative time next to the name). Reused the existing frozen-snapshot pattern (`useStatusFeed`'s own documented live-reorder hazard) for all three sub-feeds — unseen carousel, viewed list, and the synthetic own-status feed — not just the one sub-feed the original code protected.

**A real gap, disclosed rather than skipped over: none of this session's UI/layout work has been visually confirmed live.** Every item passed `tsc --noEmit`/`expo lint`/`npx expo export --platform web` clean, and item 2's fix was verified end-to-end against the real deployed project (reproduced the failure, then proved the fix), but the actual on-screen layout — carousel card sizing/spacing, ring rendering, FAB position, the new font scale's real-world legibility — was never seen rendered, only reasoned through statically. **A real, reusable environment finding from shipping that OTA:** `eas update --branch preview --environment preview --non-interactive` silently hung/no-op'd twice during its "Computing project fingerprints" step — exit code 0, no "Published!" line, no new update group actually created (confirmed via `eas update:list`, not trusted from the exit code alone). Fixed by passing `EAS_SKIP_AUTO_FINGERPRINT=1` (the CLI's own hint text), after which publish completed normally in under a minute. Recorded in memory (`eas-update-fingerprint-hang`) — **always verify a real `eas update` publish landed via `update:list`, never trust a bare exit-0.**

**Calls provider decided: LiveKit (docs/11 updated, nothing built).** User chose LiveKit explicitly — start on LiveKit Cloud's free tier, with an explicit intent to migrate to self-hosted LiveKit OSS later. Real, live-verified facts (not assumed from training data, same discipline session 17's Flutterwave check used) folded into `docs/11-VOICE-VIDEO-CALLS-SCOPING.md`: free tier is 5,000 WebRTC participant-minutes/month with a hard stop (no surprise billing) and no card required; self-hosted LiveKit OSS is genuinely Apache-2.0 with no Redis/TURN needed for a single node; Cloud and self-hosted run the identical SDK/token format, so the eventual migration is a config swap, not a rewrite — this compatibility is the actual reason LiveKit was chosen over the other managed options in the doc's original comparison table. Real corrections to the doc's own original assumptions, caught by this research rather than left standing: LiveKit doesn't actually price video higher than voice (one shared per-minute meter for both — the original doc had assumed otherwise); the RN SDK needs a custom `expo-dev-client` build (not Expo-Go-compatible), so calls joins the already-held `eas build` queue rather than shipping as an OTA-only feature; and Scale-tier-only data residency is a new, real compliance question for `docs/07`'s NDPA section that didn't exist before real-time media was in scope. `docs/11` §8 (new) lays out a concrete first-milestone plan (prove the LiveKit round-trip in isolation → real cost-model pass against actual pricing → migration → gated mobile build → CallKit/history UI), explicitly so a future session doesn't have to re-derive where to start.

**Real LiveKit Cloud free-tier credentials now exist and are stored — a live-infrastructure change, not just docs.** The user pasted `LIVEKIT_URL`/`LIVEKIT_API_KEY`/`LIVEKIT_API_SECRET` directly in chat; per explicit confirmation, they were immediately moved into Supabase Edge Function secrets (same mechanism as `FLW_CLIENT_SECRET`/`TWO_STEP_PIN_PEPPER`) rather than left sitting in conversation history, and confirmed present via `supabase secrets list`. User explicitly declined rotating the key/secret pair afterward (comfortable leaving it as-is on a no-traffic free-tier dev account) — worth reconsidering if this project moves toward real usage before the self-host migration. **No Edge Function reads these yet** — nothing in `supabase/functions/` references `LIVEKIT_*` — storing the credential was prep, not integration.
