## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 14, continued again — settlement-aware auto-withdrawal holds)

Resumed after the emergency grants-lockdown PR (#44) merged — that fix was discovered and shipped mid-flight while starting this exact task, so it landed first. This is the original task: docs/06 §3's other, harder bullet, picked from a "what's naturally next" menu presented per explicit request.

The doc's literal wording ("if its originating topup is very recent and the payer account is low-trust... tie the 24h promise to the payee's trust tier") reads at first like it wants per-fund lineage tracing — but `withdrawable_cash` is a pooled, fungible balance with no per-source tracking (`fn_run_auto_withdraw_sweep`'s own header comment already documents this as a deliberate "v1 proxy, not hidden"), and the bullet's own next sentence clarifies the actual intended fix is the same mechanism as §4's table. That table already described a Tier-1-vs-"Tier 2 (30 days clean history)" distinction — confirmed by reading the real code, not assumed, that `kyc_tier = 2` is never assigned anywhere in this app (grepped) — no liveness-KYC vendor integration exists, so waiting for a real third tier isn't buildable. The actual fix doesn't need one: both halves of "Tier 2" (account age, clean fraud-signal history) already exist as data.

**Built:** `fn_is_withdrawal_trusted(user_id)` computes that distinction — `kyc_tier >= 1`, account older than `new_account_age_days` (reused from the existing topup-velocity key, not a new duplicate), and no `severity = 'high'` `fraud_signals` row within a new `withdrawal_trust_signal_lookback_days` (90, config — a rolling window, not a lifetime ban, since there's no admin UI to mark a signal cleared). `fn_run_auto_withdraw_sweep` redefined to use it: trusted payees keep today's 24h behavior; untrusted payees wait a longer `withdrawal_auto_sweep_hours_untrusted` (72h, config) and are never force-swept below the minimum while a disqualifying signal is active, however long the money has sat — held, not force-paid, same posture the existing unverified-bank-account exclusion already uses. Scope matches §4's table exactly: only auto-sweep timing is gated, manual withdrawal is untouched. A disqualifying signal never sets `is_frozen` — consistent with the standing "fraud signals are for manual review, never auto-freeze" decision from an earlier session. Its own `SECURITY DEFINER` grant uses the now-mandatory explicit `revoke ... from public, anon, authenticated` / `grant ... to service_role` pattern (CLAUDE.md rule #11) from the moment it was created.

**Manually verified against the dev DB before trusting the SQL:** five synthetic scenarios (trusted-sweeps-at-24h, untrusted-holds-until-72h, untrusted-sweeps-once-aged-past-72h, flagged-held-indefinitely-past-force-sweep-and-below-minimum, signal-aged-out-of-lookback-sweeps-normally) plus a manual-withdrawal-unaffected check — all matched the design exactly on the first real run.

**Tests:** 5 new cases in `fraud-functions.test.js` (31/31 total in the file now) covering the same five scenarios plus the manual-withdrawal-unaffected check. Full regression re-run, each suite alone per the concurrency-contention finding from the previous PR.

**A git-hygiene mistake worth recording:** mid-session, this branch's uncommitted work was stashed to isolate the emergency security fix onto a clean branch, then later reset via `git checkout --` instead of re-stashing when splitting the security-fix commit from this one — discarding the doc/test edits (not the migration file, which had been separately backed up). Recovered by re-applying the same edits from this conversation's own context rather than redoing the design work, but the lesson stands: `git stash` to shelve, never `git checkout --` to discard, when the goal is "set this aside, not delete it."

**Docs:** `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §3 now fully closed (every buildable bullet built, 3DS N/A) and §4's table updated from aspiration to enforced behavior with the mechanism explained; §12 checklist lines for both corrected. `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` §6 gets the trust-gate note. `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` new §6.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 14, continued — CRITICAL: every SECURITY DEFINER function was directly callable by any client, bypassing every Edge Function's auth layer)

Found mid-flight while starting the next task (settlement-aware withdrawal holds, still paused, not in this PR) — checking that a freshly-added function's execute grant had actually locked down led to checking an _existing_ one, `fn_initiate_withdrawal`, which should already have been safe per Phase 1's own `revoke execute ... from public` statement. It wasn't: `has_function_privilege('anon', ..., 'execute')` returned `true`.

**Root cause, confirmed live via direct queries, not assumed:** every `revoke execute on function ... from public` statement across this codebase's entire migration history — starting with the very first one — has been a no-op. This Supabase project's own default-ACL setup grants EXECUTE to `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` **directly** on every new public-schema function at creation time, a separate grant path from the `PUBLIC` pseudo-role; revoking from `PUBLIC` never touches it. Confirmed by inspecting `pg_default_acl` directly. Tables were never exposed this way — `20260912070729_create_core_schema.sql`'s own header comment already documented and mitigated the equivalent table-level default via RLS-enabled-with-zero-policies — but there is no RLS equivalent for functions, so this gap was specific to them and had no parallel mitigation.

**Practical impact:** any client holding the project's public anon key (embedded in the mobile app, not secret) could call `fn_transfer_credit`, `fn_send_message`, `fn_initiate_withdrawal`, `fn_buy_credit` — every money-moving function — directly via PostgREST RPC, passing _any_ `p_sender_id`/`p_user_id`/`p_recipient_id`. The "you can only ever act as yourself" check lives only in the Edge Function layer (pulled from a verified JWT) and was entirely bypassable by calling the database function directly. Concretely: `fn_transfer_credit(victim_id, attacker_id, victim_balance)` would drain any user's credit with no auth beyond the anon key.

**Fixed:** `20260915161500_lock_security_definer_execute_grants.sql` explicitly revokes EXECUTE from `public, anon, authenticated` and grants to `service_role` only, on all 21 real `SECURITY DEFINER` business functions this codebase defines (trigger functions and the `pg_trgm` extension's own functions excluded — not applicable/not ours). Verified live, function by function, after applying: all 21 now show `anon=false, authenticated=false, service_role=true` via `has_function_privilege`.

**A second attempt that looked right but wasn't, also confirmed by direct testing rather than trusted on faith:** also tried `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES ... REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC` so future migrations couldn't reopen this gap. Tested it live by creating a throwaway function afterward — it still came back with `PUBLIC` execute granted (`{=X/postgres,...}` in its `proacl`), despite `pg_default_acl` showing no `PUBLIC` entry for the relevant role/schema/objtype. Root cause not fully pinned down in reasonable time (a Supabase-platform-level mechanism this project doesn't have visibility into is the leading suspect) — kept in the migration as harmless defense-in-depth, but **not relied upon**. The only verified-reliable fix, confirmed the same way: an explicit `revoke ... from public, anon, authenticated` + `grant ... to service_role` immediately after every single `create function` for a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, every time, no exceptions. **Now a documented standing rule — CLAUDE.md rule #11.**

**Tested:** this is a grants-only change, no logic touched — full regression re-run to confirm nothing broke (Edge Functions run as `service_role`, which keeps EXECUTE throughout): `test:functions` 133/133 (across all 14 files), `test:db` 22/22, `test:fraud` 31/31, `test:group-chat` 19/19, `test:chargeback` 24/24, each run alone per the concurrency-contention finding from the previous PR. One transient `test:functions` flake (a real Flutterwave network hiccup, not reproduced on immediate re-run) — not a regression.

**Shipped ahead of the settlement-aware-holds work already in progress** (stashed, paused) specifically so no other work merges on top of an unpatched database — this was a live security hole in a money-handling app, treated with that priority rather than folded quietly into an unrelated feature PR.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 14 — reserve buffer + clawback-as-debt for chargebacks)

Picked per explicit standing instruction from the previous session ("when done and merged, start 2 — reserve buffer/clawback-as-debt for chargebacks") once the app-store-blockers PRs (#41 ToS/Privacy/age-gate, #42 content moderation) both merged clean. Closes `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §3's last two open bullets — 9 of 11 items in that doc's §12 checklist are now checked.

Investigated directly (not assumed) before planning: no DB-level non-negative constraint exists on `wallets.balance` (confirmed live via `pg_constraint` against the dev DB) — a debt is representable with zero schema change. `platform_revenue_topup_fees`/`platform_revenue_earnings_cut` are credited from exactly four sites (`fn_confirm_topup`, `fn_release_escrow`, `fn_transfer_credit`, the kill-switched `fn_send_group_message`). No Flutterwave dispute/chargeback webhook event has ever been confirmed against a real payload — the handler only ever confirmed `charge.completed`/`transfer.completed` — and this app has no card-collection path anyway (NGN bank-transfer/virtual-account only), so §3's "3DS-only card collections" bullet is moot as written; wiring a guessed event shape was rejected on the same "verify live, don't guess" discipline that cost two days on the original webhook incident.

**Built (migrations `20260915150000`/`20260915151500`):**

1. **Reserve buffer** — `fn_credit_platform_revenue(p_revenue_kind, p_reserve_kind, p_amount, p_reason, p_ref_type, p_ref_id)` is the one place platform revenue splits: `platform_reserve_bps` (config, 400 = 4%) goes to a new reserve wallet, the remainder to the existing revenue wallet, net+reserve always summing to the original amount (subtraction, not two independent roundings). Two new platform-owned wallet kinds mirroring the existing kobo/credits split exactly: `platform_reserve_topup_fees`, `platform_reserve_earnings_cut`. `fn_confirm_topup`, `fn_release_escrow`, `fn_transfer_credit`, and `fn_send_group_message` (kept in sync for consistency even though it's still kill-switched off) were all redefined to call the helper instead of inserting into the revenue wallet directly.
2. **Clawback-as-debt** — `fn_process_chargeback(p_topup_id, p_reason default 'chargeback')`: given a `completed` topup, debits the payer's `topup_credit` wallet by the full `credits_issued` (negative balance **is** the tracked debt), reverses the platform's `platform_fee_kobo` for that topup, marks the topup `'reversed'` (new status value), freezes the payer's `topup_credit` wallet (`is_frozen`, already checked by every spend path — `fn_send_message`, `fn_post_status`, `fn_transfer_credit`, `fn_initiate_withdrawal`), and logs a high-severity `fraud_signals` row. Idempotent (replay on an already-reversed topup is a no-op) and safe under concurrency (row lock + status check serializes two racing calls). The payee is never touched — they keep what they earned in good faith unless collusion is separately flagged by the existing §2 detection.

Invocation is a direct RPC call by support/ops, the same precedent the `manual_adjustment` ledger reason already establishes — no admin UI exists in this app, so there's nothing to build there yet.

**Manually verified against the dev DB before trusting the SQL** (same discipline that caught the collusion job's `min(uuid)` bug and the duplicate-content payer-leak bug in earlier sessions): funded a real user, confirmed a real topup, spent the credits on a real message, charged it back, and confirmed by hand — negative balance, frozen wallet, blocked further send, untouched payee, correct fee reversal, idempotent replay, correct errors on an unknown/pending topup.

**A real test-design mistake caught before trusting the formal suite, not a bug in the SQL:** the first version of the concurrency test asserted the post-chargeback balance would be negative, but that scenario never spent the topup's credits first — the wallet correctly nets to exactly 0 (294 credited, 294 clawed back), not negative. Fixed by computing the expected balance relative to the balance actually observed before the chargeback, rather than assuming a negative result. Separately, a single message's platform cut is small enough that 4% of it can legitimately round to 0 — the escrow-release reserve-skim test was rewritten to assert the conservation identity (net + reserve == the actual cut) instead of a hardcoded nonzero expectation, and a new credit-transfer-based test was added (larger per-call amounts) to demonstrate a genuinely nonzero skim.

**Existing tests updated for the reserve split, not just the new file:** `wallet-functions.test.js`, `send-message-function.test.js`, `transfer-credit-function.test.js`, `webhook-flutterwave-function.test.js`, and `group-chat-functions.test.js` all had `resetPlatformWallets`-style cleanup helpers that only cleared the two revenue wallets — extended to also clear the two new reserve wallets, since they're shared singletons on the same dev DB and would otherwise leak balance across test runs (the same cross-test-pollution class flagged in the previous session's handoff entry). `send-message-function.test.js`'s one real exact-equality assertion against `platform_revenue_earnings_cut` (expecting the _full_ cut) was updated to sum revenue+reserve — the conservation property under test didn't change, only which wallet holds which slice.

**New:** `supabase/tests/chargeback-functions.test.js` (24/24) — reserve-skim conservation on real `fn_confirm_topup`/`fn_release_escrow`/`fn_transfer_credit` calls, the full debt/freeze/reversal/signal scenario, idempotency, concurrency, and both error paths. Wired into `package.json` (`test:chargeback`/`test:chargeback:ci`) and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new step mirroring `test:fraud:ci`'s).

**Tests:** `test:chargeback` 24/24. Full regression (`test:functions`, `test:db`, `test:fraud`, `test:group-chat`) re-run before commit — see below for final counts.

**Docs:** `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §3/§12 updated (checklist item now checked, §3's own text rewritten to describe what actually shipped); `docs/08-BUILD-PHASES-ROADMAP.md`'s exit-criteria count corrected (7→9 of 11, and found the "one partially, three genuinely open" framing was itself stale — §6 had already closed in the prior session but the summary line was never updated); `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` new §5 plus corrected the wallets/ledger-entries/topups tables (several other kinds/reasons had also drifted out of date before this pass, not just the ones this session added — `credit_transfer_*`/`group_message_*` reasons and the `platform_revenue_*` kind split were already live but undocumented); `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` new §11.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 13, continued — real ToS/Privacy Policy, age gate, content moderation: the app-store submission blockers)

Same session, continued after §6's fraud infra merged. Scoped explicitly: content moderation, ToS/Privacy Policy, and an age gate — all three previously "not built," confirmed by direct investigation (the Settings "Terms & Privacy" row was a plain `Alert.alert` saying "not yet published"; zero moderation code anywhere; signup was phone → OTP with no age/consent step of any kind, no column recording it).

**Two real facts needed that don't exist anywhere in this codebase, provided directly:** legal entity **Sun Media Limited, a company registered in Nigeria**; support contact **support@sunmedialimited.com.ng**. Both are in the drafted documents.

**Per explicit instruction, the ToS/Privacy Policy are drafted in full, real detail** — `apps/mobile/content/legal/terms.ts` (17 sections) and `privacy.ts` (13 sections), grounded in how comparable messaging/fintech apps structure these documents plus every feature that actually makes InvolveMe different: pay-per-message escrow billing, KYC-tiered withdrawal, peer-to-peer transfer with its new (session 13, earlier) KYC gate, device-fingerprinting/collusion detection, content moderation, and an explicit no-end-to-end-encryption disclosure per `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §5's own standing instruction — not boilerplate with the product name swapped in. Deliberately does **not** hardcode specific fee percentages (`pricing_config` values are tunable without a deploy per CLAUDE.md rule #9 — a static legal document hardcoding today's numbers would silently go stale the next time ops tunes them); states fees are disclosed in-app at the time of the action instead. One clause flagged inline in the document itself as the least-settled: §15 (Governing Law) — the same "not legal advice, get real counsel" posture `docs/07` already holds.

Rendered with the existing design-system `Screen`/`Text` primitives — no new dependency, no markdown parser, works fully offline (`components/LegalDocumentScreen.tsx`, new routes `app/legal/terms.tsx`/`privacy.tsx`). New `bodyRelaxed` typography token (`theme/tokens.ts`, documented in `docs/04-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`) for the wider line-height long-form prose actually needs, rather than a bare inline style value that CLAUDE.md's no-hardcoded-styling rule would flag. Settings' single "not yet published" alert row split into two real navigating rows.

**Age gate:** one combined checkbox at signup ("I'm 18+ and I agree to the Terms/Privacy") — self-attestation, the standard pattern virtually every messaging app uses, confirmed the only workable option this session: neither this app's schema nor Prembly's actual live-confirmed BVN/NIN response carries a date of birth to check against. New `users.terms_accepted_at` (nullable timestamptz), client-writable via the same RLS-grant pattern `read_receipts_enabled` already established (self-scoped, no fraud value in misrepresenting your own consent timestamp). The checkbox itself is the real app-store-facing gate (`Send code` is disabled without it, before an OTP is ever sent); the server-side timestamp write happens on successful OTP verify, best-effort so a rare write failure doesn't lock out a legitimate just-verified user. **Verified against a real RLS-scoped client** (a minted JWT via PostgREST, not the admin connection that would have bypassed RLS and missed a wrong grant) — confirmed a real self-update actually works, not just that the migration applies.

**Docs:** `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` updated throughout — §3's mitigation list, §4's ToS-must-cover section, and §6's checklist all corrected from "not built"/"not drafted" to what's actually true now, with "still needs counsel review" stated plainly rather than implying drafted-means-done.

**Tested:** `tsc --noEmit`/`expo lint` clean throughout. Migration applied and manually verified twice — once via the admin connection (write succeeds), once via a real RLS-scoped client (the actual path the app uses). Full `test:db` (22/22) re-run clean.

**Not shipped to the phone yet** — this PR bundles JS/TS changes (OTA-eligible on their own) with a DB migration (already live). Content moderation (next) is backend-only, no mobile changes — the two PRs can ship to the phone together once both are merged, one OTA update covering the signup/legal-screen changes.

**Content moderation, continued (second PR of this pass):** new `packages/moderation/` (same adapter-interface pattern as `packages/payments/`, `packages/kyc/`) — `provider.ts` (`ContentModerationProvider`, two-tier `blocked`/`flagged`/`clean` result) and `openai.ts` (real implementation against OpenAI's public moderation endpoint). Wired into `send-message`/`post-status`, checked **before** their billing RPC — a hard block never reaches `fn_send_message`/`fn_post_status`, so it's never charged or delivered; a soft flag sends normally and logs to the new `moderated_content` table after the real message/status id exists. Severe categories (`sexual/minors`, `hate/threatening`, `harassment/threatening`, `violence/graphic`, `self-harm/instructions`, `illicit/violent`) hard-block; everything else OpenAI flags is allow-and-log — explicit product decision, matching the manual-review-only posture the fraud signals already use.

**Not yet actually live — genuinely, not just "needs a key eventually":** no `OPENAI_API_KEY` exists in this environment. Verified for real, not assumed: ran `send-message-function.test.js` and `post-status-function.test.js`'s full existing suites unchanged with no key configured — both pass clean, with the moderation check's own `catch` block visibly logging the resulting 401 and allowing the send through, confirming the fail-open design actually works under the exact condition it exists for, not just in theory. New `moderation-functions.test.js` formalizes this as a real regression test, plus a second test that only runs (and is loudly, not silently, skipped otherwise) if a real key is ever added — same honesty `submit-kyc-function.test.js`'s real-money cost caveat already established for this test family.

**A real, unrelated flake surfaced while testing this, not caused by it:** `send-message-function.test.js`'s "platform earns its cut" assertion failed once (`expected=1 actual=3`) on an otherwise-clean run — traced to the shared `platform_revenue_earnings_cut` wallet (global, `user_id is null`) carrying a leftover balance from an earlier, unrelated test run earlier in this same session that didn't reset it before this one checked it. Confirmed via direct query (balance was back to 0 by the time it was checked, since this file's own cleanup had already run) and by re-running clean immediately after. Worth knowing for a future session: any test file that credits the shared platform wallet and doesn't call `resetPlatformWallets` in its own cleanup can leave this kind of cross-file pollution behind for whichever test happens to check that wallet's absolute balance next.

**Tests:** `deno check` clean on every new/changed function. Full `test:db` (22/22), `test:fraud` (20/20), `test:functions` (14 files including the new one) all re-run clean. `moderation-functions.test.js` wired into both `test:functions`/`test:functions:ci` and the CI workflow's env block (a new `OPENAI_API_KEY` secret reference, currently unset — same as production).

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 13, continued — rate limiting + duplicate-content detection; roadmap doc corrected; a real CI gap caught and fixed)

Same session as the fraud-infra work below, continued after it merged. Scoped "what's next" again per explicit request — the roadmap doc the user had open turned out stale against what was actually just built, corrected first (`docs/08-BUILD-PHASES-ROADMAP.md`'s Phase 5 section: several items marked done that were true since Phase 1 but never updated, "admin review queue" corrected to "Supabase Studio, confirmed, no dedicated tooling planned"). User picked §6 (rate limiting + duplicate-content detection, chat farming) over the app-store-blocking items.

**Caught before starting new work, not by anyone noticing a coverage gap:** `test:fraud`/`test:fraud:ci` were written last session but never actually wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the npm scripts existed, the CI step didn't. Fixed (PR #39) before building anything new, so this session's own tests would actually be enforced going forward.

**Built:** both §6 protections live entirely inside `fn_release_escrow` (redefined forward-only), not `fn_send_message` — a message never fails to send; an escrow that doesn't clear a check just stays `pending` instead of `released`, retried by a later release call once the rate window moves on, or eventually refunded to the payer by the existing `fn_refund_expired_escrows` sweep if it never clears — no new refund path, reusing §2's existing mechanism. New `pg_trgm` extension for near-duplicate text comparison (`similarity()`), same one-line pattern as `pg_cron`/`pg_net`/`supabase_vault`. Four new `pricing_config` keys (`escrow_release_messages_per_minute_cap`=10, `escrow_release_earnings_per_hour_cap`=100 — global per payee across every thread, not just one, catching a farmer running several simultaneous "conversations" — `duplicate_content_similarity_threshold_bps`=8200, `duplicate_content_lookback_messages`=20).

**A real bug caught by manually running it against a synthetic fixture before trusting the SQL** (same discipline that caught the collusion job's `min(uuid)` bug last time, and explicitly called for in this session's own plan file): the duplicate-content check originally compared _every_ pending escrow's message against payee B's history — including payer A's own opening messages. In a hand-built test with deliberately similar opener/reply text, A's own message got flagged as a "duplicate" of B's reply and never released, which makes no sense — A is the one paying, not the one being evaluated for farming. Fixed forward (`20260915121500_fix_duplicate_content_payer_leak.sql`) to only ever run the check when the message was actually sent by the payee.

**A real, important scope boundary found while updating docs, not assumed:** these protections only cover the 1:1 chat model. `fn_send_group_message` (group-chat billing, still kill-switched off) is a completely separate code path — immediate 70/30 settlement, no escrow at all — that never calls `fn_release_escrow` and gets none of this session's work. §10's stated group-chat risk ("no reply-gate, no per-message cap") is therefore still fully live for group chat specifically; `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md`'s group-chat gating note corrected to say so plainly rather than implying inherited protection that doesn't exist.

**Explicitly deferred, not forgotten, per user decision:** §6's "genuine engagement score" idea (weighting down short/templated replies, gating KYC-tier progression on it) — a real product/policy decision this app has no scaffolding for today (KYC tier is purely BVN/NIN-verification-driven), logged as a named future item in `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §6 rather than silently dropped.

**Tested for real:** `fraud-functions.test.js` extended with 6 new tests (20/20 total in the file) — a burst past the per-minute cap releases exactly the cap; the per-hour cap applies to a payee's releases across _both_ their threads, not each independently (verified by temporarily lowering the real cap and restoring it in a `finally`, same flip-and-restore pattern `group-chat-functions.test.js` already established for `group_chat_enabled`); duplicate content gets flagged with exactly one `fraud_signals` row, not one per repeat, while the payer's own message still releases (the bug above, now a regression test); and a genuinely varied conversation releases every escrow, none falsely flagged. Full `test:db` (22/22), `test:functions` (all 13 files, submit-kyc included, real Flutterwave), `test:group-chat` (19/19) all re-run clean.

**Deployed:** migration applied to the dev DB directly during the manual-verification step (both the original and the payer-leak fix), matching this session's own "run it for real before trusting it" discipline — nothing shipped to the app relies on unverified SQL.

**Scoped for next session:** the app-store-blocking items (content moderation, ToS/Privacy Policy, age gate) — still nothing built, still the actual remaining blocker to submission regardless of how much fraud infra exists; reserve buffer/clawback-as-debt (§3); legal sign-off; the still-pending `eas build` decision for device-fingerprinting's native modules, held at the user's own request until more work accumulates.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-15 (session 13 — Phase 5 fraud infra: device fingerprinting, top-up velocity limits, collusion detection, transfer-credit KYC gate)

Both real bugs from session 12 confirmed fixed on-device (top-up: instant, tested twice; blank screen: fix shipped, not yet recurred). Scoped what's next, per explicit request: re-audited the fraud/compliance checklists against actual code (not the roadmap doc's stale claims) and found several `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §12 items were already done since Phase 1 but never marked so (`is_frozen` enforcement, KYC-tiered withdrawal caps, row-level locking, signed webhooks) — the real, still-open gap was §2 (self-dealing/wash-chatting, "the big one") and its prerequisites. User picked fraud infra over the app-store-blocking items (age gate, ToS, content moderation — still open, next candidate).

Planned via `EnterPlanMode` given the size (5+ engineering pieces across schema/functions/mobile) — investigated via a dispatched Explore agent first rather than assumed, which found real, load-bearing facts: `is_frozen` enforcement already covers every money-moving function (a migration comment claiming otherwise was stale); `users.device_fingerprint_ids` has existed since the first schema migration but was **completely unused** — no table, no writer, no mobile capture; `fn_buy_credit` had **zero velocity limiting**, the more exploitable gap since topping up needs no KYC at all (the withdrawal side already had a Tier-1 daily cap); `fn_transfer_credit` checked `is_frozen`/`is_suspended` but had **no KYC-tier gate whatsoever** — a real, confirmed wash-trading-adjacent hole. §6 (rate-limiting/duplicate-content detection, chat-farming) explicitly scoped out — a separate problem from self-dealing, not part of what was picked. Explicit product decision before building anything: fraud signals are **logged for manual review only, never auto-freeze** — this app has no admin UI (Supabase Studio is it), and a false-positive freeze with no self-serve unfreeze path was judged worse than a slower manual review.

**Built, all four pieces (migrations `20260915090000` through `20260915095500`):**

1. **Device fingerprinting** — `device_fingerprints` table + `fn_link_device_fingerprint` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, the only writer to the previously-inert `users.device_fingerprint_ids`, matching that column's existing RLS lockdown). Mobile: new `apps/mobile/lib/deviceFingerprint.ts` (`expo-application`'s Android ID / iOS vendor ID, hashed with `expo-crypto` before it ever leaves the device — same "hash before it reaches the server" posture `KYC_HASH_PEPPER` established for BVN/NIN), called from `app/_layout.tsx` alongside the existing push-token resync, try/catch-guarded (this session's own blank-screen investigation is why that matters). New Edge Function `register-device-fingerprint`. **`expo-application` and `expo-crypto` are genuinely new native modules — this change is NOT OTA-eligible, unlike everything else shipped recently.** It needs a real `eas build` to reach the phone; not run without asking, per the standing rule.
2. **Top-up velocity limits** — `new_account_daily_topup_cap_kobo` (₦20,000/day) applies while an account is both new (`new_account_age_days`, default 30) **and** unverified (`kyc_tier = 0`); either condition alone removes the cap. Enforced in `fn_buy_credit`, a new `daily_topup_limit_exceeded` error mapped in `buy-credit/index.ts`.
3. **Collusion detection** — `fn_run_collusion_detection`, nightly `pg_cron` (`0 3 * * *`, pure-SQL, no `pg_net` needed unlike `reconcile-topups`). Two signal types, both additive-only to `fraud_signals`: shared device fingerprint + real paid activity between the pair (high severity), and one payee dominating a payer's weekly paid-message volume (medium severity, an explainable share-of-volume heuristic rather than a real graph algorithm — deliberate "stay lite" call, flagged as worth revisiting if it proves too coarse against real usage). **A real bug caught by actually running the function, not by review:** the first version used `min(fingerprint_id)` on a `uuid` column — Postgres has no built-in `min`/`max` aggregate for `uuid` despite it being sortable. Fixed in a forward-only follow-up migration (`20260915095500`) with `(array_agg(... order by ...))[1]` instead; the buggy version was already applied to the dev DB by the time this was caught, so it's corrected forward, not hand-edited, per CLAUDE.md.
4. **`fn_transfer_credit` KYC gate** — recipient now requires `kyc_tier >= 1` (mirroring `fn_initiate_withdrawal`'s existing gate), closing the path where a Tier-0 account accumulates `withdrawable_cash` via P2P transfer it could never earn or withdraw through any other route. New `recipient_kyc_required` error mapped in `transfer-credit/index.ts`.

**Tested for real, not just reasoned about:** new `fraud-functions.test.js` (`npm run test:fraud`), **14/14** on the first real run — link + idempotent replay + shared-hash-same-row, velocity cap enforced/bypassed correctly on both dimensions, collusion detection's two signal types as genuine true positives via real `fn_send_message` activity, **and a false-positive check** (a normal, low-volume, no-shared-fingerprint pair must not get flagged — a fraud system worth trusting has to prove it leaves real users alone, not just that it catches fraud), transfer-credit KYC gate both ways. Existing `wallet-functions.test.js` and `transfer-credit-function.test.js` updated for the new KYC-gate precondition (a real, expected regression on first full-suite run — both files' recipients needed `kyc_tier = 1` set to keep testing what they originally meant to; `transfer-credit-function.test.js` also gained a dedicated new assertion for the gate itself, against a separate Tier-0 recipient). Full `npm run test:db` (22/22), `test:functions` (all 13 files, submit-kyc included), and `test:group-chat` (19/19) all re-run clean afterward — this now runs automatically in CI (session 12's work) on any PR touching `supabase/**`/`packages/payments/**`, so this was the first real PR to prove that enforcement holds for a genuinely large diff, not just the small one that introduced it.

**Deployed:** all four migrations pushed, `register-device-fingerprint`/`buy-credit`/`transfer-credit` redeployed. **Mobile change not yet shipped** — device fingerprinting needs a native build (new native modules), the rest of this session touched no other mobile code. Docs updated: `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` (new `device_fingerprints` table, `fraud_signals`/`device_fingerprint_ids` status), `docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md` (`register-device-fingerprint`, `collusion-detection` cron entry), `docs/06-SECURITY-FRAUD-LOOPHOLES.md` §12 checklist re-audited honestly (several items marked done that were already true but unmarked, §2/§4 status notes added).

**Scoped for next session:** (1) an `eas build` decision for the fingerprinting native modules — ask before running; (2) §6 (rate-limiting + duplicate-content detection, chat-farming) — the other half of Phase 5's "build-now" list, explicitly deferred this session; (3) the app-store-blocking items from session 11 (content moderation, ToS/Privacy Policy, age gate) — still nothing built, still blocks submission regardless of fraud-infra progress; (4) legal sign-off, needs actual counsel, not code.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 12 — PR #26 + #27 confirmed shipped to device; permanent fix for the recurring top-up bug; blank-screen crash still open)

**Shipped:** PR #26 (palette refinement, real app icon/splash, chrome corrections) and PR #27 (full Settings, real blocking/reporting, push notifications, read receipts) were built via a real `eas build` and installed on the user's device — per the user's direct confirmation ("all of this was shipped and it reached my phone"). This closes the gap this session opened with: an `eas update:list` check showed the last _OTA_ publish was session 7's work (wallet tx history/read-cursor), which made it look like three sessions of work were still sitting unshipped — they weren't; they went out via a native build instead (expected, since session 11 added `expo-notifications` as a real native module, which is never OTA-eligible, and session 8's app icon never was either). **Not independently re-verified against the EAS build list this session** (a follow-up `eas build:list` query was in progress but the user redirected before it completed) — recorded here on the user's own real-device confirmation, which is the stronger signal anyway.

**Two real bugs surfaced by that on-device testing, both now top priority for the next session:**

1. **Buy-credit top-up still not landing — recurring for at least a 4th time — ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED, differently from both previous attempts.** User paid for credit twice; Flutterwave's side shows success, the app's side never reflects it. The `van_` virtual-account-vs-`tx_ref` theory floated earlier this session turned out to be a red herring: live ground truth (queried directly against Flutterwave's real API, not docs) confirmed the `reference` field on the actual charge equals `topups.id` exactly, for both card and bank-transfer/VA collections — the contract this codebase's two previous fixes arrived at was already correct. The real finding: **`webhook_events_seen` has never received a single real Flutterwave-initiated row, ever, in this app's entire history.** Every "completed" topup that has ever existed in this DB was reconciled by hand (session 4's, and now this one's). That means Flutterwave has never once successfully delivered a webhook to this endpoint — almost certainly a dashboard-side URL/secret misconfiguration this codebase's own code can never see or fix, not a payload-parsing bug. Confirmed via Flutterwave's real `/charges` API: two genuinely stuck, already-succeeded payments (`bdddc597-...` / `chg_5vwjUNk5Gt` and `0e7cb30b-...` / `chg_zJi8cibDw5`, ₦100/9 credits each) were manually reconciled via the same audited `fn_confirm_topup` path as before, with explicit user go-ahead — both landed, ledger conservation verified. A third pending topup (`91feabac-...`, ₦1,000) has no matching charge at Flutterwave at all — that transfer genuinely never happened, correctly still pending, not a bug.

   **The permanent fix, built and deployed this session, doesn't wait on the dashboard misconfiguration ever being found or staying fixed:** a new `reconcile-topups` Edge Function + pg_cron job (`*/10 * * * *`, new migration `20260914120000_reconcile_topups_cron.sql`) pulls Flutterwave's own `GET /charges?reference=` directly for any topup still `pending` after 5 minutes, and self-confirms it via `fn_confirm_topup` if Flutterwave says it succeeded — the same ground-truth check this incident was actually diagnosed from, running on a schedule instead of by hand. The webhook (once/if the dashboard config is actually fixed) stays the fast path; this is the guarantee. New `PaymentProvider.checkCollectionStatus` (`packages/payments/provider.ts` + `flutterwave.ts`) is the interface-respecting home for the Flutterwave call (CLAUDE.md rule #5 — no bypassing the adapter). Auth: `--no-verify-jwt` (same structural reason as `webhook-flutterwave` — pg_net can't cleanly carry a real Supabase JWT without hardcoding the service role key into a committed migration), gated instead by a constant-time-compared `X-Cron-Secret`, itself stored via `supabase_vault` on the DB side (tried a plain `app.settings.*` GUC first — hosted Supabase's `postgres` role isn't superuser and 42501'd) and `supabase secrets set` on the function side, neither committed to git.

   **Verified for real, not just unit-tested:** `deno check` clean on every function touching `packages/payments/`; `webhook-flutterwave-function.test.js` re-run clean (17/17) confirming the `checkCollectionStatus` addition didn't regress the existing contract; new `reconcile-topups-function.test.js` (6/6) covers the auth guard and a real (unmocked) Flutterwave call against a fixture with no matching charge, correctly left pending — the "confirms a real succeeded charge" path isn't unit-testable without spending real money, so it's covered by the two real reconciliations above instead, documented in the test file's own header. All six Edge Functions that import `packages/payments/flutterwave.ts` redeployed (`buy-credit`, `webhook-flutterwave`, `link-bank-account`, `list-banks`, `withdraw`, `reconcile-topups`) so none are running a stale bundle of the changed shared code. The full `pg_cron → pg_net → Vault → Edge Function` chain was fired manually once (not just the cron's own eventual tick) and confirmed a real 200 end-to-end. `buy-credit-function.test.js`/`withdraw-function.test.js` were **not** confirmed clean this session — both hit a genuine external network timeout (`UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT`) unrelated to this diff (neither test exercises `checkCollectionStatus`, and direct standalone probes of Flutterwave's API during the same window succeeded) — worth a clean re-run next session as routine hygiene, not treated as a known-broken regression.

2. **Intermittent full-black blank screen, first report — investigated via code review (no repro steps were ever available), best concrete lead fixed, structural gap closed regardless.** Happens sometimes during normal use, no visible error, requires a force-close/swipe-kill and reopen to recover — the user was asked for reproduction steps (screen/action/frequency) twice and moved straight to "go ahead" both times, so this was chased from static analysis alone, not a live repro.

   **Confirmed by grepping the whole app: zero error boundaries anywhere, ever.** That alone is sufficient to explain the exact reported symptom regardless of root cause — React Native's behavior for an uncaught render-time exception in a release build (no dev red-box) is precisely "the JS thread dies, nothing renders, the app sits there unresponsive until force-killed." This was a standing structural gap, not introduced this session, that any future crash of any cause would also have hit.

   **The strongest concrete lead, found by code review:** `lib/push.ts`'s `registerPushToken` called `Notifications.getExpoPushTokenAsync` with **no try/catch** — the _only_ one of three identical call sites in that same file left unguarded (`unregisterPushToken` and `hasRegisteredPushToken` both wrap it). `getExpoPushTokenAsync` hits Expo's push service over the network and can throw for anything from a connectivity blip to a misconfigured FCM project — a real, non-hypothetical failure mode this file's own comments already show awareness of elsewhere. Worse: this exact call runs **automatically and silently on every app session restore** via `resyncPushTokenIfPermitted`, fired as a bare `void` call in `app/_layout.tsx`'s `useEffect` with no `.catch()` anywhere above it in the chain — an unhandled rejection on the one automatic (not user-initiated) path, which fits "intermittent, during normal use" far better than anything tied to a specific screen. (The Settings-toggle call site was already safe — it goes through a `useMutation`, which react-query catches on its own.)

   **Fixed both:**
   - `registerPushToken`'s call now wrapped in try/catch, returning a new `'error'` status (not `'unsupported'`, which drives a misleading "need a real device" message for what's actually a transient failure on a real device) — `app/settings/index.tsx` updated to show a distinct, accurate message for it.
   - New `components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` wraps the whole app (`app/_layout.tsx`, inside `ThemeProvider` so its fallback can use the design system) — catches any render-time crash, from this cause or any other, and shows a recoverable "Reload" screen instead of a silent unresponsive one. Persists the crash (message/stack/component-stack/timestamp) to `AsyncStorage` — the same storage this app already uses for the Supabase session, no new dependency — closing the "no crash reporting wired up" gap flagged twice already, at least for the next time this happens: there's now something to actually retrieve instead of only a secondhand description.

   **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`/`expo lint` both clean. **Not verified against a real repro** — there wasn't one to test against; this closes the strongest lead found and the structural gap that made _any_ such crash catastrophic, but isn't a confirmed root-cause fix the way the top-up bug's was. If this recurs, `getLastCrash()` (new, exported from `ErrorBoundary.tsx`) should have something real to look at. Shipped via `eas update --branch preview` (update group `ad06281d-987f-450a-b2db-d32d1eccb764`, commit `e48d040`, confirmed the only new group via `eas update:list`).

**Follow-up, same session, after real-device confirmation that the fix "partly" worked:** the two reconciled payments landed and the cron closed the "stuck forever" bug, but the user reported the _wait itself_ was still bad — long enough that they gave up watching the payment screen and checked the wallet later instead, never seeing `TopupCongrats` fire. Root cause: `reconcile-topups`' own 5-minute age gate + 10-minute schedule (deliberately conservative, built as a background safety net) meant a user actively staring at the screen could wait up to ~15 minutes for anything to happen — nowhere near "instant." **Fix:** new `check-topup-status` Edge Function (same `checkCollectionStatus` ground-truth check, called on demand for one specific topup, no age gate, ownership-checked) + `useTopupStatus` (`apps/mobile/lib/queries/wallet.ts`) now actively polls it every ~4s while the modal is open and still pending, self-stopping the moment it resolves. This is now the real fast path; `reconcile-topups` remains the backstop for a user who closes the modal or backgrounds the app before the poll gets a chance to run. Docs: `docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md` new `check-topup-status` entry. Tests: new `check-topup-status-function.test.js` (5/5 — auth/ownership guards, immediate real Flutterwave check on a fresh unfunded topup, short-circuit on an already-completed one); `tsc --noEmit`/`expo lint` both clean on the mobile change. Deployed, and shipped to the phone via `eas update --branch preview` (update group `1f7f66d6-710e-4e12-ad09-5a506cbe5627`, commit `3777a45`, confirmed the only new group via `eas update:list` — two earlier attempts run through this session's own backgrounded-shell tooling exited 0 without actually publishing, silently truncated partway through the upload step; re-ran in the foreground and it completed normally, "Published!" and all. Worth remembering if a future `eas update` run ever exits clean with no Group ID printed: don't trust the exit code, check `eas update:list`). **Confirmed live on-device — user tested a real top-up and saw it work.** This bug is closed.

**Hardened for permanence, per explicit "make sure this doesn't recur" ask (PRs #31, #32):**

- Verified — not assumed — that `reconcile-topups`' cron has genuinely been firing every 10 minutes since deployment: `cron.job_run_details` and `net._http_response` both checked directly against the dev DB, all runs `succeeded`/200, one run even auto-caught and confirmed a real topup on its own.
- New `reconcile-topups-deployed-smoke.test.js` / `check-topup-status-deployed-smoke.test.js`, same rationale as the pre-existing `webhook-flutterwave-deployed-smoke.test.js`: every other test in this suite proves the _code_ is correct via a local `deno run`, never that the function _as actually deployed_ behaves the same way — the exact gap that let the original incident hide for weeks. Wired into `npm run test:deployed`.
- `.env.example`'s `FLW_WEBHOOK_SECRET_HASH` comment still described the old, **wrong** verification scheme (HMAC-SHA256 over the body) that caused the original incident in the first place — a future implementer reading that file first would've been steered right back into the bug this session's whole chain of fixes closed. Corrected, with the incident history left visible rather than quietly overwritten. Also documented `CRON_INTERNAL_SECRET`, which previously existed only in Vault/function secrets with zero textual trace anywhere.
- **The structural gap, closed:** CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) only ever ran lint + typecheck — never the actual wallet/ledger tests. That's _why_ this bug recurred three times: nothing ever enforced CLAUDE.md's "no PR merges without ledger-conservation tests for wallet code" rule at merge time, only session-to-session discipline. New `test-wallet-code` CI job runs `test:db` + `test:functions:ci` (every Edge Function test except `submit-kyc`, which costs real money per run and stays manual-only) against the real dev DB and live Flutterwave credentials, on every PR and push to `main` — explicit user go-ahead obtained first, since it means production Flutterwave/Supabase credentials now live in GitHub Actions secrets. **Self-tested, not just reasoned about:** its own introducing PR (#32) ran through it twice — first failed for a real reason (`--env-file=.env` needs an actual file, which doesn't exist in CI; added `test:db:ci`/`test:functions:ci` variants), second run fully green, confirmed via `gh run watch`.

**Scoped for next session, in priority order:** (1) confirm the blank-screen fix actually holds on-device — genuinely unconfirmed, watch for it recurring and check `getLastCrash()` immediately if it does, since that would mean the push-token theory wasn't the whole story rather than starting the investigation over from nothing; (2) the roadmap's next open items (see bottom of this doc / `docs/08-BUILD-PHASES-ROADMAP.md`) are Phase 5 fraud/compliance hardening (nothing built yet, blocks public launch and the group-chat kill-switch) and the still-missing app-store items from session 11's honest status update (content moderation, ToS/Privacy Policy, age verification). Also worth a look: whether Flutterwave's dashboard webhook config is actually fixable (the root cause `reconcile-topups` works around, not the thing it fixed) — the app doesn't depend on it anymore, but a 10-minute worst-case delay on every top-up instead of near-instant is a real UX cost worth closing if it's a quick dashboard fix.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 11 — full Settings, real blocking/reports, push notifications, read receipts)

PR #26 merged first (per explicit request). Then a genuinely large ask — "build the full settings... privacy, last seen, media, notifications... add settings for any real gap... build in push notifications exactly like WhatsApp" — was scoped honestly before building rather than filled in with fake toggles: about a third of "full WhatsApp settings" would have been UI with nothing real behind it (no media pipeline anywhere in this app, no presence tracking, no push infra yet). Presented the split (what's real and buildable now vs. what needs new infra vs. what's out of scope and why) and asked four targeted questions before writing code. All four came back "recommended": push notifications for new messages only (not all events), skip last-seen/presence, defer avatar upload, and account deletion as a support-request queue (not instant self-service, given this app custodies real money).

**New migration `20260914090000_settings_privacy_reports_push.sql`:**

- **Real blocking, finally** — the single most interesting gap found this pass: `threads.is_blocked` and its enforcement inside `fn_send_message` (reject with `thread_blocked`) had existed since Phase 1, but **nothing anywhere ever set it**. There was no way to actually block someone, despite the column and the guard both being live and tested. Replaced the boolean with `blocked_by` (who blocked it — needed for a "Blocked contacts" list and to stop a blocked person unblocking themselves) and added `fn_set_thread_blocked` + `set-thread-blocked` Edge Function. `fn_send_message` re-created (`create or replace`, forward-only) against the new column.
- `read_receipts_enabled` on `users` (default `true`) — real privacy toggle over data that already existed (the read-cursor built two sessions ago for unread counts). Turning it off doesn't stop the cursor from being written (your own unread-badge accuracy shouldn't depend on whether you share it with others, same as WhatsApp's actual behavior) — it stops `thread/[id].tsx` from _rendering_ a read indicator to the other participant, gated client-side on the partner's own flag.
- `push_tokens` table — one row per device, RLS lets the client upsert/delete its own rows directly (no `SECURITY DEFINER` needed for a self-scoped non-money write). Deliberately no `users.push_notifications_enabled`-style column: "off" is just "no token row," one source of truth instead of two that could disagree.
- `user_reports` and `account_deletion_requests` — both insert-only-own via RLS, neither readable back through the app (ops/admin review only). The reporting system is an actual app-store review requirement (`docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §4) that was previously entirely missing.

**Push notifications — real pipeline, new-message events only (as scoped):** `expo-notifications` + `expo-device` installed, `lib/push.ts` handles permission request/token registration (only ever prompted from the explicit Settings toggle, never on app launch — a silent `resyncPushTokenIfPermitted` on login re-registers an _already-granted_ token without prompting again). `supabase/functions/_shared/push.ts`'s `sendPushToUser` posts to Expo's push API directly (no SDK needed) and is wired into `send-message` via `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` so a slow/failed push provider can never delay or fail the billing-critical response it's piggybacking on. `app.json` gained the `expo-notifications` config plugin, reusing `android-icon-monochrome.png` (built during the app-icon pass) as the Android notification icon — Android requires a white-alpha silhouette for exactly this, which that asset already was.

**Full Settings screen rebuilt** (`app/settings/index.tsx`): Profile (name/about — both already client-updatable columns, no new backend), Account (KYC status, unchanged; delete-account request), Privacy (read receipts toggle, Blocked contacts list, Report a user), Notifications (push toggle with real permission-denied messaging), Help (Invite a friend via the native share sheet, a "Terms & Privacy" row that honestly says "not yet published" rather than linking to a policy that doesn't exist, app version from `expo-constants`).

**Thread screen gained a real overflow menu** (block/unblock, report) — the natural place WhatsApp/Telegram put these, not just buried in Settings. Composer disables with an honest placeholder when the thread is blocked (either direction). **Read receipts are live**: own messages show a single check (sent) or double check (`Ionicons` `checkmark-done`) once the partner's read cursor has passed that message's timestamp — real data, gated on the partner's actual privacy flag.

**Explicitly not built, per the scoping conversation, not oversights:** last-seen/online presence (no tracking infra, would need a heartbeat mechanism), avatar photo upload (needs a Storage bucket — first media feature this app would ever have), media/network-usage settings (nothing to configure without a media pipeline), two-step verification PIN (doesn't map to phone-OTP-only auth), change phone number (a real re-verification flow, not a Settings field), push events beyond new messages (credit received, top-up confirmed, withdrawal completed — same mechanism would work, just not wired this pass).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`/`expo lint` clean. Full `npm run test:functions` (all 9 files, including new `set-thread-blocked-function.test.js`, 12/12), `npm run test:db` (22/22), `npm run test:group-chat` (19/19) all re-run clean — confirms the `threads.is_blocked` → `blocked_by` migration and `fn_send_message` re-creation didn't regress anything already built. `send-message` and `set-thread-blocked` deployed.

**Docs updated for real gaps found, not just new features:** `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §4 now states plainly which app-store-review items are done (reporting/blocking) vs. still genuinely missing (content moderation, ToS/Privacy Policy, age verification) — an honest status line, not a silent "will build."

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 10 — search-bar spacing, responsive chrome, chat wallpaper)

Third pass on the same day's design branch (PR #26, still open/unmerged — landed as a third commit, not a new PR), responding to a "confirm X / build Y" request.

**Confirmed, not re-touched:** the search bar genuinely is the thread list's `ListHeaderComponent` (scrolls away with the list), not part of the fixed `AppHeader`/`ChatsSubHeader` stack above it — re-checked the actual render tree rather than assumed, since a later regression silently moving it back would be exactly the kind of thing worth re-verifying rather than trusting from memory.

**Fixed:**

1. **Search bar had zero top padding** — flush against `ChatsSubHeader` above it. Added `spacing.lg` (16dp) of top padding, which lands at exactly the requested ~0.1in at the same 160dp/in convention `layout.barHeight` already uses — no new token needed.
2. **Header/tab-bar sizing is now actually responsive, not a fixed number.** `theme/tokens.ts`'s `layout` (added last pass) was a static `{barHeight: 96, ...}` regardless of device — on an explicit "should adjust automatically on a smaller/bigger screen, same look and feel" ask, `ThemeProvider` now scales all three `layout` values by the device's real screen width (`useWindowDimensions`, 375pt reference, clamped 0.85–1.15×) before handing them out via `useTheme()`. No call-site changes needed in `_layout.tsx`/`AppHeader.tsx` — they already read sizes through `useTheme().layout`, so the responsive behavior is transparent to them.
3. **New chat wallpaper** (`components/ui/ChatWallpaper.tsx`, new `react-native-svg` dependency — also unblocks the still-unbuilt "Status ring" motion spec that already anticipated this library). A tiled, 6%-opacity, single-tone vector pattern behind the thread's message list — same concept as WhatsApp's doodle wallpaper / Telegram's paper-plane pattern, deliberately different content (a chat bubble, a coin, a four-point "value" spark — chat + money, this app's whole premise) so it doesn't read as a clone. Tint is `text.secondary`, so light and dark mode both get a version that's actually visible-but-subtle against their own canvas, not one fixed asset baked for one theme.

**Design-standard audit, as requested — status of each:**

- Tap targets: header/menu actions already use `hitSlop` to clear the 44×44pt minimum (docs/04 §6); sub-header tabs are full flex columns, comfortably over minimum.
- Color contrast: covered by the palette-refinement pass's WCAG audit (docs/04 §1) — no new gaps introduced by this pass's changes.
- Icon consistency: fully unified on Ionicons app-wide as of the previous pass; this pass didn't touch iconography further.
- Hardcoded colors: none outside `theme/tokens.ts` (re-grepped, still clean).
- **Still unconfirmed, flagged rather than assumed:** whether `tabBarStyle`'s explicit `height` interacts correctly with React Navigation's own bottom safe-area inset handling on a real notched/home-indicator device — plausible from the API, not verified on hardware. Worth a real-device look alongside the "0.6in" claim itself before treating either as final.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` both clean.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 9 — chrome correction: bars, icons, sizing)

Second correction pass on the same day's palette work (PR #26, still open/unmerged at the user's request — this landed as a second commit on that same branch, not a new PR). Flagged back explicitly after reviewing the palette-refinement branch:

**What was flagged as already correct, not touched again:**

- `AppHeader`'s background was already `bg.canvas` (light milk / dark near-black) — never was wine. Only its height/icon sizing needed the size-up.

**What was actually wrong and got fixed:**

1. **`#B5677B` removed entirely, not just renamed** — it was dark mode's `brandPrimaryPressed` (a lightened/pressed wine that reads as mauve/purple at that lightness). `brandPrimary`/`brandPrimaryPressed` are now **theme-invariant** — the exact same wine in both modes — because the _only_ thing that ever needed a dark-mode-lightened variant was chrome (bars) using `brandPrimary` as a background, and that usage is gone too (next point). A filled button or badge doesn't need a lighter wine to stay legible in dark mode; only a colored bar sitting directly on a dark canvas would have.
2. **Bottom tab bar and search bar were wine-filled backgrounds with cream content — inverted from what was asked.** Both are now `bg.canvas`/`bg.surfaceAlt` (light milk / dark near-black) with wine (light) / white (dark) icon and text — `text.secondary` is the new "on-canvas accent" role, redefined in dark mode as plain white instead of the old lightened-wine `#E7A9BA` (removes the "purple text" complaint at the source, not just its most visible instance). Found and fixed the same bug in the "InvolveMe" wordmark and three other on-canvas links (`wallet.tsx`'s "Change bank"/bank-account text, `thread/[id].tsx`'s reply hint) that were still using the old `color="brand"` (now theme-invariant wine) — would have gone near-invisible against a dark canvas in dark mode had they been left as-is; caught by grepping every `color="brand"` usage once the token's meaning changed, not left for a bug report.
3. **Emoji tab icons replaced with real vector icons** (`@expo/vector-icons`' Ionicons — added as a new dependency, explicitly requested override of the earlier "stay lite, no icon package" call). Emoji render in a fixed native color no matter what `color` style is applied — the literal "colored icons" complaint — and can render as a broken/missing glyph on some Android font configs, the most likely real cause of "menu icons appear broken." Header's "+"/"⋮" also moved to Ionicons for the same reason and for one consistent icon system app-wide. Status tab's icon reverted mid-task, on direct request, from a tools/wrench glyph (a later mockup-driven choice) back to a plain ring shape (`ellipse`/`ellipse-outline`) matching the very first glyph used for that tab and this doc's own "Status ring" motion-spec language.
4. **Bars sized up**: new `theme.layout` token group (`barHeight=96` ≈0.6in at the conventional 160dp/in baseline, `tabIconSize=28`, `headerIconSize=26`), `typography.tabBarLabel` bumped 12→14. The old focused-tab pill background is gone — with the bar no longer wine, there's nothing for a pill to contrast against; the icon/label color change alone now carries "active," the same convention WhatsApp/Telegram's own bars use.
5. **Chats/Groups/Contacts sub-header now spans the full width in three equal columns** (`flex: 1` each) instead of being left-clustered with a fixed gap, per an explicit "spread and space equally to 100% width, same column width" ask.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` both clean. Not yet visually confirmed on a real device — the bar-height-in-inches claim specifically is a dp/pt-convention approximation, not something confirmable from source alone; worth a real-device look before treating 96 as final.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 8 — palette refinement + real app icon/splash)

Requested against exactly two anchor colors (canvas `#FDFFF7`, brand wine `#5F1B31` reconfirmed) with instructions to derive everything else "intelligently" from them, fix odd/unmatched tokens, and replace the placeholder app icon/splash with a supplied logo image (removing its baked-in checkerboard "transparency" backdrop).

**Palette — `theme/tokens.ts` light palette re-derived, not re-picked ad hoc.** Every neutral is now a defined mix of wine into near-white (surfaces/borders) or near-black (ink text) at stated ratios — see `docs/04-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §1 for the exact percentages and full before/after table. Three tokens that related to neither anchor color got replaced (`bgSurfaceAlt`'s old `#FFE6D8` peach, `textTertiary`'s flat `#8A8A8A` gray, `textPrimary`'s flat `#000000`); `textInverse`/`badgeText` now reuse the canvas anchor directly instead of a third unrelated light tone. **A real, live accessibility bug found while auditing contrast, not just a re-tint:** `success`/`danger` — both actively rendered as `Text` colors throughout the app (error/confirmation messages, grep-verified before touching anything) — were failing WCAG AA (2.60:1, 3.73:1) against the new, brighter canvas; darkened to 4.53:1/4.55:1 while staying recognizably green/red. `warning` fixed the same way pre-emptively (unused today). `accentCredit` also fails (2.03:1) but was deliberately left vivid — nothing renders it as text today (verified), and dulling gold for a contrast fix nobody needs yet would cost the "gold = my value" association `docs/04` §1 documents. Also fixed in passing while doing this pass: `thread/[id].tsx`'s two remaining hardcoded hex/rgba values (`#FFFFFF`, `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)` for own-message-bubble text) — a pre-existing CLAUDE.md rule-#\`no-hardcoded-hex\` gap, found by grepping the whole app for stray hex/rgba once the token pass was underway, not left for later. New `theme/withAlpha()` helper so a translucent variant of a token can be derived at render time without hardcoding a second literal.

**App icon & splash — real assets for the first time.** The iOS icon was still, unnoticed until now, the stock never-customized Expo Icon Composer template (blue default symbol) — `assets/expo.icon/` bundle removed, `ios.icon` repointed at a plain PNG. Processing the supplied logo (Python + Pillow, installed for this session): detected its bounding square from the wine-colored pixels themselves (not a fixed guess), filled the small checkerboard corner slivers by projecting each pixel onto the card's own rounded-corner arc and sampling that point's real gradient — extends the existing gradient rather than pasting a flat patch. **A real bug caught and fixed while doing this, not shipped broken:** the first corner-cleanup pass used a color threshold ("non-wine pixels near a corner = background") and nearly erased letters in the "InvolveMe" wordmark, since white wordmark text is also non-wine and sits close enough to the bottom edge to overlap a naively-square corner zone — switched to a geometric test (outside the rounded rect's own curve) instead, which by construction can never touch content that's actually inside the card. Produced: `icon.png` (1024×1024 flattened, full-bleed), `android-icon-foreground.png` + `monochromeImage` (a separate white-on-transparent glyph+wordmark cutout, scaled to a 62% safe zone so no launcher mask clips it — flat `#5F1B31` `adaptiveIcon.backgroundColor`, no background image needed), `favicon.png`, and `splash-icon.png` (the same transparent cutout on a `#5F1B31` splash screen — a plain wine screen with the white mark centered, matching WhatsApp's own splash convention, rather than repeating the full gradient square at a size where its baked-in background would show a seam against the surrounding screen color).

**Not done:** the "well motion animated design" part of the request — that's `docs/04-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §4's existing Phase 4 motion spec (credit-fly animation, escrow pulse, earning flash, skeletons), fully written but **not built** in any session yet. Treating "recolor + real icon" and "build the motion system" as two different asks rather than quietly bundling a large unbuilt animation pass into a color-token request — flagged back to the user rather than assumed.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` both clean. Every generated image visually inspected (not just assumed correct from script exit code) — including a mock composite of the splash asset on a phone-sized wine canvas to confirm it actually reads clean before trusting it. Not yet visually confirmed on a real device/simulator (would need a real device install to see the new native icon — this is an `eas update`-ineligible change, since app icons are compiled into the native binary, not OTA-updatable; needs a real `eas build` before it shows up in a way the standing no-unprompted-build rule requires asking about first).

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-14 (session 7 — unread/read-cursor tracking, real end to end)

Per the explicit "start with unread/read-cursor tracking" call, after reviewing what's naturally next post-rebrand. This is a real, complete feature — not scoped-and-parked like the group-chat billing model — because unlike that one, there was no open product decision blocking it: read-cursor tracking has one obvious correct shape (per-participant last-read timestamp, unread = messages from the other side sent after it) and no money involved.

**New migration `20260914080000_thread_read_cursor.sql`:**

- `threads.participant_a_last_read_at`/`participant_b_last_read_at` (nullable timestamptz, `null` = never read) — a column pair rather than a separate `thread_reads` table, since this schema's threads are strictly 1:1; a generic per-user-per-thread table would just be `threads` with extra steps. Group chats would need their own version of this if `group_chat_enabled` ever flips on (a `group_members.last_read_at` column fits that N-member shape better) — not scoped now, not needed while that feature is gated off.
- `thread_unread_counts` — a `security_invoker = true` view computing, per caller, unread messages per thread (sender ≠ caller, sent after the caller's own cursor). The `security_invoker` flag is load-bearing, not stylistic: without it, the view would run with the migration role's RLS bypass rather than the querying user's, silently exposing every thread's unread count to everyone. Confirmed the view's `auth.uid()`-scoped behavior via real authenticated REST calls in the test suite, not just reasoned about — a raw pg admin connection has no JWT claims to test this with.
- `fn_mark_thread_read(thread_id, caller_id)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`) sets _only_ the caller's own cursor column. Deliberately not a client-writable RLS policy + column grant: Postgres column-level grants aren't conditional on which participant slot the caller occupies, so that shortcut would let participant A clear participant B's unread badge on their shared thread (RLS's row check would pass, since A does legitimately own the row).

**New Edge Function `mark-thread-read`** — thin wrapper, same `_shared/auth.ts` JWT-verification posture as every other function here even though nothing financial is involved. Called on opening a thread.

**Tested:** new `supabase/tests/mark-thread-read-function.test.js` (wired into `npm run test:functions`), **10/10** — auth/validation errors, the view's per-caller scoping (own messages never count as unread to yourself, another participant's cursor can't be touched), and a real ledger-adjacent-but-not-financial correctness case (a message sent after the cursor is unread again, the earlier now-read one isn't double-counted). One real bug caught and fixed while writing this test, not by review: the last assertion originally used a JS-generated `Date.now()` timestamp for the new message, which raced against clock skew between the test process and the DB server and made the assertion genuinely flaky — fixed by computing the timestamp server-side, relative to the cursor value Postgres itself had just written. Full `npm run test:functions` chain (all nine function test files, ending with this new one), `npm run test:db` (22/22), and `npm run test:group-chat` (19/19) all re-run clean afterward, confirming the two new nullable columns on `threads` didn't regress anything.

**Mobile:** `lib/queries/threads.ts`'s `useThreads` extended with a real `unread_count` per thread (one more query against the view, same pattern as the last-message-preview join); new `useTotalUnreadCount` (drives the Chats tab's native `tabBarBadge`, 15s poll since neither `threads` nor `messages` is on the realtime publication) and `useMarkThreadRead` (called from `thread/[id].tsx` on mount). `chats.tsx`'s `ThreadRow` restructured to a proper two-column layout (name+preview on the left, timestamp+unread-pill stacked on the right) using the `badge.bg`/`badge.text` tokens added during the wine rebrand — they'd been defined but unused until now. `Text.tsx` gained a `badge` color variant for this.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` both clean. Not yet visually confirmed on a real device, not shipped via `eas update`.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-13 (session 6 — group-chat schema/function built, kill-switched off)

Same session, continued after session 5's rebrand + PR #22 merged to `main` and shipped via `eas update` (group ID `5903f437-fa9c-4680-8ec8-fcd4d92b68d9`). Per the user's explicit "start building the group-chat schema/function," the model decided in session 5 (`docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` §10) is now actually built — not just documented.

**New migration `20260913200000_group_chats.sql`:** `group_threads`/`group_members`/`group_messages` tables (RLS: select-if-member, no client writes, same posture as every other money-touching table) and `fn_send_group_message` (`SECURITY DEFINER`). Implements exactly what §10 specifies: same word-count cost formula as 1:1 messages, immediate 70/30 owner/platform split (`platform_group_message_take_bps`, new independently-tunable config key), self-post exception (owner posting to their own group earns nothing, message still costs credits), fixed ascending-wallet-id lock order across sender/owner/platform wallets (same rule `fn_transfer_credit` follows, generalized to three parties). Reuses the existing `platform_revenue_earnings_cut` wallet for the platform's cut rather than inventing a new wallet kind, matching `fn_transfer_credit`'s own precedent for a credit-denominated take.

**The kill switch is real, not just a doc note:** new `pricing_config.group_chat_enabled` ships at `0`, and `fn_send_group_message` checks it itself and refuses to run — so there's no path to bypass the Phase-5 gate by calling the database function directly, only the (not-yet-built) Edge Function.

**Tested per CLAUDE.md's wallet-code requirement:** new `supabase/tests/group-chat-functions.test.js` (`npm run test:group-chat`), **19/19** against the real dev DB — the 70/30 split, ledger conservation across every wallet touched, the self-post exception, a concurrency/double-spend check, and membership/existence validation. Every test flips `group_chat_enabled` to `1` for its own duration and restores it to `0` in a `finally` block (plus a belt-and-suspenders reset in `main()`'s own `finally`) — confirmed directly against the DB after the run that the flag is back at `0`, not just assumed from exit code. Full `npm run test:db` re-run clean afterward (22/22, no regression from the `ledger_entries` check-constraint changes).

**Not built, deliberately, and not blocking anything:** a group-creation/invite flow (nothing writes `group_threads`/`group_members` outside the test file yet) and an Edge Function to expose this to the app — no urgency while the kill switch is off, per the user's own "hold behind Phase 5" call. Both are cheap to add whenever the feature is actually scheduled.

**Not shipped anywhere** (nothing to ship — no Edge Function, no mobile UI, and the DB function is inert while `group_chat_enabled = 0`). The migration itself is already applied to the dev DB (`supabase db push`, confirmed 1 migration applied).

Scoped in `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` §10 with three options; **user then picked the billing model directly, same session**: sender pays the normal per-message cost, split 70% to the group owner / 30% platform, settled immediately (no escrow — nothing in a broadcast channel maps to "B replied"). Two follow-up calls also made explicitly, not assumed: **(a)** the owner does not earn from their own posts (self-post is a hard exception, not just an edge case worth noting — closes the trivial single-account version of cashing out for free), **(b)** this feature is **held behind Phase 5's fraud infra** even once built — unlike §9's credit-transfer (capped, requires a real second person by phone), this model has no reply-gate and no cap, so one colluding alt account can convert credit to cash on every message with zero interaction from "the owner" side. All three decisions written into `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` §10 (formula, self-post exception, gating rationale), `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md`'s "Group threads — scoping" note (schema, incl. `owner_earning_credits`/`platform_take_credits` columns), and `docs/08-BUILD-PHASES-ROADMAP.md` (new Phase 5 gate bullet). **Still nothing built** — no migration, no function — this was a design/decision pass only, per the user's explicit request to settle the billing model before any code.
