## Snapshot as of 2026-09-13 (session 4 — both top-priority items from the last handoff closed)

**Both items scoped at the end of the previous session are done: the top-up-credit webhook bug is fixed at its actual root cause (not the same fix re-applied), and the header/nav UI overhaul is built.** Not yet shipped to the phone (pure JS/TS, OTA-eligible per the standing no-unprompted-`eas build` rule) — asking before `eas update`, per the user's explicit instruction to ask once both items were done and tested.

### 1. Top-up credit not landing — re-diagnosed from scratch, real root cause found and fixed

The previous session's writeup was explicitly flagged as possibly incomplete, and it was: **the JWT-gate and `Buffer`-import fixes were real bugs, correctly fixed, but neither was the actual reason credit never landed.** The real bug: `webhook-flutterwave` was verifying signatures against a header (`flutterwave-signature`) and algorithm (HMAC-SHA256, base64) that Flutterwave **does not send or use** — and reading `data.reference`/`type`/`succeeded` field names Flutterwave also doesn't use. Every real webhook was silently rejected with 401 from the moment that "fix" shipped, which is _why_ it recurred after two rounds of fixes that were each individually correct but aimed at the wrong layer.

**Found by re-verifying from scratch, not by re-reading the previous session's notes:**

- Queried `webhook_events_seen` directly against the dev DB: 0 rows, ever — confirming no webhook had ever been successfully processed, consistent with (but not yet proof of) the earlier incident.
- Cross-checked Flutterwave's own `GET /charges?customer_id=...`: found **two real ₦100 payments made that same day**, both `status: "succeeded"`, both still stuck `pending` in this DB — live, current, active proof the bug was still happening, not historical.
- Fetched developer.flutterwave.com/docs/webhooks and .../reference/webhooks fresh (not from memory or the previous session's claims), independently, twice, both agreeing on every field: header is **`verif-hash`**, verification is a **plain string comparison** (no HMAC, no digest — Flutterwave's webhook scheme is simpler and older-style than the previous session assumed), envelope is `{ event, data }` not `{ type, data }`, a charge's reference is `data.tx_ref` (lowercase status `successful`/`failed`), a transfer's reference is `data.reference` (uppercase status `SUCCESSFUL`/`FAILED`, and there's only **one** `transfer.completed` event, not the previously-guessed `transfer.disburse`/`transfer.reversal` pair).

**Fixed:** `packages/payments/flutterwave.ts`'s `verifyWebhook` (plain compare against `verif-hash`, synthesizes the idempotency key as `` `${event}:${data.id}` `` since Flutterwave's payload has no top-level delivery id) and `supabase/functions/webhook-flutterwave/index.ts` (correct field names, correct event routing, plus new diagnostic logging on any verification failure or a recognized-event-with-unexpected-shape — so if this ever breaks again for a fourth reason, it's visible in `supabase functions logs webhook-flutterwave` within minutes, not silently invisible for weeks the way this exact class of bug was, twice). Both test files (`webhook-flutterwave-function.test.js`, `webhook-flutterwave-deployed-smoke.test.js`) rewritten to exercise the _real_ contract instead of this project's own prior (wrong) assumptions — the previous version of this test suite passing 87/87 never caught the bug precisely because it was testing the code against itself. `docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md`'s webhook section corrected to match, with the wrong-twice history left visible rather than quietly overwritten.

**Verified, not just re-argued:**

- `deno check` clean, `prettier --check` clean.
- Local test (`node --env-file=.env supabase/tests/webhook-flutterwave-function.test.js`, real dev DB): **17/17 passing.**
- Deployed (`supabase functions deploy webhook-flutterwave --use-api --no-verify-jwt`, now version 7, `verify_jwt` confirmed still `false` via the Management API): deployed smoke test **2/2 passing.**
- `npm run test:functions`'s chained run hit this project's well-documented general pooler/process flakiness partway through (an `AddrInUse` from a leftover `deno` process, and one run that was simply slow, not broken — both operational, not code, issues); re-run individually instead: `send-message-function.test.js` 20/20, `buy-credit-function.test.js` 11/11 (the other real consumer of `packages/payments/flutterwave.ts`) — both clean. `deno check` across every deployed function's `index.ts` also clean. The other five function test files (withdraw, post-status, find-user-by-phone, submit-kyc, link-bank-account, transfer-credit) weren't re-run this session — none of them touch anything changed here, and `submit-kyc`'s test specifically costs real money per real run (~₦45/Prembly call), so it wasn't re-run without a reason to suspect it.
- **Real-transaction confirmation, per the user's explicit "confirm it holds with a real transaction, not just a passing test":** the two real stuck ₦100 payments found during diagnosis (`chg_uaP4X5W6rO`, `chg_8AHFxYniLv`) were manually reconciled via `fn_confirm_topup` — same audited, idempotent function the webhook itself calls, using data independently confirmed from Flutterwave's own API, **with the user's explicit go-ahead asked and given before touching any balance.** Credits landed (9 credits each, confirmed in `topups`/`wallets`). **What this does NOT yet prove: a real Flutterwave-initiated webhook actually reaching the fixed code with zero manual steps** — the manual reconciliation and the deployed smoke test both prove the code is correct, but neither is Flutterwave itself calling the endpoint. The next real top-up (fresh, made after this fix) is the one that closes that last gap — watch `webhook_events_seen` for a new row appearing automatically when it happens.

## Snapshot as of 2026-09-13 (session 3, continued — same session, past midnight)

**Standing rule from the user, going forward: never run `eas build` without an explicit go-ahead in the moment.** EAS free-tier build minutes are limited, and most fixes are JS/TS-only — the previous section already wired up EAS Update (OTA) for exactly this reason. Reach for a native rebuild only when something actually native changed (permissions, icons, native modules, SDK bump), and ask first even then.

**Two real auth bugs found the moment the user tried the app for real** (see below) got fixed the same session, plus the mobile UI got wired to the backend for the first time — chats, wallet, and status were placeholder screens before this. Later the same session: a real `eas update` misconfiguration was found and fixed (below), and KYC + bank-account linking — the last gap on the withdraw path — got built against a real vendor (Prembly), closing the loop CLAUDE.md rule #7 requires. **Then a real production incident**: a real user top-up sat unconfirmed for over an hour because `webhook-flutterwave` had silently rejected every webhook since its very first deploy — see below for the full incident, root cause, and fix. **Then, once the incident was resolved and the fix verified**, the mobile UI for KYC submission and bank-account linking got built — the piece the earlier KYC/bank-linking section had left as "backend done, UI pending."

### Mobile UI for KYC submission + bank-account linking

`(auth)`-adjacent identity verification lives in Settings (previously an unreachable placeholder screen — nothing linked to `/settings` at all; added a "Settings" link to the Chats header so it's actually reachable now), bank-account linking lives in Wallet alongside buy-credit/withdraw (a wallet action, not an account setting).

- **New `lib/queries/kyc.ts`**: `useKycTier` (plain read, not an Edge Function — reading `kyc_tier` isn't money-affecting), `useSubmitKyc` (wraps `submit-kyc`).
- **New `lib/queries/banks.ts`**: `useBanks` (wraps `list-banks`, `staleTime` an hour — bank codes don't change often), `useLinkBankAccount` (wraps `link-bank-account`).
- **`lib/edgeFunctions.ts` extended** to support `GET` (previously POST-only) — needed for `list-banks`; body is omitted entirely for GET rather than sent as an empty object, since some of this project's functions reject a GET carrying one.
- **Settings screen**: BVN/NIN type picker + number input, submits to `submit-kyc`, shows "Verified ✓" once `kyc_tier >= 1`. No auto-retry or speculative resubmission — every call costs the platform real money (see the KYC section above), so the UI only ever submits on an explicit tap.
- **Wallet screen**: an "Add bank account" flow (bank search list from `list-banks` → account number → `link-bank-account`) appears once KYC is verified; before that, a "Verify your identity first →" link to Settings instead of a form that would only ever get rejected by the server's own KYC-tier gate.
- **Not built**: media upload for status (unchanged from earlier), presence/typing/read-receipts (unchanged from earlier).

**Not shipped to the phone yet** — this is pure JS/TS (no native dependency added), so it's an `eas update` away, not a build, once the user asks for it — per the standing rule, not run automatically here.

### Wallet UX polish + peer-to-peer credit transfer (`fix/topup-webhook-incident` follow-up, same session)

Three small UX fixes plus one large, deliberately-flagged new feature, requested together after the KYC/bank-linking UI above shipped.

**Pull-to-refresh:** added to Chats' thread list and Wallet's balance/actions scroll view (`RefreshControl` wired to each screen's existing query `refetch`s) — a plain addition, nothing to flag.

**Copyable virtual account number:** `BuyCreditModal`'s account number is now `<Text selectable>`, which gives the OS's native long-press "Copy" menu with zero new dependency. Deliberately **not** `expo-clipboard`/`@react-native-clipboard/clipboard` — either would be a new native module, meaning a new EAS _build_, not an OTA update, which this whole batch of work needs to ship as (per the standing no-unprompted-build rule). `selectable` text is the correct call here, not a workaround.

**Auto-detect top-up success:** `topups` added to the `supabase_realtime` publication (new migration `20260913090000_enable_realtime_topups.sql`; `wallets` and `messages` were already on it, `topups` wasn't). New `useTopupStatus(topupId)` hook subscribes to `postgres_changes` on the specific topup row (RLS already scopes it to its owner) with a 5s poll as a websocket-miss safety net. `BuyCreditModal` shows a "Congratulations! ... you can now start chatting ... and also earn from it" screen the moment status flips to `completed`, then a button closes the modal back onto the already-live Wallet balances — no manual "check again" step.

**Peer-to-peer credit transfer — flagged, built on an explicit informed decision, not an oversight.** The user asked for "transfer/share chat credit with another, and convertible to cash for withdraw." `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §1 names this _exact_ pattern — wallet-to-wallet transfer, convertible to cash, unconnected to chat activity — as something that changes InvolveMe's regulatory classification and should not be built without a legal check first; CLAUDE.md's regulatory-posture section backs the same rule. This was surfaced to the user directly, with three options (hold behind a flag and log for legal review / ship a non-cash "gift chat credit only" version / ship the full cash-convertible version live). **The user chose to ship the full version live.** Built accordingly, with the risk logged a second time in `docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §1 and its pre-launch checklist, not just here.

Design choices made to keep this on the app's _existing_ audited rails rather than inventing a second money-movement path (full rationale in `docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md` §9):

- New migration `20260913091500_credit_transfer_between_users.sql`: `credit_transfers` table (own-rows RLS, no client writes — same posture as `topups`/`withdrawals`), two new `pricing_config` keys (`platform_transfer_take_bps` = 2000 default, independently tunable from the escrow-release take rate it mirrors; `credit_transfer_max_credits` = 1000, a blast-radius cap on a brand-new money-moving path, not a product decision), and `fn_transfer_credit` (`SECURITY DEFINER`).
- `fn_transfer_credit` debits the sender's `topup_credit` exactly like a message spend, then credits the recipient's `earnings_pending` and immediately converts it to `withdrawable_cash` — **the exact ledger-entry shape `fn_release_escrow` already uses** for a real chat-earned credit, so the recipient's cash-out inherits the same KYC-gated, name-matched `withdraw` path (CLAUDE.md rule #7) automatically, rather than needing its own.
- Locks every wallet it touches (sender's `topup_credit`, recipient's `earnings_pending` + `withdrawable_cash`, the platform's `platform_revenue_earnings_cut`) in a single fixed ascending-wallet-id order before touching any of them, per `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` §3 — necessary here specifically because, unlike every other wallet function so far, this one locks _two different users'_ wallets in the same call, so a concurrent A→B and B→A transfer could otherwise deadlock by taking the same two rows in opposite order.
- New Edge Function `transfer-credit`: auth → resolves `recipient_phone` the same way `find-user-by-phone` does → calls `fn_transfer_credit` → maps its exceptions to HTTP status (mirrors `withdraw`'s `mapInitiateWithdrawalError` prefix-matching convention). No financial logic in the function itself, per CLAUDE.md rule #1.
- Mobile: `useTopupStatus`/`useTransferCredit` in `lib/queries/wallet.ts`; a new `TransferCreditModal` in Wallet (same phone-lookup-then-act shape as Chats' `NewChatModal`) behind a "Send credit to someone" button.
- **Tests, per CLAUDE.md's wallet-code requirement:** two new cases in `wallet-functions.test.js` (`npm run test:db`) — a split/ledger-conservation check across all four wallets touched, and a concurrency check (two simultaneous transfers draining a sender funded for exactly one, exactly one must win). New `transfer-credit-function.test.js` (`npm run test:functions`) covers the HTTP-layer contract (auth, validation, every mapped error code, one real funded success). **22/22** on `test:db`, **10/10** on the new function test, both against the real linked dev DB — not mocked.
- Deployed: `transfer-credit` (`supabase functions deploy transfer-credit --use-api`, default JWT verification — this is a normal user-authenticated call, not a webhook).

**Not shipped to the phone yet** — pure JS/TS, no new native dependency, so this whole batch (polish + transfer feature) is an `eas update` away once the user asks, per the standing rule.

### Incident: a real ₦100 top-up stuck unconfirmed for 1+ hour — root cause and fix

The user paid real money for chat credit; the payment succeeded on Flutterwave's side, but the credit never landed. Diagnosis, in order:

1. `public.webhook_events_seen` was **completely empty** — not just for this topup, ever. No webhook had ever been received by this project, for any event, since the table was created.
2. Confirmed independently via Flutterwave's own API (`GET /charges?customer_id=...`) that the payment genuinely succeeded (`status: "succeeded"`, real charge id, the user's real name as `originator_name`) — the money was never at risk, Flutterwave just never told us.
3. Manually probing the deployed `webhook-flutterwave` URL directly (not `deno run` locally — every existing test in this suite only ever did that) surfaced the real cause immediately: **HTTP 401 `UNAUTHORIZED_NO_AUTH_HEADER` from Supabase's own platform-level Edge Function gateway**, before this project's code ever ran. `webhook-flutterwave` had been deployed with the platform's default JWT-verification gate enabled — correct for every other function (called by the app with a real user session), wrong for this one specific function, which is server-to-server and will never carry a Supabase auth header. This has been broken since the very first batch deploy of all 5 functions together, several sessions ago — nobody had passed `--no-verify-jwt` for this one function specifically, and nothing in the test suite would ever have caught it, since every test spawns the function locally rather than calling the real deployed URL.
4. Fixed: `supabase functions deploy webhook-flutterwave --use-api --no-verify-jwt`. Confirmed via the Management API (`GET /v1/projects/.../functions/webhook-flutterwave` → `"verify_jwt": false`) that this is a **persisted per-function platform setting** — but the flag still needs to be included on every future deploy of this function to be safe, since its stickiness across a deploy that omits the flag isn't something worth testing by accident.
5. Redeploying past the gateway surfaced a **second, independent bug**: a real 500 crash. `packages/payments/flutterwave.ts`'s `verifyWebhook` used the global `Buffer` with no explicit import — tolerated by a local `deno run`, not tolerated by Supabase's deployed edge-runtime. Fixed with `import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer'`. Confirmed via `grep` that this was the only unguarded-global-`Buffer` instance anywhere in the Deno-side codebase.
6. **The user's money**: fixed directly by calling `fn_confirm_topup` with the real Flutterwave charge id — the exact same idempotent, ledger-safe DB function the webhook would have called, using data independently confirmed from Flutterwave's own API. Not a manual balance edit; fully auditable through the normal ledger (`topup_purchase` ledger entry, `ref_type: topup`, `ref_id` matching the real topup row). Credits landed, topup marked `completed`.
7. Attempted to prove the _full_ real pipeline via Flutterwave's own `POST /resend-webhook` (`{ id: "chg_...", type: "charge.completed" }`, confirmed `200 success` from Flutterwave) — this never actually arrived at `webhook_events_seen` even after both bugs were fixed and directly-probed synthetic requests worked perfectly. Left unresolved and explicitly flagged, not silently assumed fine: either Flutterwave's resend has more latency than observed here, or there's a subtle mismatch in the dashboard-saved webhook URL/secret that only a real future transaction (or the user re-checking the dashboard fields directly) will surface. **Whether a real webhook now lands successfully is unconfirmed — the endpoint is proven healthy, Flutterwave's delivery to it is not.**
8. **New regression test**: `supabase/tests/webhook-flutterwave-deployed-smoke.test.js` (`npm run test:deployed`) — the first and only test in this suite that calls the real deployed URL instead of spawning the function locally. This is a permanent gap-closer: it would have caught both bugs above on day one. Every other test file's 87/87 "passing" said nothing about whether the deployed function actually worked.

**Lesson recorded for future sessions**: passing every local `deno run`-based test proves the _code_ is correct; it proves nothing about whether the _deployed_ function is reachable or configured correctly on the actual platform (JWT gate settings, secret propagation, runtime API differences). Any function this project cares about being reachable by an external, non-app caller (webhooks, in particular) needs at least one test that hits its real URL.

### A real `eas update` bug, found the moment the user tried to log in after the first OTA push

The first `eas update` after OTA was wired up shipped a bundle missing `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` — the app fell back to its `placeholder.supabase.co` default and every network call DNS-failed. Root cause: `eas update --environment <env>` reads from **EAS's own hosted environment-variable store** (`eas env:set`/`eas env:list`), a completely different mechanism from `eas.json`'s `build.<profile>.env` block that `eas build` reads — setting one doesn't populate the other, and nobody had run `eas env:set` yet. Fixed by setting `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` across all three environments (development/preview/production) and re-publishing. **Before running `eas update` again, always confirm `eas env:list --environment <target>` actually shows what the app needs.**

### KYC vendor decision resolved: Prembly, Tier 1 only (not the camera/liveness widget)

The user provided real (live, prefix `live_`) Prembly credentials and initially pasted Prembly's `react-native-identity-kyc` widget integration snippet — a **native module** with camera/liveness capture (Android manifest wants CAMERA/RECORD_AUDIO/VIDEO_CAPTURE), which is Tier 2 per `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md`, more than `withdraw` actually needs (Tier ≥ 1), and would need a new native build (triggering the "ask before building" rule) plus knowing what the widget's dashboard-configured `config_id` actually verifies. Flagged this mismatch back rather than building the heavier path silently; **user chose Option A: plain BVN/NIN number entry, server-side REST verification, no camera, ships via OTA.**

**Confirmed live before building anything** (same discipline as the Flutterwave research): a real BVN Basic call against a documented sample number returned a real `"Record not found"` — proving auth/reachability without needing real PII — plus a critical finding neither doc excerpt showed: **every Prembly verification call is billed (~₦45), success or failure.** Flagged to the user before spending more of their money on speculative test calls; they chose to spend one real ₦45 confirming the actual happy path with their own real BVN. That call also surfaced a second finding: the BVN Basic response **already includes watchlist screening** (`data.watchListed`) — resolves what looked like an open compliance gap (`docs/07-COMPLIANCE-LEGAL.md` §2 requires screening on KYC completion; the docs excerpt read while scoping this didn't show that field, so it looked like it might need a separate call).

**New: `packages/kyc/` (provider.ts + prembly.ts)**, mirroring `packages/payments/`'s interface pattern (CLAUDE.md rule #5's spirit, extended here even though that rule is written about payment providers specifically — `kyc_records.provider` already anticipated more than one vendor). `verifyIdentity()` covers both BVN Basic and NIN Basic, synchronous, no webhook needed.

**New Edge Function `submit-kyc`:** auth → `KycProvider.verifyIdentity()` → hashes the raw BVN/NIN with a server-only pepper (`KYC_HASH_PEPPER`) before it ever touches a row (never raw, per `docs/07` §5) → writes `kyc_records` → bumps `users.kyc_tier` to 1 on success. A watchlist match is blocked (no tier bump) and logged to `fraud_signals`, not surfaced to the user as anything different from a generic failure — standard sanctions-screening practice, per `docs/07` §2's "block or flag, never silently allow."

**A real gap found while scoping the _next_ function, not this one:** `kyc_records` had nowhere to put the verified name, but `link-bank-account` needs it to name-match against a bank account — and it can't be recovered from the (deliberately one-way) `bvn_or_nin_hash`. New migration `20260913080419_kyc_records_verified_name.sql` adds `verified_first_name`/`verified_middle_name`/`verified_last_name` (nullable, only set on a verified record) — a legal name is materially less sensitive than a raw BVN and is exactly the retention this compliance function requires, not scope creep.

**`PaymentProvider` interface extended** with `listBanks()`, `resolveBankAccountName()`, `createTransferRecipient()` — the three Flutterwave calls `link-bank-account` needs, confirmed against live docs (`GET /banks`, `POST /banks/account-resolve`, `POST /transfers/recipients` — the last one already anticipated correctly back in Phase 2 batch 2's schema design, per `PayoutRequest`'s existing comment).

**New Edge Function `link-bank-account`:** the flow that actually populates `bank_accounts.provider_account_id` for a real user — nothing had ever called this before this session; every previous test used a manually-inserted fixture. Resolves the account's registered name via Flutterwave, name-matches it (order-independent token match — Nigerian BVN records and bank names don't agree on name order) against the user's KYC-verified identity, and only on a match creates a real Flutterwave transfer recipient and writes `bank_accounts`. Per CLAUDE.md rule #7, a name mismatch is a **hard rejection**, not a row inserted with `name_match_verified: false` for later review — there's no admin-review flow for that state yet, so "nothing was linked" is the safer failure mode than "something ambiguous was linked." A mismatch also logs to `fraud_signals`.

**A real error-mapping bug caught while writing this function's test, not by review:** the first version mapped every Flutterwave failure (including a plain invalid-account-number rejection, a 4xx) to a 503 "provider unavailable" — telling the user to retry a request that would fail identically every time. Fixed to distinguish 4xx (real input problem → 400 `invalid_account`, don't retry) from a genuine 5xx/network failure (503, retry might help).

**New Edge Function `list-banks`:** thin read-only proxy for the bank picker UI — routed through the provider interface rather than hardcoded, since Flutterwave is the actual source of truth for which `bank_code` values the other two functions will accept.

**Test coverage, with one deliberate asymmetry:** `submit-kyc-function.test.js` (5/5) covers only what's reachable _before_ the paid Prembly call (auth, validation) — baking a real verification attempt into a test that runs on every `npm run test:functions` would mean every future test run spends real money, unlike this project's other live-API tests where the free operations (creating a virtual account, attempting a transfer to a fake recipient) cost nothing. The happy path was confirmed manually, once, with the user's own real BVN and explicit go-ahead on the cost — not repeated in CI. `link-bank-account-function.test.js` (5/5) has no such constraint (Flutterwave doesn't charge per account-resolve/recipient attempt) and does hit the live API for a real invalid-account rejection — but still can't test the full success path automatically, since there's no known real account number that resolves against these live credentials without using someone's actual bank details (the docs' own example account returns a real `INVALID_ACCOUNT` live) — same class of gap as `withdraw-function.test.js` never completing a real payout.

**Also fixed in passing:** `packages/payments/index.ts` (the `@involveme/payments` workspace package's barrel export) had drifted stale since the `PaymentProviderError` rename in the earlier Flutterwave section — nothing imports from it today (every Edge Function imports `flutterwave.ts` directly), but it's a real workspace package (`main`/`types` both point at it), so left broken it would silently mislead the next thing that does try to import from it. Brought back in sync with current exports.

**Deployed:** `submit-kyc`, `link-bank-account`, `list-banks`, with `PREMBLY_SECRET_KEY`/`PREMBLY_PUBLIC_KEY`/`KYC_HASH_PEPPER` set via `supabase secrets set`. Full `npm run test:functions` as one uninterrupted chain: **87/87** across all eight function tests, no regressions.

**Not built:** the mobile UI for KYC submission (Settings) and bank-account linking (Wallet) — the backend is complete and tested, same "backend first, UI as fast-follow" pattern as the chat/wallet/status work earlier this session. See "Immediate next step."

### Two real bugs, found only by using the app on a real phone

1. **Phone auth was disabled at the project level** (`external_phone_enabled: false`, `sms_provider: twilio` with every credential field `null`) — nobody had caught this because every prior test created users via the Admin API with `phone_confirm: true`, never exercising real `signInWithOtp`. Fixed for now via Supabase's `sms_test_otp` config (Management API), mapped to the user's own number with a fixed code — unblocks testing today, but **still needs a real SMS provider before any other user can ever log in.** The user will set this up themselves via **Termii** later, "toward the ending phase" — not Twilio; whatever gets wired in will likely go through Supabase's custom `hook_send_sms` mechanism rather than a native provider dropdown entry, since Termii isn't one of Supabase's built-in options.
2. **The phone-entry screen never normalized to E.164.** Fixed with `apps/mobile/lib/phone.ts`'s `toE164NigerianPhone()`.

### Mobile UI wired to the real backend for the first time (`feat/mobile-chat-wallet-status-ui`)

`(tabs)/chats.tsx`, `(tabs)/wallet.tsx`, `(tabs)/status.tsx`, and `thread/[id].tsx` were Phase-0 placeholders (see the earlier mobile-build section above) — this is the natural next step that section flagged, scoped and built without further back-and-forth per the user's "scope the natural next" instruction.

- **New Edge Function: `find-user-by-phone`.** A real gap, not anticipated by any prior phase: `send-message` takes a `recipient_id` (uuid), but nothing anywhere resolved a phone number to one, and RLS correctly blocks looking up an arbitrary stranger's row directly. Narrow, minimal-field lookup (`id`/`display_name`/`avatar_url` only), auth-gated, no financial logic. Test: 5/5 passing.
- **A second real bug, caught by this function's own first test run:** `users.phone` is stored **without** a leading `+` (Supabase Auth strips it before the trigger-copied value ever lands in `public.users`) — confirmed by creating a real user and inspecting the row. `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` had said plain "E.164," which technically includes the `+`; corrected there and in the function, which now strips the client's `+` before comparing.
- **New migration: `supabase_realtime` publication was never populated for any table**, confirmed by querying `pg_publication_tables` directly and getting zero rows — every `postgres_changes` subscription anyone ever wrote would have connected successfully and silently never received an event. Added `messages` and `wallets` (the two tables the new UI subscribes to) — safe with existing RLS, since Realtime enforces each table's own SELECT policies per-client.
- **New `lib/` layer:** `edgeFunctions.ts` (shared authenticated-fetch helper — every money/auth-affecting call goes through this, not a bespoke `fetch` per screen), `lib/queries/{threads,messages,wallet,status,findUserByPhone}.ts` (TanStack Query hooks, realtime-backed where docs/05 §3 specifies it).
- **Chats:** real thread list (two-query join against `users` for partner info, not a single embedded select — avoids depending on exact FK constraint names for the two-FKs-to-the-same-table disambiguation PostgREST embedding would need), a "new chat" flow (phone lookup → first message via `send-message`, which creates the thread), and a real thread screen (live messages, escrow-status badges, composer).
- **Wallet:** live balances (`topup_credit`/`earnings_pending`/`withdrawable_cash`), a real buy-credit flow (shows the actual bank-transfer virtual account from the live Flutterwave call), and a real withdraw flow — gated on whether the user actually has a verified, provider-linked `bank_accounts` row (none can, for real, until bank-account linking is built — see "Immediate next step"), so the UI shows an honest "not set up yet" state instead of a form that could only ever fail.
- **Status:** text-only composer (no media upload UI — needs Supabase Storage wiring, out of scope here) + a list of the user's own posts.
- **Not implemented, deliberately:** Presence/typing indicators and broadcast read-receipts (docs/05 §3 lists these; this pass only did the `postgres_changes` message/wallet subscriptions the core flows need). No contacts-sync/phone-book matching — "start a chat" requires knowing the other person's exact phone number, one at a time.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` and `expo lint` clean. Full `npm run test:functions` reran clean as one uninterrupted chain: **65/65** across all six function tests (send-message, withdraw, webhook-flutterwave, post-status, buy-credit, find-user-by-phone), confirming the new function and migration didn't regress anything already built. New Edge Function deployed live (`supabase functions deploy find-user-by-phone --use-api`) — this doesn't touch EAS build quota, a separate system from the mobile build the user asked to be asked about. **The mobile UI changes themselves have not been shipped to the user's phone yet** — per the standing rule above, that's an explicit ask (an `eas update`, not a build — see the OTA section above), not something to just do.

**The Flutterwave API-generation question from sessions 1/2 is resolved — no more waiting on a vendor conversation.** The user provided real Flutterwave v4 credentials this session (client_id/client_secret/encryption key — confirmed **live/production**, not sandbox) and explicitly approved testing with small real amounts. Rather than build against memory/stale docs again, every endpoint below was either fetched from developer.flutterwave.com directly this session or **actually called live** before being trusted. That live-testing discipline caught two real, would-have-shipped-broken bugs (see below) that reading docs alone did not.

**PR #11 (Phase 2 batch 3, `post-status`) merged to `main` this session** — see the session-2 section below for what it contains. Also fixed while merging: **CI has been red on every merge since PR #6** (`turbo` 2.x needs a `packageManager` field in `package.json` to resolve the workspace; it was missing). Added it — first green CI run in this project's history.

### Flutterwave v4 — what's real now (not a separate PR yet — see "Immediate next step")

**Credentials, cleaned up:** `.env`'s `FLW_PUBLIC_KEY`/`FLW_SECRET_KEY` (v3-era names, holding v4 values) renamed to `FLW_CLIENT_ID`/`FLW_CLIENT_SECRET`. Added `FLW_ENVIRONMENT=production` (confirmed by calling the API — the sandbox host `developersandbox-api.flutterwave.com` rejects these credentials with 403, while `f4bexperience.flutterwave.com` returns real merchant data), a generated `FLW_WEBHOOK_SECRET_HASH` (the user still needs to enter this same value in the Flutterwave dashboard — see "Immediate next step"), and `FLW_TRANSFER_SENDER_ID=sdr_uojUD2jctp` (a one-time, non-monetary `/transfers/senders` registration for "InvolveMe" done live this session — required on every payout). `.env.example` updated to match.

**Migration `20260912171059_users_provider_customer_id.sql`:** adds `users.provider_customer_id` (nullable) — buy-credit needs a Flutterwave Customer object per user; this caches it so a returning user's top-up reuses one instead of creating a duplicate. Applied to the dev DB.

**`packages/payments/flutterwave.ts` — rewritten, not patched:**

- OAuth2 `client_credentials` token exchange (`idp.flutterwave.com`), cached at module scope, refreshed a minute before the documented 10-minute expiry.
- `flwFetch` helper with retry (network errors and 502/504/503 only, never 4xx) — the production host was observed to be genuinely flaky during this session's own testing (intermittent 503s, `ECONNRESET`, connect timeouts, recovering on retry every time); baked the retry in rather than treating it as one-off bad luck.
- **`initiatePayout` — real, confirmed live.** Calls `/transfers` with a pre-created recipient + this project's own sender. Tested against the real API with a deliberately-fake `recipientId` (`'rcb_test_1'`, the existing test fixture) and got back a genuine `RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND` (404) — proof the whole request shape, auth, and sender registration are correct end-to-end, not just that the code compiles.
- **`initiateCollection` — real, confirmed live, redesigned mid-session after the first design turned out wrong in practice.** Original plan (per the docs read first): Customer → PaymentMethod (`type: 'bank_transfer'`) → Charge. **Live-tested and rejected**: Flutterwave's real API doesn't accept `'bank_transfer'` as a payment-method type (`400 Invalid value 'bank_transfer' for PaymentMethodIn`); `'bank_account'` is accepted but returns an empty `bank_account` object — no usable virtual account number, under any field combination tried. Found instead, by testing directly: **`POST /virtual-accounts`** with `account_type: 'dynamic'` does the whole job in one call — a real account number, immediately, and (contrary to what the docs for this specific endpoint say) does **not** require BVN/NIN for a dynamic (one-off) account, only for a reusable `static` one. Switched to this. Verified with a real ₦100 top-up: real account number, real bank name, real expiry, all returned and correctly wired into the response.
- **A second real bug, caught by the first live test run, not by reading code:** the original design created the Flutterwave Customer _inside_ `initiateCollection`, in the same try/catch as the rest of the flow. When the payment-method step failed (the bug above), the customer had _already_ been created at Flutterwave but its id was never persisted to `users.provider_customer_id` — so the retry tried to create a second customer with the same stable synthetic email and got a real `409 CUSTOMER_ALREADY_EXISTS`, with no way to recover the first id (v4 has no get-customer-by-email lookup). Fixed by splitting customer resolution into its own `PaymentProvider.resolveCustomerId()` method that the Edge Function calls and persists **before** ever attempting a collection — removes the failure mode instead of working around its symptom. `CollectionRequest` now takes a required `providerCustomerId`, not an email/phone the adapter resolves internally.
- **`verifyWebhook` — a real, confirmed bug fixed, not just refactored.** The previous version used `.digest('hex')`. Flutterwave's own webhook-docs verification code sample uses `.digest('base64')` — hex would have rejected every single real webhook Flutterwave ever sent. Caught by reading that sample directly this session, not assumed to still match a v3-era convention. Existing test's `sign()` helper updated to match.
- **Webhook event names corrected:** `transfer.completed`/`transfer.failed` (the session-2 guess, never confirmed against real v4 docs) were wrong. The real names, per developer.flutterwave.com's own API-overview reference, are **`transfer.disburse`** and **`transfer.reversal`**. `webhook-flutterwave/index.ts` and its test updated. Also added a `data.status === 'succeeded'` check on `charge.completed` — that event fires for every terminal charge state (succeeded/failed/voided), not just success, per the charges endpoint's own documented status enum; a non-succeeded `charge.completed` now marks the topup `failed` directly instead of being silently treated as a confirmation.

**`supabase/functions/buy-credit/index.ts` — new, built and verified against the real live API, not stubbed.** Auth → resolve-or-create + persist the Flutterwave customer id (its own step, per the bug above) → `fn_buy_credit` (fee/credit math, already existed) → `initiateCollection` → returns real bank-transfer instructions. On provider failure, marks the topup `failed` and returns 503 — same compensating-path discipline as `withdraw`. `docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md` updated with the real (not aspirational) contract.

**Test coverage — `supabase/tests/buy-credit-function.test.js`, new, 11/11 passing, wired into `npm run test:functions`.** This is the first test in the suite that calls the real Flutterwave API for collections: every run creates a real Customer and a real ₦100 dynamic virtual account (harmless — nothing here completes an actual bank transfer, so every topup created stays `pending` forever, same as an abandoned real checkout). Covers the fee/credit math, a real account number coming back, `provider_ref`/`provider_customer_id` persistence, and — critically — that a **second** top-up for the same user reuses the cached customer id rather than hitting the 409 bug above. `withdraw-function.test.js` and `webhook-flutterwave-function.test.js` re-run clean after all the above changes: 12/12 and 17/17 respectively (the withdraw test's "provider fails" path now fails for a real reason — a real `RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND` — instead of a `PaymentProviderNotImplementedError` stub, and its header comment was updated to say so). `post-status-function.test.js` (12/12) and `send-message-function.test.js` (20/20) confirmed unaffected. A combined `npm run test:functions` run hit this session's general network flakiness partway through (see below) — every file has been confirmed passing individually; not re-verified as one uninterrupted chained run.

**Not done, deliberately, still:**

- **Bank-account linking** — the flow that would populate a real `bank_accounts.provider_account_id` via `/transfers/recipients` for an actual user. Still just test fixtures. This is now the actual remaining gap on the payout side (the provider call itself is confirmed correct) — see "Immediate next step."
- **The webhook secret hash isn't registered with Flutterwave yet** — a value is generated and in `.env`, but the user still needs to enter it in the dashboard for real webhooks to ever arrive signed with it. See "Immediate next step."
- **No real bank transfer or real payout has actually completed** — both need a human to actually move money (or wait for one to), not something to trigger from here. The exact webhook payload a real completed virtual-account transfer produces is assumed (`charge.completed`, per Flutterwave's own framing of it as the universal collection-completion event) but not yet seen for real.
- Card/other payment methods for buy-credit — out of scope, NGN bank transfer only, matching this project's phone-only, KYC-light v1 posture.

### Two real auth bugs found the moment the user actually tried logging in — neither would have been caught by code review alone

The user installed the preview build and got "unsupported phone provider" entering their number. Two separate, real problems, both found by using the real app on a real phone rather than by reading the auth screens:

1. **Phone auth was never actually turned on for this Supabase project.** `external_phone_enabled` was `false`, and `sms_provider` was set to `twilio` with every Twilio credential field `null` — so even if enabled, no real SMS could ever have sent. Nobody had caught this before because every previous session's testing created users via the Admin API with `phone_confirm: true`, which never exercises the real `signInWithOtp` path at all. Fixed for now via Supabase's built-in test-OTP mechanism (`sms_test_otp` config, set via the Management API) mapped to the user's own number with a fixed code — unblocks testing today with zero real SMS provider needed, but this only ever works for that one hardcoded number. **A real SMS provider (Twilio, matching the project's existing `sms_provider` selection, or MessageBird/Vonage/TextLocal) still needs to be provisioned before any other real user can ever receive an OTP** — flagged in "Immediate next step."
2. **The phone-entry screen never normalized the number to E.164.** The user typed the everyday local format (`0902...`); Supabase's phone auth requires `+234902...` and rejects anything else. Nothing in `(auth)/index.tsx` converted it — the placeholder text (`+234 800 000 0000`) hinted at the right format but didn't enforce it. Fixed with a small `apps/mobile/lib/phone.ts` helper (`toE164NigerianPhone`, deliberately Nigeria-only, not a full libphonenumber integration — matches this NGN-only app's "stay lite" posture) used both when calling `signInWithOtp` and when storing the pending phone for the verify screen's later `verifyOtp` call. **Needs a new EAS build to reach the user's phone** — the already-installed APK doesn't have this fix; told the user to type the `+234...` form directly as an immediate workaround on the existing install.

### Phase 2 batch 3 — `post-status` (migration `20260912152602_fn_post_status.sql`, PR #11)

- **`fn_post_status(p_user_id, p_media_url, p_caption)`** — new `SECURITY DEFINER` function, same shape as `fn_buy_credit` (single-wallet debit, no escrow/earning per docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md §7). Locks the caller's `topup_credit` wallet `FOR UPDATE`, checks `is_frozen` (per the guard-frozen-wallets migration's "freeze everywhere" rule), charges `status_upload_credits_media` if `media_url` is present else `status_upload_credits_text` (both already seeded in `pricing_config`), inserts the `status_updates` row with `expires_at = now() + 24h`, and records the debit as a `ledger_entries` row with `reason = 'status_upload_debit'` / `ref_type = 'status_update'` — both values already existed in the item-1 CHECK constraints (foresight from that migration), so no constraint changes were needed here. Locked to `service_role` via the same revoke/grant pattern as every other function.
- **`supabase/functions/post-status/index.ts`** — authenticates, forwards to `fn_post_status`. No financial logic in the Edge Function itself, per CLAUDE.md rule #1. Error mapping: `empty_status`/`invalid_request` → 400, `insufficient_credit` → 402 (same structured `credits_required`/`credits_available` shape as `send-message`, for a consistent client contract across both credit-spending endpoints), `wallet_frozen` → 403.
- **`docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md` updated** with the actual response shape and error mapping — the previous version only documented the request body, no response/errors, since the function didn't exist yet.
- **Verified against the real dev database, through the actual HTTP function** (`deno run`, same workaround as every other function this repo tests — still no Docker on this machine): a funded test user posts a text-only status (charged `status_upload_credits_text`) then a media status (charged `status_upload_credits_media`), proving the function actually branches on `media_url` rather than charging one flat rate; `expires_at` lands at exactly `created_at + 24h` on both rows; ledger conservation holds on the wallet touched. Plus the full error-mapping chain: 401 (missing auth), 400 (`empty_status` on neither field / whitespace-only, `invalid_request` on a non-string `caption`), 402 (`insufficient_credit` with structured amounts), 403 (`wallet_frozen`). 12/12 assertions passing. Full `npm run test:functions` reran clean afterward (61/61 across all four function tests, no regressions) and `npm run test:db` reran clean too (11/11).
- Test file: `supabase/tests/post-status-function.test.js`, wired into `npm run test:functions`. `deno check` and `prettier --check` clean.

**Operational notes from this session, not code issues:**

- The dev pooler dropped/timed-out multiple times while running tests this session (`Connection timed out` on `supabase db push`, `HeadersTimeoutError` on the Admin API during a test run, `EAUTHTIMEOUT` on a `test:db` run) — same documented flakiness as last session, always recovered on retry, never a partial/incorrect result once a run actually completed.
- **A run that dies mid-test before its own cleanup runs leaves stranded rows** — hit this three times this session (once from the `HeadersTimeoutError`, once from an `EAUTHTIMEOUT` mid-`test:db`, and 2 of 4 users stranded after an otherwise-fully-passing `test:db` run, likely a swallowed connection error during that suite's own multi-user cleanup loop — `wallet-functions.test.js` isn't new code from this session, not investigated further). Caught by directly querying `auth.users`/`status_updates` for recently-created rows after every run rather than trusting a clean exit code alone (same discipline prior sessions used), and manually cleaned up each time via the same disable-trigger/delete-ledger-entries/delete-dependent-rows sequence the test files themselves use. Confirmed zero stray rows before finishing. Worth knowing: a clean exit code on this suite is necessary but not sufficient proof of a clean run when the pooler is being flaky — always verify directly.

**Phase 1 is complete and merged.** The core money path works end-to-end against the real dev database, including its safety nets, and has a committed, re-runnable test suite proving the locking actually holds under genuine concurrent load — not just reasoned about.

### Phase 2 batch 1 — `send-message` Edge Function (merged, PR #8)

Built: `supabase/functions/_shared/auth.ts` (JWT verification helper — every Edge Function that touches money uses this to re-derive the caller's identity server-side, per CLAUDE.md rule #1, rather than trusting a body-supplied user id) and `supabase/functions/send-message/index.ts`.

**Contract decisions made while building (both applied to `docs/05-API-REALTIME-SPEC.md`, not left to drift):**

1. Request accepts `{ thread_id?, recipient_id?, body }` as the handoff doc recommended — missing `thread_id` validates and calls `fn_start_thread` with the caller as participant_a/payer.
2. **Response now also returns `thread_id`.** The original spec's 200 response never included it — with no way to learn the thread you just started, a client could open a conversation but never continue it. Found while writing the test's reply step, not anticipated going in.
3. **A reply must use the returned `thread_id`, not `recipient_id`.** `fn_start_thread(payer, payee)` looks up `(participant_a, participant_b)` as an _ordered_ pair — the payee replying with `recipient_id` set to the payer would look up (and, finding none, create) a second, reversed-role thread instead of continuing the first. Documented in docs/05 so this doesn't get rediscovered the hard way by a client implementation.

**Verified against the real dev database, through the actual HTTP function** (not by calling `fn_send_message` directly, matching the handoff's exit criteria): two Admin-API-created test users, real minted JWTs (HS256, signed with `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET`, no external JWT library), a full paid exchange — A opens a thread with a 30-word message (2 credits), B replies with 60 words (4 more credits, still debited from A — B never pays, only earns), both escrows release in the same call, B's earnings convert immediately to `withdrawable_cash` (5000 kobo), platform earns its cut (1 credit), ledger conservation holds on every wallet touched. Plus the full documented error mapping: 401 (missing/invalid auth), 400 (empty body, neither `thread_id` nor `recipient_id`, self-threading, 501-word cap), 404 (`recipient_not_found`), 402 (`insufficient_credit` with the structured `credits_required`/`credits_available` shape). 20/20 assertions passing. All test data cleaned up afterward, confirmed zero rows left.

**Environment gap found this session, worked around:** `supabase functions serve` (the handoff's documented local-test path) shells out to Docker for its local edge-runtime gateway, and this dev machine has neither Docker nor Podman installed. Asked the user how to proceed rather than guessing; chose installing a standalone Deno CLI binary and running the function directly via `deno run -A supabase/functions/send-message/index.ts` (`Deno.serve` is native Deno, not a Supabase CLI wrapper) with `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` set to the real linked dev project — same env vars the CLI's gateway would inject. `_shared/auth.ts` does its own `auth.getUser()` check regardless of which gateway is in front of it, so this isn't a verification gap, just a different way of reaching the same code path. The Deno binary download itself was badly throttled on this network (~15KB/s from GitHub's releases CDN, repeated resets; ~5x faster from `dl.deno.land` directly) — worth knowing if a future session hits the same thing. Documented in `supabase/tests/README.md`; swap back to `supabase functions serve` once Docker is available, the test's spawn call is the only thing that changes.

**A real bug in the test harness itself, caught before it caught anything in the function:** the first version of `deleteTestUser` in the new test file used the Admin API's `DELETE /admin/users/:id` for cleanup. That works for an _unfunded_ test user, but any wallet with `ledger_entries` against it can't cascade-delete through `auth.users` — `ledger_entries` is deliberately append-only (a trigger blocks deletes, per CLAUDE.md rule #4), and the Admin API's delete hits that FK violation and returns 500 without surfacing it anywhere the test would notice, leaving two funded test users stranded. Caught by directly querying the DB for recently-created rows after a run rather than trusting a clean exit code. Fixed by switching `deleteTestUser` to the same disable-trigger / delete-ledger-entries / re-enable-trigger / delete-dependent-rows sequence `wallet-functions.test.js` already established, rather than reinventing it. Same run also silently hung for two full minutes after every assertion had already logged and passed, with no summary line and no error — root-caused to something not draining Node's event loop after cleanup (most likely the killed `deno` child process's stdio pipes); fixed with an explicit `process.exit()` at the end of `main()` rather than relying on natural event-loop drain. Both fixes verified by rerunning to a clean, fast exit with the same 20/20 result.

**Not done, by original scope, not an oversight:** `buy-credit`, `webhook-flutterwave`, `withdraw` (batch 2 — blocked on Flutterwave credentials), `post-status` (needs a new DB function first), `kyc-callback` (no KYC vendor chosen yet), and actually deploying anything (`supabase functions deploy`) — local testing only so far, deploy was explicitly scoped as a later step once more of the batch is verified.

### Phase 2 batch 2 — `withdraw` + `webhook-flutterwave` (merged, PR #9), `buy-credit` deferred

**The batch 2 plan changed mid-session, for a real reason, not a whim.** The original plan (see "Immediate next step" below, left as historical reference) assumed Flutterwave's API just needed live credentials to exercise for real. While building, Flutterwave's _current_ live docs (developer.flutterwave.com) turned out to describe a materially different API than this project's `.env`/docs assumed — not just new credentials, a different generation:

- **Auth:** OAuth2 `client_id`/`client_secret` token exchange against a separate IDP host, not `FLW_SECRET_KEY` as a static Bearer token.
- **Payouts:** pay by `recipient_id` (create a recipient via `POST /transfers/recipients`, then reference its `id` on the transfer) — this part actually **validated** this project's own schema design (`bank_accounts.provider_account_id`, docs/02's "tokenized by provider" comment), confirmed via live docs, not guessed.
- **Collections:** no single "give me a checkout URL" call anymore — needs separate `Customer` → `PaymentMethod` → `Charge`/`Order` objects, and card payment methods need client-side tokenization before the server ever sees a request (bigger than an Edge Function; touches the mobile app).
- **Webhooks:** `flutterwave-signature` header, HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body — not the `verif-hash` direct-compare header this project's docs originally described.

Flagged back to the user rather than guessing at an unfamiliar, evolving vendor API (with real compliance/security weight — CLAUDE.md's "hold behind a flag" guidance for payout paths). **Decision: build the DB layer and Edge Functions now with the actual Flutterwave HTTP calls stubbed**, deferring `buy-credit` entirely and the _provider_ half of `withdraw`/`webhook-flutterwave` until the API-generation question gets a real answer.

**What's actually built and verified against the real dev database:**

- **Migration** (`20260912123949_webhook_events_and_withdrawal_completion.sql`): `webhook_events_seen` table (idempotency claim for inbound webhooks, per docs/06 §9 and CLAUDE.md rule #6) and `fn_complete_withdrawal` (a new `SECURITY DEFINER` function — pure status transition to `paid` once a transfer webhook confirms, idempotent the same way `fn_confirm_topup` is; not a balance mutation, since `fn_initiate_withdrawal` already debited up front).
- **`packages/payments/provider.ts`:** `PayoutRequest` changed from `accountNumber`/`bankCode` to `recipientId` — the original Phase-0 interface never matched `bank_accounts`' own tokenization design (nothing had implemented against it yet, so fixed rather than left to drift).
- **`packages/payments/flutterwave.ts`:** `verifyWebhook` is real (HMAC-SHA256, constant-time compare) — self-contained crypto, doesn't depend on which API generation ends up handling collections/payouts. `initiateCollection`/`initiatePayout` throw `PaymentProviderNotImplementedError` — deliberate, documented, not silently faked.
- **`supabase/functions/withdraw/index.ts`:** auth → validates the bank account is verified _and_ provider-linked (checked before ever debiting, avoiding a pointless debit-then-reverse round trip) → `fn_initiate_withdrawal` → `initiatePayout()` → since that always throws right now, this always reaches `fn_fail_withdrawal` and returns 503 — meaning **every real test run exercises the compensating-transaction path for real**, not just proves it compiles. 12/12 assertions passing: the reversal itself (balance restored exactly, withdrawal marked `failed`, ledger reconciles) plus the full error-mapping chain (auth, validation, unverified/unlinked bank account, `kyc_required`, `below_minimum`).
- **`supabase/functions/webhook-flutterwave/index.ts`:** signature verification → idempotency claim → routes `charge.completed` → `fn_confirm_topup`, `transfer.completed`/`transfer.failed` → `fn_complete_withdrawal`/`fn_fail_withdrawal`. 17/17 assertions passing: a real topup fixture (via `fn_buy_credit`, which isn't deferred — only its Edge Function wrapper is) confirmed end-to-end through a real signed webhook, replay-safety (identical event id doesn't double-credit), three distinct signature-rejection cases (missing, garbage, valid-signature-for-a-different-body), and both transfer outcomes against real withdrawal fixtures.
- All test data cleaned up afterward, confirmed zero rows left — same standard as every prior PR. `deno check` and `prettier --check` clean on every new/changed file.

**A second instance of the same test-hang bug Batch 1 found, this time in the pre-existing `wallet-functions.test.js`:** while re-running it to confirm no regressions from the new migration, it hung for several minutes after all 11 assertions had already logged and passed, no summary line, no error — identical symptom to the bug Batch 1 fixed in `send-message-function.test.js`, just never hit there before because nothing had re-run that specific file end-to-end in a while. Same fix applied (`process.exit()` at the end of `main()`), verified by rerunning to a clean, fast exit with the same 11/11 result. Not a regression from anything in this PR, just the first time this particular file's version of the bug got noticed.

**Not done, deliberately:**

- **`buy-credit`** — deferred pending the Flutterwave API-generation decision above. `fn_buy_credit` (the DB function) already exists and works; only the Edge Function wrapper that would call Flutterwave to create the actual charge is missing.
- **The real Flutterwave HTTP calls** in `initiateCollection`/`initiatePayout` — stubbed, same reason.
- **Bank-account _linking_** (the flow that would populate `bank_accounts.provider_account_id` for a real user) — not built, not scoped anywhere yet; every test here uses a fixture row inserted directly.
- **Deploying anything** — local testing only, same as batch 1.

### Phase 1 item 6 — what was verified, not just written

A **permanent, committed** test suite (`supabase/tests/wallet-functions.test.js`, run via `npm run test:db`), not a scratch script — it stays in the repo for the next person (or the next database change) to re-run. Fires genuinely concurrent requests from separate physical connections (a single `pg.Client` processes queries serially even unawaited; real concurrency needs real separate connections) against the real dev database:

- Two simultaneous `fn_send_message` calls against a wallet funded for exactly one message → exactly one succeeds, the other gets `insufficient_credit`, balance never goes negative, ledger reconciles
- Two simultaneous `fn_confirm_topup` calls for the same topup (a racing duplicate webhook, not a sequential retry) → credits issued exactly once, not twice
- Two simultaneous `fn_initiate_withdrawal` calls against a wallet funded for exactly one withdrawal → exactly one succeeds
- Two independent concurrent conversations (4 users, 4 connections, 4 simultaneous sends) → every wallet touched, including both platform wallets, reconciles against its own ledger sum afterward

11/11 passing. All test data cleaned up automatically after each run — confirmed zero rows left anywhere.

### Bugs this test suite caught in itself before it caught anything in the functions

Worth recording because they're exactly the kind of test-infrastructure mistake that quietly invalidates a test suite's results if not caught:

1. **Cross-user cleanup ordering.** A thread's messages can have `sender_id` pointing at _either_ participant, so tearing down "everything belonging to user X" one user at a time left the other participant's messages dangling and blocked the thread delete with an FK violation. Fixed by adding a `deleteTestThread` helper that tears down a thread as a unit, by thread id, before either participant is deleted.
2. **Platform wallet cleanup drift.** `resetPlatformWallets` zeroed `wallets.balance` directly without deleting the corresponding `ledger_entries` — exactly the bug class `fn_run_reconciliation_check` exists to catch — which leaked a stale mismatch into whichever test ran next. Fixed by deleting the ledger rows too, not just the cached balance.
3. **Deterministic phone numbers.** Test users originally used a fixed counter (`+234000100005`, etc.) for their phone number. A run that crashed before cleanup (which happened twice, from bugs 1 and 2 above) left those numbers taken, so the next run's `INSERT` failed on `users_phone_key` before the actual test logic even ran. Fixed by randomizing the phone number per run, same as the user ID already was.

None of these were bugs in the database functions themselves — the functions passed every time once the test harness itself was correct. Worth noting: **this suite is intentionally not wired into CI** (it runs real inserts/deletes against whatever `SUPABASE_DB_URL` points at, which should only ever be a dev/staging project) — it's a manual pre-merge check for now, run via `npm run test:db`.

**Operational note, not a code issue:** the dev pooler (`aws-1-eu-west-1.pooler.supabase.com`) dropped connections mid-session and once timed out on auth entirely across several runs while building this suite — always recoverable on retry, never a partial/incorrect result. Added a `client.on('error', ...)` handler so a drop fails the affected test cleanly instead of crashing the whole process with an unhandled exception. Worth keeping an eye on once real traffic exists; not urgent now.

### Phase 1 item 5 — what was verified, not just written

Ran against the real dev database (fresh test users each time, cleaned up afterward, confirmed zero rows left):

- `pg_cron` enabled successfully on the dev project; all three jobs registered and confirmed via `cron.job` with the right schedules: `escrow-expiry-sweep` (`*/15 * * * *`), `auto-withdraw-sweep` (`0 * * * *`), `reconciliation-check` (`5 * * * *`)
- `fn_run_reconciliation_check`: a deliberately corrupted wallet (balance set directly, bypassing the ledger — the exact bug class this exists to catch) is detected, frozen, and logged to `fraud_signals` with severity `high`; a clean wallet reports zero mismatches
- `fn_run_auto_withdraw_sweep`: a below-minimum balance is correctly left alone before the 7-day force window, then correctly swept once past it; a wallet with no verified bank account is excluded entirely (held, not force-paid); a mid-sweep failure (KYC dropped after aging) is caught, logged to `fraud_signals`, and doesn't crash the sweep for other wallets
- **Frozen-wallet enforcement, added this session after noticing it didn't exist:** `fn_send_message` now rejects a frozen payer wallet, `fn_release_escrow` now rejects a frozen payee wallet (and the whole `fn_send_message` call rolls back — the payer isn't charged for a reply that fails to release), `fn_confirm_topup` and `fn_initiate_withdrawal` reject frozen wallets too; `fn_refund_expired_escrows` skips (not aborts) a frozen payer's escrow so the bulk sweep still processes everyone else

### The gap that made item 5 necessary to extend beyond its original scope

`fn_run_reconciliation_check` sets `wallets.is_frozen` on a mismatch, but nothing anywhere actually _checked_ that flag — freezing a wallet did nothing to stop further activity on it, which defeats the point per `docs/02-DATA-MODEL.md` §4. Added `is_frozen` guards to every wallet lock in every money-moving function (`20260912082046_guard_frozen_wallets.sql`, `CREATE OR REPLACE` — no function signatures changed, so grants/permissions carried over automatically). Not something the roadmap called out as its own item; found while writing this doc's summary of item 5, on the theory that it belongs with the reconciliation check rather than as a separate future fix.

### Two things intentionally left as documented gaps in item 5 (not silent, not blocking)

- **"Notify" half of "hold and notify" isn't implemented.** A user without a verified bank account is correctly excluded from the auto-sweep, but nothing pushes them a reminder to add one — that needs an Edge Function + push service that doesn't exist yet.
- **Reconciliation "pages on-call" isn't implemented either** — a mismatch freezes the wallet and logs a high-severity `fraud_signals` row, which is the detectable/actionable part, but nothing pages anyone; that needs an external alerting integration.
- **The auto-sweep's "how long has this sat unwithdrawn" check uses `wallets.updated_at` as a proxy**, which is exact only when withdrawals always take the full balance (which `fn_initiate_withdrawal` defaults to). A partial withdrawal would reset the clock on older money still in the same wallet. Documented in the migration; exact per-credit aging would need dedicated tracking this migration doesn't add.

### Phase 1 items 2+3 — what was verified, not just written

Ran against the real dev database with two real test users (created via the Admin API, then fully cleaned up in FK-safe order — confirmed zero rows left in any table afterward, platform wallets reset to 0):

- `fn_buy_credit` on a ₦1,000 top-up computes exactly ₦20 fee / 98 credits; `fn_confirm_topup` issues them and is a no-op on a retried/duplicate call (webhook-retry safe)
- `fn_start_thread` is find-or-create, not create-every-time
- A (payer) sends a 30-word message → 2 credits charged, escrowed, **not** yet earned by B
- B (payee) replies with a 60-word message → 4 more credits charged to A, **and** releases both the earlier escrow and this new one in the same call (per docs/03 §5's "up to and including this exchange") — A ends up debited 6 credits total, B ends up with **5000 kobo in `withdrawable_cash`**, platform earns **1 credit**, `earnings_pending` nets back to zero (auto-converted immediately, matching the doc's worked-example rounding exactly: 2×80%→2/0, 4×80%→3/1)
- Insufficient-balance and >500-word messages are both rejected with the expected error codes
- `fn_initiate_withdrawal` debits `withdrawable_cash` immediately; `fn_fail_withdrawal` correctly reverses that debit and marks the withdrawal `failed` (the compensating path for when a provider transfer call fails after the DB already committed)
- Withdrawal below the ₦500 minimum is rejected without the bypass flag; a KYC-tier-0 user is rejected outright
- Anon key still gets zero rows / denied writes on every table (RLS posture unchanged by adding read policies for `authenticated`)

### Two real bugs found and fixed during this build (not hypothetical — both would have shipped broken otherwise)

1. **`make_interval(hours => v_refund_hours)` failed at runtime** — `pricing_config.value` is `bigint`, `make_interval` wants `integer`, and Postgres won't implicitly narrow in a named-argument function call. Fixed with an explicit cast. Caught by actually calling the function, not by the migration applying cleanly (DDL applies fine; the bug only shows up when the function body executes).
2. **Earnings never actually became withdrawable.** The original function only credited `earnings_pending` at escrow release and stopped there — nothing converted it to `withdrawable_cash`, contradicting docs/03-ECONOMY-LEDGER.md §6's "automatically and immediately on release." Fixed by adding the conversion as two more ledger legs in the same release transaction (debit `earnings_pending`, credit `withdrawable_cash`, both reason `earnings_conversion` — which is exactly why that reason already existed in the item-1 CHECK constraint enum).

### One schema correction made mid-session (forward migration, nothing hand-edited)

The item-1 schema had a single `platform_revenue` wallet kind, but top-up fees are cash (kobo) and earnings-take fees are credits — two different units that can't share one balance without silently mixing them. Split into `platform_revenue_topup_fees` and `platform_revenue_earnings_cut` via a new migration (`20260912072739_extend_schema_for_functions.sql`), which also added the `withdrawal_refund_failed` ledger reason needed for `fn_fail_withdrawal`.

### One workflow mistake worth remembering for next time

Pre-created all four migration files via `supabase migration new` before writing their content, then ran `supabase db push` — which applied the still-empty files and marked them "applied" in the remote's migration history. Editing them afterward meant `db push` would silently skip the real content on the next run (matches by filename/timestamp, not content). Fixed by deleting the stale rows from `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` and re-pushing, but the correct sequence is: **write a migration's full content before pushing it, not batch-create-then-fill.**

### A schema behavior worth knowing, not a bug

`threads.participant_a/b` reference `users(id)` **without** `ON DELETE CASCADE` (unlike `wallets`, which does cascade from `users`). This means a user with any thread history can't be hard-deleted — consistent with `ledger_entries` already being permanently undeletable once it exists. For a money-moving app this is probably the right default (can't erase transaction/conversation history via account deletion), but it means "delete a user" will need to become "suspend/anonymize a user" as a product decision later, not literal `DELETE FROM users`.

If you're picking this up fresh: ask whoever has them, or pull them from the Supabase dashboard (Project Settings → API, and Settings → Database for the pooler connection string) — this doc intentionally never contains the actual values.

- ✅ `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` — verified live (HTTP 200 against the admin-only `/auth/v1/admin/users` endpoint). The user explicitly chose to proceed with live keys and small real amounts rather than requesting separate sandbox keys — see session-3 section above. If a sandbox pair is ever wanted for money-free testing later: Flutterwave dashboard → switch to Developer/Sandbox mode → generate a separate client_id/secret (sandbox and production are NOT interchangeable — a live token gets a 403 on the sandbox host).
